# Milestone 0.31 — Instruments run; the 0.30 language measured once

Make every measuring instrument run on the 0.30 language, then measure that language with an agent once, against a concurrent C# arm. Nothing later can be sized until this publishes.

## Entry criteria

- v0.30 released (done, 2026-11-21).

## Exit criteria

- #1605 stage 1 published on 0.30 with a concurrent C# arm on the same pinned model; or, if only the full design is declined, the reduced design registered in #1770 published; or "no paid run authorised" recorded, with the rows that then read "not measured" listed in the spend record of #1770 and in the plan of record (#1765). Stage 2 (delivery variants) and the end-to-end leg do not hold the release; stage 2 must publish, or be recorded on #1605 as not run, before #1823 is decided.
- Soundness sweep green on a registered 0.30-syntax packet for the 0.31 run count stated in #1783.
- Evidence ledger published and the README benchmark paragraph corrected (#1774).
- With the released packages (built by the .NET 10 SDK), on a machine with only the .NET 11 SDK, calor init, calor check and dotnet build of a Calor.Sdk consumer succeed (#1639; may ship earlier as a 0.30.x point release).
- Adopter-outreach decision recorded (#1777), or "not decided, treated as decline until changed".
- Release CI flakes are fixed, or the reruns of the 0.31 release are recorded (#1749 is a release-process prerequisite, not a gate).

## Maintainer decisions, batched

- Evaluation registration (#1770): arms, pairs, n, model and commit pin, spend, close rule, class thresholds, delivery rule, MCP rule. Covers #1605, #1764, #1760, #1773, the paid leg of #1736.
- Tracker sign-off: #1759 with #673, #1555, #1670, #1728 and the allowlist classification in #1763.
- Toolchain and sweep record: #1639 (tool framework, SDK pin, roll-forward), #1769 (packet design, the frozen-whitelist disposition, the procedure for adding rows), #1778 (verdict classification), #1783 (run counts).
- Adoption session: #1777, #1648, #1786.

## Contents

Actions: **new** (to be created; `NEW-NN-xx` is a placeholder until filed), **amend** (text added to or replaced in an existing issue), **move-in** (moved from another milestone, usually amended), **rewrite** (body replaced because it is stale against 0.30), **keep**, **close**. "May slip" marks issues that are off the exit chain.

Issue bodies below are shown with their section headings two levels down so this document has one outline. The bodies to file, with their original headings, are in `issues.json`.

## Epic #1758 · [0.31] Epic: tracker and plan of record re-baselined against 0.30

New epic. Labels: epic.

#### Goal

Bring the tracker and the written plan into line with what 0.30 shipped, in one pass at the start of 0.31. After this epic every open issue has a milestone, a title, labels and a body that are true of the current compiler. The reworked 0.31–0.39 plan is in the repository in two forms: the bundle it was filed from, under `docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39/`, and the plan of record, `docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39.md`, which holds the planning rules, the dependency edges between milestones, the fallback for the case that no external maintainer is found, the capability scorecard, and a close-out checklist that keeps these current at every milestone close.

#### Why

Coding agents are handed one issue each with no other context, so a stale issue is executed as written. At HEAD bc80daeb (v0.30) and in the tracker snapshot of 2026-11-23 (89 open issues):

- 11 open issues have no milestone (#673, #851, #875, #922, #1084, #1670, #1694, #1723, #1726, #1731, #1732). One of them, #1694, is the failing soundness sweep.
- Titles carry the wrong release or none. #1594 and #1590 say `[0.29]` and #1605 says `[0.30]`; all three are in milestone 0.31. Eight issues that have a milestone have no prefix (#709, #711, #909, #1142, #1177, #1511, #1555, #1728).
- 35 open issues have no label.
- Bodies describe the pre-0.30 compiler. #1654 says "Today `record` is `NotSupported`"; `src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/FeatureSupport.cs:60-66` marks it Full. #1659 gives a baseline of 48 unsupported constructs; the registry has 58 (`grep -c "Support = SupportLevel.NotSupported"` on that file). #1555 and #1670 are written against `§` markers, each of which is lexer error Calor0006 since 0.30 (`CHANGELOG.md:28-32`).
- The reworked plan is in no file of the repository. Its rules (displacement, base rate before new guarantees, conditions on paid runs, no breaking change after the 0.32 edition), the order between milestones, and what happens to 0.36–0.39 if no external maintainer consents are written nowhere that an issue can cite.

#### Work

| Issue | Item |
|---|---|
| #1759 | Apply the plan of record to the tracker: commit the plan bundle, file the new epics and issues, record the placeholder-to-number map, apply every milestone, title, label and body change to existing issues, sign off |
| #1765 | Publish the plan of record `docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39.md` (rules, registry, dependency edges, scorecard, adopter fallback, restated assessment) and add the milestone close-out checklist to `docs/release-policy.md` |
| #673 | Close: superseded by #1652 and the 0.30 teaching work; its one live defect moves to #1772 and #1652 |
| #1555 | Close: the `§ARR` form it concerns is a lexer error in 0.30; the dead helper it names goes on the #1741 residue list |
| #1670 | Re-test on 0.30 syntax, then close, or file the remaining defect as a conditional child of #1649 |

#### Sequence

1. #1759 first. Its first step commits the plan bundle, and its filing step gives every new issue the number that other issues cite. #673 and #1555 are closed as rows of its checklist.
2. #1765 after #1759, so that the plan of record cites issue numbers and not placeholders. It merges before #1777 (the adopter-outreach decision) is decided, because the decline branch of that decision applies the fallback table this document holds, and before the spend record of #1770 is signed, because a "no paid run authorised" outcome is recorded in this document as well.
3. #1670 at any point in 0.31. It may slip to 0.32 without holding the release.

#### Done when

- #1759 is closed with the maintainer's sign-off comment: `docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39/` holds the bundle and `placeholder-map.json`; no open issue is without a milestone; every open issue in milestones 0.31 to 0.39 except #1694 has the `[0.NN]` prefix of its milestone and at least one label; and the per-milestone open counts equal the table in #1759.
- `docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39.md` is merged with its six sections, `docs/release-policy.md` has a `## Milestone close-out` section with seven steps that `.claude/skills/create-release/SKILL.md` links to, and `calor self-check docs` exits 0.
- #673 and #1555 are closed as not planned, with no milestone, with the comments in this epic.
- #1670 is closed with a recorded 0.30 re-test, or has moved to 0.32 with its amended body.

#### Not in this epic

- The text of any amendment, rewrite or new issue outside this epic. #1759 applies text; each owning epic writes it.
- The resolution of #1728 and the retitles of #1594, #1590 and #1738 (epic #1594), and the allowlist classification (#1763, epic #1761). They are signed off in the same sitting as #1759 but their text is not here.
- The evidence ledger and the README correction (#1774, epic #1775). The plan of record links to the ledger and does not duplicate it.
- The adopter-outreach decision (#1777, epic #1776). This epic only writes the fallback that decision may apply.
- Any compiler change. The only code this epic can add is the pinning test in #1670.

### #1759 · [0.31] Apply the plan of record to the tracker

**Action:** new · **Labels:** documentation · **Blocks:** #1765 · **Displaces:** text-only

#### Change

Apply the reworked 0.31–0.39 plan to the issue tracker in one pass, from files that are committed first. The issue has two small pull requests (steps 0 and 2); everything else is done on the tracker with `gh`, using a token that can create and edit issues. It closes on the maintainer's sign-off comment, not on a merge.

The *plan bundle* is the set of files the plan was written in: one document per milestone, `0.31.md` to `0.39.md` (entry and exit criteria, then every epic and issue of the milestone with its action, labels, dependencies and text), an overview `README.md` if the bundle has one, and `issues.json`, the same content as data. `issues.json` has an `epics` array (`key`, `milestone`, `title`, `existing`, `body`) and an `issues` array (`ref`, `title`, `action`, `milestone`, `epic`, `labels`, `depends_on`, `blocks`, `displaces`, `may_slip`, `from`, `body`). A `ref` is an existing issue (`#1605`) or a placeholder of the form `NEW-NN-xx` for an issue still to be filed. An epic `key` is of the form `#1758`. `action` is one of `new`, `amend`, `move-in` (moved from another milestone and amended), `rewrite` (body replaced), `keep` and `close`.

This issue carries no issue text of its own except the two syntax edits below. For every other row the text to apply is the `body` of the row's entry in `issues.json`; the same text is readable in the milestone document named in the last column of the checklist.

Steps:

0. **Commit the bundle.** One pull request adds `docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39/` with the bundle files, unchanged. The maintainer supplies them: they are not in the repository before this step, and no later step can be done without them. No test is added, so `eng/test-manifest.json` does not change.
1. **File.** Create the 21 new epics (the `epics` entries whose `existing` is null), then the 93 new issues (the `issues` entries whose `action` is `new`; this issue and its epic #1758 are among them), each with the title, milestone and body of its entry. An issue takes the `labels` of its entry; an epic takes the labels the "Epic labels" rule below gives. Create them with a script that reads `issues.json` and calls `gh issue create`; do not paste by hand.
2. **Record the map.** A second pull request adds `docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39/placeholder-map.json`: one JSON object with 124 keys, the 93 placeholders and the 31 epic keys, each mapped to an issue number. The ten existing epics map to their current numbers: #1644 to #1644, #1594 to #1594, #1649 to #1649, #1653 to #1653, #1659 to #1659, #1662 to #1662, #1697 to #1697, #1698 to #1698, #1699 to #1699, #1700 to #1700. Then replace every placeholder and epic key in the titles and bodies filed in step 1 with its number, and make the same replacement in every text applied in steps 3 and 4. This issue's own body is exempt, because it names placeholders and epic keys on purpose. The files committed in step 0 keep their placeholders; the map resolves them.
3. **Apply the checklist** below, row by row: milestone, title, labels, then the text. For `amend` and `move-in` the entry's **Amendment** part says what to add or replace. For `rewrite` the entry holds the replacement body. For `close` it holds the closing comment and the `state_reason`. A `keep` row needs only the title and labels. For an existing epic (#1644, #1594, #1649, #1653, #1659, #1662, #1697 to #1700) the body to apply is the `body` of its `epics` entry, with placeholders replaced. The `issues` entry of the same number lists what changed and why; where it says "the text under 'Epic body' above", it means that `epics` entry.
4. **Apply the two syntax edits** this issue owns (#909, #922).
5. **Run the checks** under "Done when" and paste their output into the sign-off comment.

Rules for every row:

- **Title prefix.** Every open issue in a milestone 0.31 to 0.39 carries the prefix `[0.NN]` of that milestone. The one exception is #1694, "Soundness sweep failing on main": the scheduled sweep opens its tracking issue under exactly that title (`.github/workflows/soundness-sweep.yml:187`) after looking for an open one by the `soundness-sweep` label (`:183`), so the title stays as the workflow writes it.
- **Closed as not planned.** The milestone is cleared: #1555 loses 0.32, and #673 has none.
- **Labels.** An issue's labels become the `labels` field of its entry. 35 open issues have no label today; the Notes column marks the 30 that receive labels in this pass. #673, which is closed, and the four paused issues stay without. Every label the bundle uses already exists in the tracker (`bug`, `ci`, `dev-experience`, `documentation`, `enhancement`, `epic`, `p1`, `p2`, `soundness-sweep`, `tests`).
- **Epic labels.** The `epics` entries carry no labels. A new epic is created with `epic` plus its priority: `p2` for #1838, #1840 and #1872, `p1` for the other 18. The ten existing epics keep their labels.
- **"Part of" line.** Six issues end with a line that names an epic they leave: #1605 (`Part of #1606.`, a closed epic), #1648 (`Part of #1649.`) and #1640 to #1643 (`Part of #1644.`). After this pass each names its new epic (#1775, #1776 and #1852). Where the row's text does not already replace the line, edit it.
- **Paused research.** The four issues in the milestone "PP-W-rows research (paused)" (#1254, #1262, #1267, #1436) are not edited, moved or labelled; #1648 decides them.

The checklist. "Text" names the milestone document under `docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39/` and the epic whose section holds the row. The table and the counts further down were generated from the row list of the bundle; if the `issues.json` committed in step 0 differs from them (the first check under "Done when" prints other numbers), regenerate both from that file before applying anything.

Not in the table: seven `keep` rows that already have the right prefix and labels (#1704, #1706, #1707, #1714, #1715, #1719, #1720), and the four paused issues.

Syntax edits owned by this issue (no other row carries them):

- **#909.** Replace the paragraphs that begin "**Double-reporting**" and "**Over-strong suggestion**" with the two paragraphs below. The #909 section of `0.32.md` then appends a re-test procedure and a done-when, so apply this edit first.

  > **Double-reporting (re-test on 0.30 before fixing).** On the pre-0.30 checker a division nested under a traversed wrapper was reported at the wrapper and again at the division node: the probe `x ?? 10 / y` (written `(?? x (/ 10 y))` in the retired prefix syntax) produced 2× Calor0920 against 1× for plain `10 / y`. At 0.30 `DivisionByZeroChecker` only delegates to `TypedBugPatternAnalysis`, which drops a diagnostic it has already reported for the same span, code and message (`src/Calor.Compiler/Analysis/BugPatterns/TypedBugPatternAnalysis.cs:38`, `:2733`), and `DivisionByZeroChecker.CheckExpression` no longer exists. Re-run the probe; if it reports once, strike this half.
  >
  > **Over-strong suggestion.** The Calor0926 suggester ("consider the refinement `y | (# != 0)`", `src/Calor.Compiler/Analysis/BugPatterns/Patterns/PreconditionSuggester.cs:182-190`) fires for divisions that only execute conditionally (for example the right operand of `??`), so the suggested refinement is stronger than the code requires. A deferral-aware suggester should consult `BoundChildren.DeferredOf()` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Binding/BoundNodes.cs:1005`, introduced in #908) before proposing a parameter refinement for a conditionally executed division.

- **#922.** Replace the bullet that begins "Module rename additionally needs `§U{...}`" with the bullet below. The #922 section of `0.33.md` replaces the sections of the body that contain this bullet; if that text has already been applied, this edit has nothing left to change and is recorded as superseded in the sign-off.

  > - Module rename. Since 0.30 a root `module` header only labels its file: the namespace is the optional second name on the header (`module FileLabel Contoso.Tools`), and the label is not a namespace (`docs/syntax-reference/current-language.md:23-24`). Renaming a label edits one file and leaves importers alone, and that already works (`RenameFileLabelPreservesAnExplicitImportedNamespaceAsync`, `tests/Calor.LanguageServer.Tests/Handlers/RenameHandlerTests.cs:2506`). Renaming a namespace is a different operation, which this issue does not cover: it needs `using` directives (for example `using Contoso.Tools`) indexed as occurrences, so that importers are rewritten with it.

Sign-off. The maintainer closes this issue with one comment that carries the check output and records, in the same sitting, the two closes (#673, #1555) and that #1670 was amended (its re-test may finish later). The same comment records or explicitly defers two decisions whose options are written elsewhere: what happens to #1728 (its row in `0.31.md`, epic #1594) and the allowlist classification in #1763 (epic #1761). Neither holds this issue open.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb; tracker snapshot 2026-11-23)

- 89 open issues: the "Open now" column above. The 11 with no milestone are #673, #851, #875, #922, #1084, #1670, #1694, #1723, #1726, #1731, #1732.
- Title prefixes today. Three do not match their milestone: #1594 and #1590 (`[0.29]`) and #1605 (`[0.30]`), all in 0.31. Eight issues have a milestone and no prefix: #709, #711, #909, #1142, #1177, #1511, #1555, #1728.
- The plan moves 14 issues out of 0.31, all titled `[0.31]`: #1746, #1743, #1741, #1735, #1734 to 0.32; #1744, #1748 to 0.33; #1733, #1747, #1742, #1643, #1640, #1641, #1642 to 0.34. #1648 moves from 0.32 to 0.31 and #1177 from 0.32 to 0.34.
- 35 open issues have no label: the 30 marked in the checklist, #673, and the four paused issues.
- `docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39/` does not exist. `docs/plans/` is outside the version scan of `calor self-check docs` (`src/Calor.Compiler/SelfCheck/DocDriftChecker.cs:265-271`), so the bundle may cite compiler versions.
- Every `§` marker is lexer error Calor0006 (`src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Lexer.cs:741-765`, `CHANGELOG.md:28-32`). Issues that still present one as current syntax: the bodies of #909 (`§Q` and prefix operators), #922 (`§U{...}`), #1657 (`§PP`, `§CDIR`), #1661 and #1662 (`§L`, `§WH`); the title and body of #1643 (`§W`), #1555 (`§ARR`) and #1670 (`§CT`). #875, #1084 and #1655 do too, and their rows replace the whole body.
- For the #909 edit: `src/Calor.Compiler/Analysis/BugPatterns/Patterns/DivisionByZeroChecker.cs` is a 23-line class that calls `TypedBugPatternAnalysis.Check`. `ContainsDivision` survives as a helper with no caller under `src/` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Analysis/Dataflow/BoundNodeHelpers.cs:316`; its three call sites are in `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/Analysis/StructuralBindingCompletenessTests.cs`). `PreconditionSuggester.CheckExpression` walks every child expression without consulting deferral (`PreconditionSuggester.cs:195-196`). Whether the double report still reproduces was not run (no .NET SDK was available when this was written).
- For the #922 edit: the test cited in the replacement renames the label of a file whose namespace another file imports, and asserts one edit in one file with the importer's `using Models` unchanged (`tests/Calor.LanguageServer.Tests/Handlers/RenameHandlerTests.cs:2506-2548`). The bullet being replaced says such a module "stays refused", which is no longer true.

#### Why

An agent that is handed a stale issue executes it as written, and the tracker is stale against 0.30 in the ways the baseline lists. Hand-written checklists for the fixes would restate text that the owning rows also carry, so the same edit would exist twice and could disagree. This issue is therefore one generated checklist with no text of its own: the table is produced from the plan's row list, each row points at the single place its text is written, and the bundle is committed first so that every pointer resolves inside the repository.

#### Done when

Run inside a checkout that contains both pull requests; each command's output goes in the sign-off comment.

- `python3 -c "import json;d=json.load(open('docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39/issues.json'));print(len(d['issues']),sum(i['action']=='new' for i in d['issues']),len(d['epics']),sum(e['existing'] is None for e in d['epics']))"` prints `178 93 31 21`, and `ls docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39/` lists `0.31.md` to `0.39.md`.
- `python3 -c "import json;print(len(json.load(open('docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39/placeholder-map.json'))))"` prints `124`.
- `gh issue list --state open --limit 500 --search "no:milestone" --json number --jq 'length'` prints `0`.
- This prints nothing (every open issue in 0.31 to 0.39 except #1694 has the prefix of its milestone):
  `gh issue list --state open --limit 500 --json number,title,milestone --jq '.[] | select(.milestone != null and (.milestone.title | test("^0\\.[0-9]+$"))) | select(.number != 1694) | . as $i | select(($i.title | startswith("[" + $i.milestone.title + "] ")) | not) | .number'`
- This prints nothing (every open issue in 0.31 to 0.39 has a label):

Any difference is explained in the comment (for example #1670 already closed by its re-test, #1728 closed by the option chosen, or issues opened since 2026-11-23).
- `for n in 909 922 1643 1657 1661 1662; do gh issue view $n --json title,body --jq '.title + "\n" + .body' | grep -c '§'; done` prints `0` six times. A remaining match is acceptable only in a sentence that names the marker as retired, and the sign-off quotes it.

- The sign-off comment exists on this issue and records the #1728 and #1763 decisions, or defers each by name.

#### Not in scope

- Writing or changing amendment text. A row whose text is found wrong before it is applied is corrected by a pull request to the bundle and then applied; after the sign-off the bundle is a snapshot and is not edited.
- Repository changes other than the two pull requests above.
- The paused PP-W-rows research: its four issues are not edited, moved or reactivated.

Part of epic #1758. Blocks #1765.

### #1765 · [0.31] Publish the 0.31-0.39 plan of record: rules, dependency edges, fallback, scorecard, release close-out

**Action:** new · **Labels:** documentation · **Depends on:** #1759 · **Blocks:** #1777, #1839, #1833 · **Displaces:** text-only

#### Change

Add one document, `docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39.md` (the plan of record), add one section to `docs/release-policy.md`, and link that section from `.claude/skills/create-release/SKILL.md`. No code changes. Write it after #1759 has filed the plan, and cite issues by number: replace each `NEW-NN-xx` placeholder and each epic key (`#1758` to `#1700`) below, and in the output of the section 2 command, with its number from `docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39/placeholder-map.json`. Give every number its source and the syntax it was measured on, and leave out any figure whose source you cannot open.

The document opens with its date, the commit it was written against, a link to the plan bundle in `docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39/` (the issues as filed, a snapshot that is not edited once #1759 is signed off), and one line saying that it links to `docs/plans/agent-native-strategy.md` and supersedes nothing in it. It has six sections, in this order.

**1. Planning rules.** Numbered, with this content:

1. Displacement. Every new item names the issue it displaces, or says it is text-only.
2. New hard errors. An issue that adds or tightens a hard error shows that the conversion scorecard's native count (files converted to Calor) and the density ratio (Calor tokens over C# tokens) do not regress, and reports how many files newly fall to whole-file preservation (a file kept as its original C# because conversion failed).
3. Base rate first. No new guarantee is built without a measured count of the defect class in agent output, and a hold branch for a count that is zero or cannot be measured.
4. Paid runs. Every paid agent run has a written spend ceiling, abort triggers, a zero-cost dry pass (`--null-agent`), one pinned model, and a reading written in advance for each outcome.
5. No training-prior criteria. No acceptance criterion depends on models having learned Calor.
6. Breaking changes. After the edition declaration (#1805) no accepted spelling is removed or given a new meaning, no compiling program changes meaning, and no default-on hard error is added; #1805 settles whether a fix that withdraws an unsound acceptance is exempt. Additive syntax is allowed. A breaking change in a later edition ships with a rewriter that migrates fixtures, pairs and documentation in the same change.
7. Comparators. Conventional C# and equipped C# (C# given executable specifications and analyzers) are the only gating comparators through 0.37. Rust is reported, never adjudicated.
8. Maintainer decisions are filed as decisions. Outreach, spend and the edition declaration are issues with the options written out.

Two items follow the rules in the same section.

- *Registry.* A registration is the record, written before a run, of its design, thresholds and the reading of each outcome. Every registration this plan asks for is appended to Annex A of `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md` and recorded nowhere else: the evaluation registrations #1770, #1816 and #1832, the collection budget #1867, and the protocols of #1708 and #1713. CI holds the annex append-only: the `annex-freeze` job runs `scripts/check-annex-freeze.py`, which lets rows and log entries be added, rejects a change to an existing one, and requires a new `A-1.N` entry in the annex revision log for every change to the annex.
- *Displacement ledger*, as a table: each issue moved out of a milestone and the new issue that claimed its capacity. Initial state: 14 issues leave 0.31. Twelve are claimed once each (#1642 by #1766, #1641 by #1762, #1744 by #1764, #1733 by #1767, #1747 by #1760, #1741 by #1763, #1742 by #1773, #1748 by #1771, #1643 by #1781, #1640 by #1778, #1746 by #1782, #1734 by #1779); #1743 and #1735 are unclaimed. No issue outside 0.31 is displaced: every other new issue is text-only, decision-only, conditional, or states its new capacity in its body.

**2. Milestone criteria and dependency edges.** The section opens with the entry and exit criteria of each milestone, copied from the `## Entry criteria` and `## Exit criteria` lists at the head of `docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39/0.NN.md`. From then on this section is where a criterion is amended, by pull request; the bundle is not edited. The edges follow, one line each, "from → to: what must be true", so that the order reads as a chain and not as delay:

- 0.31 → 0.32: the first 0.31 exit criterion met in one of its three forms: #1605 stage 1 published on 0.30 against a concurrent C# arm on the same pinned model (an arm is one side of a comparison: the same tasks done in one language under one setup); or the reduced design registered in #1770 published; or "no paid run authorised" recorded with its not-measured rows (the table at the end of this section). Soundness sweep green on the registered 0.30 packet for the 0.31 run count (#1783). The #1782 baseline recorded, and the corpus report job (#1779) running before #1802, #1784 and #1800 start.
- #1605 stage 2 → #1823 (the `calor init` delivery default): stage 2 published, or recorded on #1605 as not run. Stage 2 and the end-to-end leg do not hold the 0.31 release.
- 0.32 → 0.33: edition declared or its deferral recorded (#1805); #1823 decided.
- 0.32 → 0.34: #1647 closed.
- 0.33 → 0.34 and 0.36: adopter checkpoint recorded (#1833, which follows #1777), or its default line "not decided, treated as the fallback outcome until changed". 0.34 runs at the scope section 4 gives if the fallback applies; 0.36 is entered only on recorded consent.
- 0.31 and 0.32 → 0.35: sweep green for the proof-claim count in `docs/release-policy.md` (#1783), counted from the latest re-registration of the packet (a freeze that only adds rows does not restart the count); seeded-defect replication published or declined (#1810), with its registered consequence for 0.35 applied (#1816).
- 0.36 entry also needs #1694 closed with a disposition for every failure, no open issue carrying the `soundness-sweep` label, the sweep green for the proof-claim count of #1783 counted from the latest re-registration, and the edition in force as #1697 criterion 3 defines it (declared by #1805 or #1846, re-affirmed by #1846, and the #1084 marker in the compiler release the pilot pins).
- 0.36 → 0.37: #1697 closed on its exit record, or the fallback scope of section 4; #1702 landed (defined in section 4), or no task counted as effect-boundary evidence; the replication result recorded as an input (#1810).
- 0.37 → 0.38: a published positive verdict for the task class from the full comparison, with a `proceed` or `narrow` decision (#1699).
- 0.37 → 0.39: a published positive verdict for a task class from the full comparison, and the pilot continuing (#1700). 0.39 does not wait for 0.38.
- Every dependency between issues that crosses a milestone boundary, one line each, generated from `issues.json` with the command below. The file keeps its placeholders, so map the output before pasting it. Examples: #1648 → #1708 and #1711; #1651, #1827, #1830 → #1710; #1638 → #1643; #1639 → #1855; #1779 (the corpus report job) → #1647, #1746, #1784, #1800, #1802, #1659, #1848, #1834, #1853.
- Under the adopter fallback, an edge from a held 0.36 issue into 0.37 is met as section 4 says and not by that issue closing: #1702, #1703 and #1704 by their pilot-independent parts merged on `main`; #1697, #1701, #1706 and #1707 by the fallback scope replacing pilot findings.

  ```
  python3 -c "
  import json, re
  d = json.load(open('docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39/issues.json'))
  ms = {i['ref']: i['milestone'] for i in d['issues']}
  for i in d['issues']:
      for dep in re.findall(r'#\d+|NEW-\d\d-\d\d', i['depends_on']):
          if ms.get(dep, i['milestone']) != i['milestone']:
              print(dep, ms[dep], '->', i['ref'], i['milestone'])
  "
  ```

The section ends with a table headed "If no paid run is authorised in 0.31". It is applied only if the spend record of #1770 records "no paid run authorised"; the first 0.31 exit criterion is then met by that record together with this table, and the spend record lists the same rows. If the two lists differ when this document is written, copy the spend record's.

|---|---|

**3. Capability scorecard.** What a Rust user takes for granted, as a table that needs no benchmark. Paths are under `src/Calor.Compiler/` unless they start elsewhere.

| Capability | Calor at 0.30 | Addressed by |
|---|---|---|
| Whole bug classes rejected at build | Null at three boundaries (binding, return, argument), for `string` values by the diagnostics' own documentation (Calor0272–0274, `Diagnostics/Diagnostic.cs:367-411`); effects; refinements. `match` exhaustiveness is checked for `bool` and union types only (`Analysis/PatternChecker.cs:258-263`), and no 0.30 source form declares a union (`Migration/CalorEmitter.cs:2837-2839`). No disposal check (none among the six checkers in `Analysis/BugPatterns/Patterns/`). | #1800 (0.32), #1643 (0.34), epic #1872 (0.35) |
| Diagnostics that carry a fix | 5 sites build a fix marked safe to apply (`Formatting/CalorFormatter.cs:441`, `:481`; `Parsing/Parser.cs:621`; `Parsing/Lexer.cs:398`, `:407`, the two lexer sites conditionally) against 248 diagnostic codes (`grep -c '= "Calor[0-9]' Diagnostics/Diagnostic.cs`) | #1645, #1790 (0.32) |
| Tests written in the language | None. `calor test` and `calor check` run `tests/*.cs` xUnit files (`Commands/TestCommand.cs:19-21`), and the write hook that `calor init` installs blocks every `.cs` file except `*.g.cs` and files under `obj/` (`Commands/HookCommand.cs:283-312`) | #1809 (0.32), #1825 (0.33) |
| One command that builds, tests and checks | `calor check` (`Commands/TestCommand.cs:65`) | #1818 (0.32) |
| Fast warm feedback | Not measured on 0.30; the last latency record, `bench/phase0-agent-native/latency/ml1-003/`, predates it (`CHANGELOG.md:3787`). The index build is about 8–15x its 0.29 cost (`eng/performance-baselines.json`, `performance-suite-seconds.noisePolicy`) | #1738 (0.31), #1818 (0.32), #1836 (0.33) |
| A stable language | None. 0.30 was a no-compatibility rewrite with no rewriter (`CHANGELOG.md:35-37`) | #1805 (0.32) |
| A reference the agent already knows | No training prior. The primer is an MCP resource, `calor://primer` (`Mcp/McpMessageHandler.cs:126`) | #1605 stage 2 (0.31), #1823 (0.32), #1835 (0.33) |
| Cost per accepted task | Not measured on 0.30; not measured for Rust | #1605 (0.31), #1831 (0.33) |

**4. Adopter fallback.** Written in advance so that the two decisions that may apply it cite it and do not restate it: #1777 (the outreach decision in 0.31, on its decline branch) and #1833 (the checkpoint at the close of 0.33). Trigger: no named external maintainer has consented in writing. The recorded position is "Contacts made: 0" with no outreach window opened (`docs/plans/safe-delegation-m0/v0.23/r2a-adopter-unavailable.md:29-30`), the recorded policy is not to fund new adoption depth ahead of demand (`docs/plans/call-w-adjudication.md:39-41`), and naming an external adopter is a decision the maintainer reserved (`docs/plans/call-s-adjudication.md:187-190`).

| Scope | Under the fallback |
|---|---|
| 0.34, epic #1659 (native conversion coverage) | Stay: #1659 with its restated baseline, #1733, #1747, #1742, #1844. #1654–#1658 and #1850 convert natively only the constructs that cause preservation in at least one file of the three corpus projects (MediatR, Serilog, FluentValidation) under default `calor migrate`, read from the per-file `losses` lists of the migrate reports the corpus report job (#1779) produces; where a loss does not name the registry entry, the per-entry corpus counts of the coverage record (#1659) are used. Every other registry entry is recorded as preserved with the reason kind `no-demand`, which is valid only under the fallback and satisfies the 0.34 exit criterion. |
| 0.34, epic #1845 (decisions on converted code) | All rows stay. #1854 (can the real-scale venue reopen) becomes the only route to real-scale evidence. |
| 0.34, epic #1852 (.NET 11 and C# 15) | All rows stay. Only the reuse by #1705 lapses. |
| 0.35, epic #1662 (verification depth) | All rows stay. The repeat of the proof-status inventory on the pilot component (#1703) is recorded as not run. |
| 0.35, epics #1861 and #1872 | Unchanged. Their conditional rows already carry gates and hold branches. |
| 0.36: #1697, #1701, #1705, #1706, #1707 | Held. A held issue stays open in its milestone with a comment that links the #1833 record (the #1777 record on early application); no work is scheduled on it, and it is not closed as not planned. #1701 says that failing to recruit a maintainer is a blocker that creator self-evaluation does not replace. |
| 0.36: #1702, #1703, #1704 | Their bodies say that part of each needs no pilot, and those parts still run: for #1702 the policy, the checker, a command that classifies a base-to-head change, and their demonstrations; for #1703 the evidence record and release check without pilot-specific records; for #1704 the readiness controls. They run under F1 as well as F2 and are delivered in 0.37, ahead of any 0.37 collection. The pilot-specific parts are held, so the three issues stay open in 0.36 and are not moved in the tracker. "#1702 landed", as the 0.37 entry criterion uses it, means that the pilot-independent part is merged on `main` and linked from a comment on #1702; it does not mean #1702 is closed. |
| 0.37: #1698, #1708–#1712, #1865 to #1867 | One of the two options below. |
| 0.38, 0.39 | Held. #1699, #1700 and their children stay open with entry criteria unmet and no work scheduled. They are closed as not planned only if the maintainer records that the epic will not be pursued. |

Options for 0.37, a maintainer decision recorded by #1833 in `docs/plans/0.33-adopter-checkpoint.md`:

- **F1, no new run.** #1698 closes on the results already published (#1605, the three-condition replication #1810, and the per-agent results #1811 where they ran) and records the fallback disposition with the verdict "not collected: no independent tasks". #1708–#1712 and #1865 to #1867 are closed as not planned with that record linked. Cost: none. The claim stays at fixture scale, seeded, detection only and non-independent.
- **F2, a fixture-scale extension labelled non-independent.** What it adds to the 0.32 replication: tasks other than the nine seeded-defect pairs (benchmark tasks with one defect injected on purpose), authored by the maintainer and screened by the #1648 admission rule, and the specification-defect and boundary-defect controls of #1711 written by someone other than the implementation agent. #1708 drops pilot-rooted tasks and independent ownership and keeps stratification, the admission rule and sizing; #1865 counts the maintainer-authored candidates per stratum against the sizing minimum; #1709 is unchanged; #1710 reduces to the three-condition harness (#1815), cost accounting and the #1702 classification command applied to every run; #1712 runs only with a cleared review (#1857) and an authorised budget (#1867), publishes its verdict as "non-independent, fixture scale", and neither `proceed` nor `narrow` is available to it: its decision for 0.38 is fixed in advance as `revise` or `stop`, and 0.38 stays held. With no pilot component for #1703 to inventory, any proof claim is bounded by the corpus inventory (#1853) and the proof yield on named modules (#1843). Cost: task and control authoring plus one paid collection under rule 4.
- **Recommended rule.** F2 only if the 0.32 replication published a Calor advantage over equipped C# under its registered reading (#1816); otherwise F1. F2 reverts to F1 if #1857 records no cleared review or #1867 records a decline; issues already closed on their own record stay closed as completed, the rest are closed as not planned, and merged assets are kept. Reason: without an effect that survived equipped C#, an extension at the same scale can only repeat a null.

Rules that hold under either option:

- *Verdict not collected* means #1712 did not run: #1867 recorded a decline or F1 applies, #1712 is closed as not planned, and #1698 records "not collected". *Verdict not positive* means #1712 published a verdict that names no task class in which Calor added value beyond equipped C#; 0.38 and 0.39 then stay held. Separately, 0.38 is held when the decision #1712 records for 0.38 is `revise` or `stop`, even beside a supported task class; that decision is not read by the 0.39 entry criterion (#1700). When the verdict is not collected, 0.38 and 0.39 stay held.
- Nothing published under a fallback-scope 0.37 opens 0.38. The 0.38 entry criterion needs the full comparison on independent tasks.
- Early application: if #1777 records a decline in 0.31, the table applies from that date, held issues link the #1777 record until the #1833 record exists, and the choice between F1 and F2 waits for the replication. If #1777 carries only its default line ("not decided, treated as decline until changed"), no outreach happens and the issues this table holds are held from that date, but "demand unproven" is not written and nothing is closed, narrowed or re-scoped (the 0.34 cut and F1/F2 included) until an explicit decline or #1833.
- Lifting the hold: only a later dated maintainer entry in the #1833 record that names a consenting external maintainer lifts it, and 0.36 is then entered at the next milestone close. Creator self-evaluation, AI review and the maintainer's own projects do not lift it.

The section ends with an empty list headed "Checkpoint log"; #1833 appends one dated line to it and does not edit the table.

The table, the options and the rule are the plan's proposal, not a taken decision. Three things in it have been decided by nobody: the cut to 0.34 coverage, the two 0.37 options with their rule, and the delivery of the pilot-independent parts of #1702 to #1704 in 0.37. Merging the pull request records the maintainer's acceptance; a row the maintainer changes in review is changed in the document, and #1777 and #1833 apply the merged text.

**5. Milestone close-out.** Add `## Milestone close-out` to `docs/release-policy.md` after "Formatter write-path gate". The section names no compiler version; it says "the milestone being closed" and "the released version". The checklist is run once per milestone, after the release that completes the milestone has been published and before the tracker milestone is closed, and the completed checklist is posted as a comment on that release's pull request. Steps:

1. Scorecard: update section 3 of the plan of record to the released version. A row that was not re-measured says so and since which version.
2. Evidence ledger (`docs/benchmarking/evidence-ledger.md`, created by #1774): add every result published or adjudicated in the milestone, with the columns the ledger defines.
3. Displacement ledger: list each issue moved out of the milestone and the new issue that claimed its capacity. A moved-out issue is claimed at most once.
4. Sizing: add a row to the refresh log of the #1708/#1712 sizing and budget request (#1786), recomputed from the newest published run-to-run variability. The first refresh is due at the first close-out after #1605 stage 1 has published.
5. Tracker: every open issue in the closing milestone is closed or moved with a one-line reason, rows marked "may slip" included. Re-run the milestone, title-prefix and label checks of #1759.
6. Edges: for each criterion and edge of section 2 that enters the next milestone, record met (with a link) or not met. A milestone with an unmet entry edge does not start.
7. Milestone-specific values: publish any value an exit criterion of the milestone being closed asks for at the release commit. The plan of record lists them; the first is the proof-yield "after" table of #1843 at the close of the milestone that contains it.

Add one line to `.claude/skills/create-release/SKILL.md`, at the end of step 5 ("Watch the run and confirm on nuget.org"), that sends the reader to this section when the release completes a milestone.

**6. Restated assessment.** The claims the plan rests on, each with its source and the syntax it was measured on. Where the evidence ledger (#1774) has the entry, link it.

- Benefit: shown once, at fixture scale. Calor caught 9 of 9 injected defects and C# 4 of 9 (`bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/ws5-probe-001/VERDICT.md:23-24`; 2026-07-28, pre-0.30 syntax). Not shown against equipped C#, on natural defects, at real scale, on independently authored tasks, or on 0.30. The real-scale venue was adjudicated a miss and retired on 2026-08-05 (`docs/plans/call-s-adjudication.md:3-5`).
- Cost: last measured on the pre-0.30 syntax with the agent's default model, not a pinned one (`bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/e1a-attribution/pins.json:6`): 2.38x iterations-to-green pooled over two pairs at 30 runs per arm, and 1.62x with a syntax exemplar of about 60 lines in the prompt, which removed 0.55 of the excess (`bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/e1a-attribution/VERDICT.md:3-9`, `:17-27`). No agent has been measured on the 0.30 syntax; #1605 does that.
- Density: migrated Calor was 1.016x C# tokens overall and 0.859x on the native subset in one hand measurement, in which MediatR and Serilog came out entirely as whole-file preserved C# because of CS8669 (#1746 body: 2026-11-20, o200k proxy tokenizer). The CS8669 gap itself is recorded at `CHANGELOG.md:120-124`.
- Retired syntax in the tree: 853 of 1,823 tracked `.calr` files contain a `§` marker (`git ls-files '*.calr' | xargs grep -l '§' | wc -l`).
- Decisive comparison: 0.37, gated on the 0.36 pilot, which has no secured maintainer.
- Premise: Rust is the maintainer's reference bar. That agents do well in Rust is a hypothesis here, not a measurement: no result in this repository compares Rust with C# on one harness. #1829 documents what published results do and do not show, and #1831 takes one cost reading. Rust appears in the scorecard and in that reading and is never a gating comparator.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- `docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39.md` does not exist, and no file under `docs/` names a milestone 0.31 to 0.39 (`grep -rn --include='*.md' '\[0\.3[1-9]\]' docs/` prints nothing). The newest file named as a roadmap is `docs/plans/roadmap-v0.20.md`, which calls its version numbers "planning/release-target containers, not execution dates" (`:150-152`). The bundle directory `docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39/` is added by #1759.
- `docs/release-policy.md` (154 lines) has two top-level sections, "Immutable candidate promotion" (`:9`) and "Formatter write-path gate" (`:132`), and nothing about closing a milestone.
- Releases are prepared by following `.claude/skills/create-release/SKILL.md`. It has five numbered steps (the fifth at `:177`), links `docs/release-policy.md` once (`:235`), and has no close-out step, so a checklist that lives only in the policy file would not be run.
- CI runs `self-check docs` (`.github/workflows/test.yml:266-267`). It reports any doc under `docs/` that contains the current compiler version as a literal, except under `docs/plans`, `docs/experiments`, `docs/design` and `docs/process` (`src/Calor.Compiler/SelfCheck/DocDriftChecker.cs:265-271`, `:951-978`). `docs/release-policy.md` is scanned; the plan of record is not.
- Annex A starts at `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md:100`. `scripts/check-annex-freeze.py` guards that file (`:65`; its rules are in the docstring at `:10-30`) and runs as the `annex-freeze` job (`.github/workflows/experiment-registry-tamper-check.yml:98`). `docs/plans/evidence/` accepts no new file (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/Plans/EvidenceTreeGuardTests.cs`).
- The pair harness rejects any arm other than `calor` and `csharp` (`bench/phase0-agent-native/run-pair.sh:282`) and has the zero-cost flag `--null-agent` (`:222`).
- Every value and path in the scorecard, the three adopter records cited in section 4, and the numbers in section 6 were checked at this commit. Statements about issue bodies (#1701 to #1704, #1746) are from the tracker snapshot of 2026-11-23.

#### Why

The reworked plan depends on eight rules, an order between milestones and a fallback, and none of them is written where an issue can cite it. Milestones 0.36 to 0.39 wait on an external maintainer whom nobody has been authorised to recruit, so the edges and the fallback have to exist before the decisions that apply them (#1777, #1833), and the fallback has to name rows, because a fallback that only says "re-scope" cannot be applied. The capability scorecard stands in for a Rust benchmark arm, which the pair harness cannot run. The close-out checklist exists because the scorecard, the evidence ledger, the displacement ledger and the sizing request each need an update at every milestone close, and an issue filed in one milestone would update them once.

#### Done when

- `docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39.md` exists with the six sections in the order above, `grep -c "NEW-3" docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39.md` prints `0`, and `grep -cE '\b3[1-9]-[A-H]\b' docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39.md` prints `0`.
- Section 1 has the eight rules, the registry item naming Annex A of `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md`, and the displacement ledger table with one row for each of the 14 issues that leave 0.31.
- Section 2 has the entry and exit criteria of each milestone 0.31 to 0.39, at least one edge into each of 0.32 to 0.39 (the 0.39 edge not passing through 0.38), one line for every line the command in section 2 prints, the line on edges from held 0.36 issues, and the "If no paid run is authorised in 0.31" table.
- Section 3 has the eight rows, each with a value, its source and the issues that address it.
- Section 4 names the trigger; has a row for each scope listed above; gives both options with their cost and the rule; defines "verdict not collected", "verdict not positive" and "#1702 landed"; names the two inventories that bound a fallback proof claim; states what a #1777 non-decision means; states how the hold is lifted; and ends with the empty checkpoint log.
- Section 6 gives a source for every number.
- `docs/release-policy.md` contains `## Milestone close-out` with the seven steps, and `.claude/skills/create-release/SKILL.md` links to the section.
- `dotnet run --project src/Calor.Compiler -c Release --no-build -- self-check docs` exits 0 after a Release build, which also shows that the new policy section does not contain the current compiler version, and the CI step "Check agent-facing docs against the implementation (spec drift)" is green.
- The pull request changes those three files only. No test is added, so `eng/test-manifest.json` does not change.
- The maintainer's approval of the pull request is recorded on it as acceptance of the fallback table, options and rule.

#### Not in scope

- Applying the fallback or deciding outreach: #1777 and #1833.
- The evidence ledger (#1774), the green-sweep run counts that #1783 writes into the same policy file, and the sizing request (#1786). This document cites them.

- Reopening Option/Result (#1617) or `requires` (#1618), making Rust a gating comparator, or reactivating the paused PP-W-rows research.

### #1555 · Parser: hand-written §ARR with a type-like id and a non-type-like element type swaps id and type

**Action:** close (from 0.32) · **Labels:** bug

`state_reason`: `not_planned`. Clear the 0.32 milestone when closing. The amended #1649 drops this row from its Work table, and #1741 takes the dead helper code onto its residue list.

Before posting, compile the probe below with `calor --input Probe.calr --output Probe.g.cs`. It is a candidate in 0.30 syntax that was read against the parser and the syntax reference and not compiled; if it fails for a reason other than the binding (the effect row, for example), correct the probe first. Post the comment if the command exits 0 and `Probe.g.cs` declares a local named `MyArray` initialised with `new Guid[4]`. If the name and the element type come out swapped, do not close: keep the issue open in 0.32 with the `[0.32]` prefix, restore its row in the #1649 Work table, and replace its body with the probe and the output.

```calor
module Probe
  using System
  func Count () → int
    ! alloc
    let Guid[]:MyArray = new Guid[4]
    return MyArray.Length
```

Closing comment:

````markdown
Closing as not planned: the form this bug needs no longer exists. Since 0.30 every `§` marker, `§ARR` included, is lexer error Calor0006 (`src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Lexer.cs:741-765`, `CHANGELOG.md:28-32`), so a hand-written sized `§ARR{...}` is rejected before the parser runs. The 0.30 forms leave nothing to swap: an array is created with `new T[n]` and its internal id is generated by the parser (`src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Parser.cs:7126-7155`, `:7301`), and a typed binding such as `let Guid[]:MyArray = new Guid[4]` takes the text before the colon as the type and the identifier after it as the name (`ParseCanonicalBinding`, `Parser.cs:4159-4176`) without calling `AttributeHelper.IsLikelyType`. That heuristic is now reached only through `AttributeHelper.InterpretBindAttributes`, which has no caller under `src/`; both are dead code kept alive by unit tests (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/CodeGenBugFixTests.cs:505`, `:524`, `:635`, `:651`), and their removal is on the residue list of #1741. Checked by reading the code at bc80daeb and by compiling that binding on the current compiler.
````

### #1670 · [0.31] Re-test on 0.30: module-level documentation text and the generated C#

**Action:** amend (from none) · **Labels:** bug, p2 · **May slip:** yes

**Current.** The issue reports that a module-level `§CT "ctx"` compiled to a `// CONTEXT` comment without the "ctx" text (seen at main 399649371 while re-checking #929), so user-written content was dropped with no diagnostic. It asks to emit the text in the comment or to document why it is omitted. It has no milestone and no labels.

**Amendment.** Set the milestone to 0.31, the labels to `bug` and `p2`, and the title to "[0.31] Re-test on 0.30: module-level documentation text and the generated C#". Replace the whole body with:

````markdown
## Change

Re-test this report on the 0.30 language and close it with a recorded result. The `§CT` form it was written against no longer exists. In 0.30 the form that carries module-level prose is a `///` documentation comment directly above `module`.

1. Add one test to `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/MetadataEmissionTests.cs` that parses the probe below with the file's `Parse` helper, emits C# with `new CSharpEmitter().Emit(module)`, and asserts whether the string `Billing context` is in the result. Pin what the compiler does today and name the test for it. Do not change the emitter in this issue.

   ```calor
   /// Billing context. Totals are in cents.
   module Billing
     func Value () → int
       !
       return 1
   ```

2. Post the generated C# for the probe on this issue (`calor --input probe.calr --output probe.g.cs`).
3. Disposition:
   - Text absent: open one issue in milestone 0.32 as a child of #1649, titled "`///` documentation text is not emitted into generated C#". It states which declaration kinds are affected (module, function, type, member), whether XML documentation carried over from converted C# is affected (the converter stores it in the same field, `src/Calor.Compiler/Ast/AstNode.cs:42-46`, through `AttachDocComment`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/RoslynSyntaxVisitor.cs:1931-1937`), and the choice to make: emit as `//` comments, emit as XML documentation, or document the omission in `docs/syntax-reference/`. Link it here and close this issue as completed.
   - Text present: close this issue as not planned with the output.

## Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- `§CT`, like every `§` marker, is lexer error Calor0006 (`src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Lexer.cs:741-765`).
- No parser path builds a `ContextNode`: `src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Parser.cs:933` declares `ContextNode? context = null` and nothing assigns it before it reaches the `ModuleNode` constructor (`:1124-1126`). `CSharpEmitter` writes the `// CONTEXT` comment only for a non-null context (`src/Calor.Compiler/CodeGen/CSharpEmitter.cs:1729-1737`, `:8238-8241`), so that comment cannot appear for parsed source.
- A `ContextNode` built in memory is written by `CalorEmitter` as `///` lines above the `module` header (`src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/CalorEmitter.cs:222-227`, `:3230-3238`) and re-parses with `Context == null` and the text in `ModuleNode.DocComment` (`ProgrammaticProseMetadata_EmitsDocumentationNotRetiredMarkers`, `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/MetadataEmissionTests.cs:14-67`).
- The parser attaches `///` text to modules, functions, types and members (`Parser.cs:14-18`; 15 `WithDocumentation` call sites, the module one at `:1124`). No file under `src/Calor.Compiler/CodeGen/` references `DocComment` (`grep -rn DocComment src/Calor.Compiler/CodeGen/` prints nothing), so by reading the code the text does not reach the generated C#. This was not executed when the issue was amended; step 1 confirms or refutes it.
- `eng/test-manifest.json:8` pins `expectedTotal` for `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests` at 22559, and CI fails when the measured total differs (`scripts/check_trx.py:193-194`, run at `.github/workflows/test.yml:603`).

## Why

The report is true of a form that is now a lexer error, so it can be neither fixed nor closed as written. Re-stated for 0.30 the question is wider than the original: whether documentation written on a declaration survives into the generated C#. This issue may slip to 0.32 without holding the 0.31 release.

## Done when

- The test exists in `MetadataEmissionTests.cs`, passes in CI, and its name and assertion state whether the documentation text is present in the generated C#.
- `expectedTotal` for `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests` in `eng/test-manifest.json` is raised by the one test added.
- The generated C# for the probe is posted on this issue.
- Either a new issue under #1649 is linked and this one is closed as completed, or this one is closed as not planned with output that shows the text present.

Part of epic #1758.
````

**Why.** The issue names a retired form and had no milestone or labels. A re-test on 0.30 either closes it or replaces it with a defect stated in current syntax; reading the code at bc80daeb suggests the second, and wider than the module case, which is why the amended #1649 lists the re-filed issue as a conditional child. The row is off the 0.31 exit chain and may slip to 0.32.

## Epic #1761 · [0.31] Epic: the benchmark harness runs the 0.30 language in isolation

New epic. Labels: epic.

#### Goal

`bench/phase0-agent-native/run-pair.sh` runs the three kinds of arm the 0.30 evaluation (#1605) needs: the 0.30 Calor arm, the pinned pre-0.30 Calor arm ("arm A") and the C# arm. It runs them from workspaces in which the agent can neither name nor read a Calor checkout, and it records the telemetry that evaluation reports. Everything in this epic is verified without a paid agent run.

#### Why

The pair harness is the instrument behind every agent result this project has published. At 0.30 it cannot run the language it exists to measure. Paths below without a directory are under `bench/phase0-agent-native/`.

- **It cannot run a 0.30 Calor arm.** All 102 `.calr` files under `bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs/` are in the retired `§` syntax, and so is the arm check the harness builds before any agent starts (`templates/calor-arm/permissive-canary.calr`). The 0.30 lexer rejects every such file with `Calor0006`. Translated copies of 25 starters and 25 reference solutions exist under `tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs/`, but no harness script reads them.
- **Agents read the checkout.** In the July attribution epoch (`epochs/e1a-attribution`, 60 runs of the Calor arm without an exemplar, pre-0.30 syntax), 16 final messages report finding the task's reference solution in the repository and 2 say the implementation follows it (keyword scan of `agent.json`; the command is in #1762). Several name the route: "the Calor repo referenced by the csproj". The arm's project file embeds the absolute path of the repository, so creating workspaces outside the checkout, as the harness does, does not close that route.
- **The telemetry cannot support the report.** A journal entry for a diagnostic holds a code and a declaration id. Nothing is recorded for the C# arm. At HEAD the capture is also skipped for every Calor arm whose label is not exactly `calor`, which is every Calor arm of #1605.
- **CI does not exercise it.** The harness tests run in a job that builds no compiler (`.github/workflows/test.yml:124`), so the cases that drive `run-pair.sh` end to end are skipped there.

The first exit criterion of 0.31 is the publication of #1605 stage 1 (or the reduced design, or a recorded "no paid run authorised"). Stage 1 is blocked by the first three issues below. Stage 2 and the end-to-end leg do not hold the release.

#### Work

| Issue | Item |
|---|---|
