### #1766 · [0.31] run-pair.sh runs the 0.30 Calor arm and the pinned pre-0.30 arm A

**Action:** new · **Labels:** tests, p1 · **Blocks:** #1762, #1764, #1767, #1605, #1789, #1807, #1815 · **Displaces:** #1642

#### Change

Make `bench/phase0-agent-native/run-pair.sh` and `check-probe-pair.sh` run a 0.30 Calor arm, and document and validate the pre-0.30 arm A. Paths below without a directory are under `bench/phase0-agent-native/`.

Terms: a *pair* is one task directory with a specification, a hidden test suite and a starting fixture per arm; an *arm* is one configuration under test (language, compiler build, what the prompt contains); the *arm canary* is a small program the harness builds before any agent starts to prove the arm's compiler behaves as configured; `--null-agent` applies the pair's reference solution instead of invoking an agent, so it exercises the whole loop at zero API cost; the *primer* (the harness option calls it the exemplar) is the syntax sheet appended to the agent's prompt with `--exemplar`. No paid agent run is part of this issue.

Parts. The work may be delivered as up to three ordered pull requests, and the issue closes when the last applicable one merges.

- **Part A, the 0.30 wiring and its validation** (steps 1 to 4 and 6). It waits for nothing.
- **Part B, the arm-A procedure, and part C, the B′ primer** (step 5). They exist only if the design entry of #1770 registers arm A, and neither starts before that entry is merged. The spend record of #1770 may follow later; nothing here waits for it. If the entry registers no arm A (the reduced design, or no paid run), post a comment that links the entry and close the issue on part A.

1. **Source selection.** Add one option (the name is the implementer's choice, for example `--syntax 0.30`) under which the Calor arm takes its starter from `tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs/<pair>/calor/` and its null-agent reference from `.../<pair>/reference/calor/`. `pair.json`, `spec.md`, `tests/`, shims, `smoke/` and the C# fixture still come from `bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs/<pair>/`. Record the selected root and the SHA-256 of the starter files in `result.json`. Without the option nothing changes, so arm A keeps reading the originals. Give `check-probe-pair.sh` and `run-epoch.sh` the same option. `run-epoch.sh` also gains an arm list: each entry is a label plus the `run-pair.sh` options of that arm (`--arm`, `--arm-label`, `--exemplar`, `--arm-repo-root`, `--calor-dll`, the option above, and `--initialized-workspace` with what it carries). With a list it runs one run per entry per pair in turn (interleaved), writes one `pins.json` that names every entry, and supports `--null-agent`. Without a list `--arms` behaves as it does today.
2. **0.30 arm canary.** Add a canary file in 0.30 syntax beside `templates/calor-arm/permissive-canary.calr` and use it whenever the option in (1) is set, including in `--initialized-workspace` mode in place of the inline text at `run-pair.sh:575`. The expected outcome is unchanged: a failed build carrying `error Calor0410`. Record the canary's SHA-256 in `result.json`. A candidate, to be confirmed by building it:

   ```calor
   module ArmCanary
     func Quiet () → void
       !
       Console.WriteLine("laundered")
   ```

3. **One specification.** `pairs/W5A-003-config-echo/spec.md:14` tells the agent "in Calor that means `§E{fs:r}`". Under the option in (1), both arms of that pair receive a copy of the specification with the 0.30 spelling (`! fs:r`). Keep 0.30-only harness inputs that are not `.calr` sources (this copy, and any shim that has to differ) in a new directory under `bench/phase0-agent-native/`, outside `pairs/` and outside the ActiveSyntax mirror, whose registry test admits only copies of registered originals.
4. **Validate, and fix what fails.** Run `--null-agent` for both arms of each of the 25 pairs that have a translated copy, and `check-probe-pair.sh` on the nine `W5*` pairs (each carries one deliberate defect in its starter). For a failure, fix the translated copy, or add a 0.30 shim in the directory from (3) and select it under the option. Three things to check explicitly; none has been verified by execution on 0.30:
   - each `tests/shims/TestShim.calor.cs` (25) and `smoke/shims/SmokeShim.calor.cs` (9) binds to the namespace and class the 0.30 compiler emits for the translated headers (`module Report Report block` with a nested `module ReportModule partial`);
   - `check-probe-pair.sh:168` expects a `ContractViolationException` from the three `W5B` starters, whose contracts are postconditions (`=>`);
   - the PATH shim's telemetry compile (`run-pair.sh:1140-1142`) and its `calor ids index` call (`:1102`) accept the 0.30 CLI.
5. **Arm A and the B′ primer** (parts B and C). Arm A is the last release before the rewrite, at the tag and commit the #1770 design entry names.
   - *Procedure (part B).* Add a README section with the exact commands to build that tag in a second checkout (`Calor.Compiler`, `Calor.Tasks` and `Calor.Runtime`, Release) and to run it: `--arm-repo-root <checkout>`, `--calor-dll <checkout>/src/Calor.Compiler/bin/Release/net10.0/calor.dll`, the original starters (the option from step 1 is not set) and the retired canary. `--calor-dll` is required: without it the journal's telemetry compile and `calor ids index` run the harness checkout's 0.30 CLI, which rejects arm A's sources. Each Calor arm needs its own `--arm-label`: arms A, B′ and B all pass `--exemplar` and would otherwise share the label `calor+exemplar` and one output directory.
   - *Primer files (part C).* Add two files and a checksum file beside the thresholds file #1770 places under `bench/phase0-agent-native/registrations/` (the subdirectory name is the implementer's choice): the arm-A exemplar, which is `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/agent-syntax-exemplar.md` exactly as it is at the pinned tag; and the B′ primer, the same sheet translated construct for construct into 0.30 syntax with nothing added or removed beyond what the syntax change forces. Every complete program in the B′ primer must compile with the 0.30 compiler: add the file to an existing compile check or add a test. Arm B's primer is `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/agent-syntax-exemplar.md` at the 0.30 commit #1605 pins. #1605 copies the three SHA-256 values into its registration sub-entry.
6. **Tests.** Replace the retired-syntax fixtures the harness tests still call "current": `tests/fixtures/harness-capture/current-known-effect.calr.txt`, `current-unknown-effect.calr.txt`, and `control_pair` in `tests/test_initialized_workspace.py:31-39`. The product-backed null-agent test selects the first of these as its arm canary (`tests/test_harness_capture.py:59`, `:1552-1553`), so on a 0.30 product it is refused at the canary. Add product-free tests for the new option's admission and path resolution.

Do not modify or delete any existing file under `bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs/` or `epochs/`, or the two existing canary files.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- `bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs/` holds 31 pair directories and 102 `.calr` files, all 102 with retired markers (`find bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs -name '*.calr' | xargs grep -l '§' | wc -l`). 25 directories are two-arm pairs with one starter and one reference file each; the six `W-00x` directories hold the other 52 files.
- `tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs/` holds exactly 50 files: `calor/` and `reference/calor/` for those 25 pairs, none with a retired marker. It holds no `pair.json`, `spec.md`, test or shim. No `.sh` or `.py` file at the top of `bench/phase0-agent-native/` mentions `ActiveSyntax`.
- `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/BenchCorpusCompilesTests.cs:59-72` compiles the 25 references through the mirror with default `CompilationOptions`. It does not compile the starters (`:42-46`), build the generated C# or run a hidden suite. Nothing checks the shims against 0.30 output. `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/ActiveSyntaxInputTests.cs:111-129` requires the set of `.calr` files under the mirror to be exactly the registered original paths, so a `.calr` file with no registered original cannot be added there.
- The arm canary is `templates/calor-arm/permissive-canary.calr` (`run-pair.sh:553`), four lines of retired syntax. The initialized-workspace canary is an inline retired-syntax string (`:575`). The strict check requires a failed build with `error Calor0410` (`:628-635`). The 0.30 lexer reports `Calor0006` for a retired marker (`src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Lexer.cs:491`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Diagnostics/Diagnostic.cs:26`), so a 0.30 arm is refused with exit 3 before any agent starts (`run-pair.sh:674`).
- `--arm-canary <file> --arm-canary-sha256 <sha>` selects other canary bytes, but only for an unregistered arm writing to a new directory outside any pinned epoch (`run-pair.sh:284-293`, `harness-capture.py:464-479`), so it cannot serve a registered run. The two fixtures named `current-*.calr.txt` are retired syntax without source ids.
- The starter and the reference are resolved inside the pair directory only (`run-pair.sh:735-736`; `harness-capture.py:732-741`, `:754-756`).
- `--arm-repo-root` binds the arm template, Calor.Tasks and the canary build to another checkout while the harness runs from this one (`run-pair.sh:267-281`, `:485`). That checkout must contain `src/Calor.Tasks` and `bench/phase0-agent-native/templates` (`:276`). It does not bind the CLI that the journal uses: in an ordinary run that CLI is found under the harness checkout unless `--calor-dll` names another (`run-pair.sh:422-455`).
- `run-epoch.sh` takes `--arms "csharp calor"` (`:17`, `:25`), passes only `--pair`, `--arm`, `--runs`, `--edit-mechanism`, `--null-agent` and `--out` to `run-pair.sh` (`:64-66`), and runs all runs of one arm before the next (`:58`). It cannot express the three Calor arms of #1605, which differ in `--arm-repo-root`, `--calor-dll`, `--exemplar` and `--arm-label`, or interleave them.
- `--exemplar <file>` appends the file to the prompt and sets the label to `<arm>+exemplar` (`run-pair.sh:337-345`, `:1261-1263`); `--arm-label` replaces the label (`:356-358`), and the output directory is keyed by it (`:1777`). The live exemplar is `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/agent-syntax-exemplar.md` (191 lines, 0.30 syntax).
- The last release before 0.30 is 0.29.0 (`CHANGELOG.md:375`). This checkout has one commit and no tags, so the pre-0.30 tag was not inspected. A precedent for building an archived pre-0.30 compiler exists: `tests/E2E/agent-tasks/test-logic-old-release.py` pins `OLD_COMMIT` (`:16`, described there as v0.29), extracts it with `git archive`, copies the Z3 prerequisites and restores in locked mode (`:39-58`). Whether that commit is the 0.29.0 release was not verified.
- The harness tests run at `.github/workflows/test.yml:124`, in the `calor-first-guard` job (`:53`), which builds no compiler. The cases that drive `run-pair.sh` against a product are skipped there (`tests/test_harness_capture.py:1541`, `tests/test_initialized_workspace.py:189`). `tests/test_harness_capture.py:85-89` already overlays the translated sources onto a temporary copy of one pair (`N1-001-string-utils`).
- Compiler output paths are hard-coded to `net10.0` (`run-pair.sh:450-451`, `:715`; `check-probe-pair.sh:37`; `harness-capture.py:483`, `:487`; `templates/calor-arm/CalorArm.csproj.template:26`).

#### Why

#1605 measures whether the 0.30 language is harder or easier for an agent than the pre-0.30 language and than C#. Its three Calor arms are A (the old compiler, the old primer, the original starters), B′ (the 0.30 compiler with that primer mechanically translated) and B (the 0.30 compiler with the shipped 0.30 primer). Today the harness can run only A, and that has not been tried since the rewrite. The translation of the pair files is already done and compiled in CI. What is missing is the wiring, an arm check in the new syntax, proof that the hidden test suites still bind to what the translated programs emit, and an epoch driver that can run those arms and the C# arm interleaved in one epoch, as #1605 requires.

#### Done when

- With the option set, `run-pair.sh --null-agent` exits 0 for the Calor arm and the C# arm of each of the 25 pairs. Every `result.json` has `taskSuccess: true`, `escapedBugs: 0` and `finalBuild.ok: true`; the Calor ones have `armCanary: "strict-ok"`. The PR adds the null epoch's `pins.json` and `rollup.json` under a new `bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/<name>-null/` directory (both arms: `successRate` 1, `meanEscaped` 0, 25 runs) and states the command line.
- `check-probe-pair.sh` with the option exits 0 for all nine `W5*` pairs.
- A product-free test drives `run-epoch.sh` with a three-entry arm list and a stub `run-pair.sh` and asserts the interleaved order and the entries in `pins.json`.
- `git diff --diff-filter=MD --name-only <base> -- bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs bench/phase0-agent-native/templates/calor-arm/permissive-canary.calr bench/phase0-agent-native/templates/calor-arm/policy-canary.calr.txt` prints nothing.
- `python3 -m unittest discover -s bench/phase0-agent-native/tests` passes both with a built 0.30 Release product and without one. The pull request states the machine the product-backed run was made on, because CI does not run it.
- `bench/phase0-agent-native/README.md` documents the option and the 0.30 canary.
- Parts B and C, if the #1770 design entry registers arm A:
  - `--canary-only` against the pinned pre-0.30 checkout reports `armCanary: "strict-ok"`;
  - `--null-agent` with `--arm-repo-root` and `--calor-dll` passes for the Calor arm of each pair that entry registers; each `result.json` has a `compilerHash` different from the 0.30 arm's and a `calorDll` under the pinned checkout, and no record in its `journal.jsonl` carries a `Calor0006` diagnostic (run with the plain label `calor`, because until #1764 lands the journal captures diagnostics only under that label);
  - the arm-A exemplar, the B′ primer and the checksum file exist, and a CI test compiles every complete program in the B′ primer; if that test is a new xUnit case, `expectedTotal` for its project in `eng/test-manifest.json` is raised by the number of cases added;
  - the README documents the arm-A procedure.
- Otherwise: the issue carries a comment linking the design entry that registered no arm A.

#### Not in scope

- The six `W-00x` pairs: #1648 decides them.
- Isolating the workspace from the checkout: #1762.
- A CI job that runs `run-pair.sh` against a built compiler. No such job exists (see the baseline), and this issue adds none: CI time and flakiness are already the subject of epic #1594 in 0.31. The validation above is run locally and recorded in the pull request; #1807 repeats it after the 0.32 rewrite.
- Following a change of tool framework. If #1639 moves the tool off `net10.0`, #1639 updates the hard-coded output paths listed in the baseline.
- `guarantees-analyze.py`, which finds seeded declarations by retired markers, and a spend stop in the epoch driver: #1815.

Part of epic #1761. Blocks #1762, #1764, #1767, #1605, #1789 and #1815.

### #1762 · [0.31] Benchmark arm isolation: the agent can neither name nor read the checkout

**Action:** new · **Labels:** tests, p1 · **Depends on:** #1766 · **Blocks:** #1764, #1605 · **Displaces:** #1641

#### Change

Add an isolated mode to `bench/phase0-agent-native/run-pair.sh` and make it the default for every run that invokes an agent. It has four steps, all verified without a paid run. Paths below without a directory are under `bench/phase0-agent-native/`.

Terms: an *arm* is one configuration under test (language, compiler build, prompt content); the *staged product* is a copy of the compiler binaries an arm builds against; the *hidden tests* are the pair's test suite, which the harness runs silently and the agent must never see; `--null-agent` applies the reference solution in place of an agent and costs nothing.

Parts. The work may be delivered as two ordered pull requests: first steps 1 and 2 (the staged product and the path scrub), then steps 3 and 4 (the read sandbox, the canary and the read log).

Platform. Build the sandbox for the platform the design entry of #1770 names for the paid runs. The plan's recommendation there is macOS with `sandbox-exec` (Seatbelt), the only read-sandbox precedent in the tree. On that choice this issue builds no Linux mechanism: the tests that need the sandbox are skipped on the Linux CI runners, and the evidence for the done-when is a run on macOS recorded in the pull request. If the entry names Linux, build a Linux mechanism (a mount namespace or Landlock) and say in the README whether macOS is also supported. If the second pull request starts before that entry is merged, build for macOS and state the assumption in the pull request.

1. **Staged product.** For each arm product root, copy what a workspace build needs to a directory outside every checkout: `Sdk.targets`, `Calor.Tasks.dll` with its dependencies and `libz3.*`, `Calor.Runtime.dll`, and the `calor.dll` CLI with its dependencies. The roots are the 0.30 checkout and, only if the #1770 design entry registers arm A, the pinned pre-0.30 checkout; that half waits for the entry. Render the arm project against the staged copy with no `ProjectReference` and no path into a checkout. Point the MCP registration, the `calor` wrapper and the telemetry compile at the staged CLI. Record the staged files' SHA-256 and the source commit in `result.json`. `compilerHash` from a workspace build must equal the arm canary's.
2. **Nothing names a checkout.** After the agent exits and before the workspace is deleted, scan the workspace tree (`obj/` and `bin/` included), the prompt, the agent's environment and the directory that holds the PATH shim for the absolute path of the harness checkout and of each arm checkout. A hit makes the run invalid. This requires moving the shim out of the run's output directory and removing harness paths from its text.
3. **Read sandbox.** Launch the agent's process tree so that reads fail outside an allow-list: the workspace, the staged product, the toolchain a build needs (the .NET SDK, the NuGet cache), and the agent client's own installation, credentials and configuration. Everything else is denied, in particular the harness checkout, every arm checkout, the run's output directory (it holds the hidden tests), and the rest of the operator's home directory, including other clones and any globally installed `calor` tool. Use an operating-system mechanism. State the supported platforms in the README. An explicit `--no-isolation` is allowed and is recorded as `isolation.mode: "off"` in `result.json`, so an analysis can refuse such runs. Without it, a run that would invoke an agent on an unsupported platform exits 3.

   The `dotnet` PATH shim runs inside the agent's process tree. Today it performs the silent rebuild, the hidden-suite run and every journal write itself, none of which it can do under the sandbox. Move that observation outside: either an observer process the shim only notifies, or a snapshot of the source tree per invocation inside the workspace, replayed after the agent exits. `feedback_latency_ms` keeps its meaning (the agent-visible command only).
4. **Pre-flight canary and read log.** Before the first agent run of an invocation, run a probe inside the same sandbox wrapper that tries to read one file from each of: a `pairs/<pair>/reference/` directory, the ActiveSyntax mirror (`tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/`), `epochs/`, the run's `heldout/` directory, and each arm checkout. Every read must fail, and reading a workspace file and the staged `Sdk.targets` must succeed; otherwise exit 3. Record the outcome as `isolation.canary`. After each run write `isolation.outsideAttempts` to `result.json`: every tool call in `transcript.jsonl` (file tools and shell commands) that names a path outside the allow-list, with the tool, the path and whether the call failed. Add the sandbox's own denial log where the platform offers one. A run in which a read outside the allow-list succeeded is invalid (reason `outside-read`, handled like the existing invalid reasons: logged to `invalid.txt` and re-attempted by the loop at `run-pair.sh:1843-1867`). A denied attempt is recorded and counted and does not invalidate the run.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- Workspaces are already created outside the checkout (`run-pair.sh:1789`, `mktemp -d "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/p0-…"`). The leak has other routes.
- `templates/calor-arm/CalorArm.csproj.template` substitutes `__REPO_ROOT__` at line 26 (`CalorTasksAssembly`), lines 40 and 41 (two `ProjectReference` items) and line 50 (the `Import` of `Sdk.targets`). `render_template` (`run-pair.sh:494-498`) substitutes the arm checkout's absolute path, and `:762` writes the result into the agent's workspace as `src/Src.csproj`. The arm root must be a full checkout (`:275-277`).
- The agent runs from `$ws/src` with `--dangerously-skip-permissions` (`run-pair.sh:1352`, `:1383-1387`), so it has an unrestricted shell.
- The default output directory is inside the checkout (`:177`). The hidden tests are copied to `$ws_out/heldout` (`:731`, `:781-782`). The shim directory `$WS_OUT/.shim` is first on the agent's `PATH` (`:1803`, `:1383`), and the shim's text contains `$ws_out`, the harness script directory and the CLI path (`:986-1175`).
- The shim does the observation itself: silent rebuild and hidden-suite run (`:1076-1087`), telemetry compile (`:1112-1152`), journal append (`:1156-1171`).
- The 0.30 answer keys are at `tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs/<pair>/reference/calor/` (25 files); the originals are at `bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs/<pair>/reference/`.
- Evidence, `epochs/e1a-attribution` (2026-07-16, pre-0.30 syntax, 60 runs of the Calor arm without an exemplar over two pairs): 16 final messages mention the task's reference solution and 2 state "my implementation follows it" (`N1-003-csv-row/calor/run-15`, `W3-003-kv-journal/calor/run-17`). The exemplar arm has 1 mention and the C# arm none. Measured in that directory by `for f in */calor/run-*/agent.json; do jq -r '.result // empty' "$f" | grep -qiE 'reference solution|answer key|/reference/' && echo "$f"; done | wc -l`. This counts only runs that volunteered it.
- `harness-capture.py isolate-workspace` (`:441-452`) writes a no-op `Directory.Build.props` and `Directory.Build.targets` and a `Directory.Packages.props` that turns central package management off, so that builds do not inherit repository settings. It restricts no read.
- Precedents in the tree: a Seatbelt profile builder with `(deny file-read* (subpath …))` rules in the paused PP-W request gateway (`ppw-gateway-client.py:283-316`; macOS only, `:238-240`) and its probe (`probe-ppw-gateway.py:185-193`); a shim that notifies an observer instead of observing (`run-pair.sh:1010-1021`); source snapshots per invocation behind `CALOR_LOOP_SNAPSHOTS` (`:1067-1074`); a local `claude` stub first on `PATH` that drives the real agent path with no provider (`tests/test_initialized_workspace.py:336-404`). No Linux read sandbox exists anywhere under `bench/phase0-agent-native/`.
- The harness tests run in the `calor-first-guard` job on `ubuntu-latest`, which builds no compiler (`.github/workflows/test.yml:53-56`, `:124`), so product-backed tests are skipped in CI on every platform choice.
- `src/Calor.Sdk/Sdk/Sdk.targets:8` and `:35-39` already resolve `Calor.Tasks.dll` and `Calor.Runtime.dll` from `../tasks/net10.0/` beside the targets file, which is the layout of the packed SDK. A staged copy in that layout needs no `ProjectReference`. Not verified for the pre-0.30 tag.

#### Why

An evaluation of a language the model has never seen is an evaluation of what the agent can find to read. An agent that can open the repository reads the full reference, 1,823 `.calr` files of which 853 are in the retired dialect (`git ls-files '*.calr'`, then `grep -l '§'`), and the answer key. The result then measures none of the arms #1605 defines, which differ only in what reference the agent is given. Creating workspaces outside the checkout does not prevent this: the July agents name the project file as the route ("the reference being reachable from the fixture's own project file", `epochs/e1a-attribution/N1-003-csv-row/calor/run-15/agent.json`), and the agent has a shell. Isolation has to be enforced by the operating system and proved before each run.

#### Done when

- Isolated `--null-agent`: both arms of each of the 25 translated pairs pass as in #1766, and for each run `iterations`, `iterationsToGreen` and the journal's `heldout_pass` and `heldout_fail` equal those of the same run with `--no-isolation`.
- `result.json` of every isolated run carries `isolation` with `mode`, `platform`, `canary`, `stagedProduct` (commit, and a SHA-256 per file), `pathScrub` and `outsideAttempts`; `pathScrub.hits` is empty.
- A test with a local `claude` stub (no provider) that tries to read a reference file, a mirror reference, an archived epoch file, the run's hidden test file and `docs/syntax-reference/` in the arm checkout, and emits the `tool_use` and `tool_result` events for each read as the client's stream does: all five reads fail inside the sandbox, `outsideAttempts` lists five denied entries and the run is valid. The same stub with `--no-isolation` yields `invalid: true` with reason `outside-read`.
- The pre-flight canary exits 3 when the sandbox wrapper is replaced by a no-op (a negative control in the tests).
- On an unsupported platform a run that would invoke an agent without `--no-isolation` exits 3 with a message naming the supported platforms; `--null-agent` still runs.
- The sandbox tests are skipped where the platform has no mechanism. The pull request records the platform, the operating-system version and the output of the test run on the platform #1770 names, because CI does not run them.
- If the #1770 design entry registers arm A, the first three bullets also hold with `--arm-repo-root` set to the pinned pre-0.30 checkout.
- `bench/phase0-agent-native/README.md` documents the mode, the allow-list, the supported platforms and whether a Linux mechanism exists.

#### Not in scope

- The PP-W gateway's spending, registration and request-proxy parts. Only its sandbox profile is a precedent; the paused research is not reactivated.
- Network restrictions. The agent client needs its provider; this issue restricts file reads.
- The repository-wide allowlist of retired files: #1763. With the checkout unreadable, #1605 does not depend on it.

Part of epic #1761. Depends on #1766. Blocks #1764 and #1605.

### #1764 · [0.31] Loop telemetry: fix flags on diagnostic entries, and build diagnostics captured for the C# arm

**Action:** new · **Labels:** tests, p1 · **Depends on:** #1766, #1762 · **Blocks:** #1767, #1760, #1605, #1815 · **Displaces:** #1744

#### Change

The pair harness journals one record per build or test the agent runs (`journal.jsonl`, schema `loop-telemetry/2`). At HEAD the records are written by a `dotnet` PATH shim that `bench/phase0-agent-native/run-pair.sh` generates (`write_shim`, `:977-1177`) with its helper `telemetry-helpers.py`. #1762 moves that observation (silent rebuild, hidden-suite run, telemetry compile, journal append) out of the agent's process tree; make the changes below in the observation path as #1762 leaves it. No paid agent run is part of this issue. Paths below without a directory are under `bench/phase0-agent-native/`.

Terms: an *arm* is one configuration under test, identified in the archive by its *label* (`calor`, `calor+exemplar`, `csharp`, or any `--arm-label`); the *hidden suite* is the task's test suite, which the harness runs silently after each build and never shows the agent; the *telemetry compile* is the harness's own `calor --format json` compile of the agent's `.calr` files, whose JSON output is the source of a record's `diagnostics`.

1. **Fix flags.** Each entry of a record's `diagnostics` array gains `severity`, `hasFix` (the compiler's JSON entry carries a `fix` object) and `isSafeToApply` (`fix.isSafeToApply`; false when there is no fix). `code` and `declarationId` stay.
2. **Capture for every Calor arm.** Gate the Calor-only steps on the arm language, not on the arm label.
3. **Build diagnostics for both arms.** Add a `build_diagnostics` array, parsed from the silent rebuild the harness already performs: one `{code, severity, generated}` per distinct MSBuild-format diagnostic whose code is not a `Calor` code, where `generated` is true when the file is a `.g.cs`. Cap it at 50 with a `build_diagnostics_truncated` flag. For the C# arm this is the only diagnostic record. For the Calor arm it holds the C# compiler's errors on generated code, which the telemetry compile (it compiles `.calr` only) cannot see.
4. **Keep the evidence.** Archive the compiler's JSON output and the rebuild log once per distinct `src_tree_hash` under the run directory (`envelopes/<hash>.json`, `build-logs/<hash>.txt`) instead of overwriting `.envelope.json` and `.src_build.txt`.
5. **No harness writes in the workspace.** Run `calor ids index` on a copy of the changed file, as the telemetry compile already does with its inputs, not on the file in `$ws/src`.
6. **Schema.** Bump the discriminator to `loop-telemetry/3` (the `mcp-write/1` to `/2` change is the precedent). Update `loop-telemetry.schema.json`, the hand-written validator in `validate-telemetry.sh` (it must keep accepting archived `/2` records), `loop-telemetry-schema.md`, the `telemetrySchema` pin that `run-epoch.sh:46` writes (and the same literal at `run-m5-epoch.sh:207` and `run-guarantees-epoch.sh:150`, which also call `run-pair.sh` and would otherwise pin `/2` over `/3` journals; nothing else in those two drivers changes), and the two filters in `extract_metrics` that select records by the literal `loop-telemetry/2` (`run-pair.sh:1598`, `:1601`); left as they are, `meanFeedbackLatencyMs` and `envelopeValidAll` in `result.json` would read null for every `/3` run.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- `loop-telemetry-schema.md:32`: `diagnostics` is an array of `{code, declarationId?}`. `:33`: `envelope_valid` is null for the C# arm. `telemetry-helpers.py:112-138` copies only those two keys. `loop-telemetry.schema.json` forbids other keys on an entry (`additionalProperties: false`), and so does `validate-telemetry.sh:198`.
- The compiler's JSON entry is `EnvelopeDiagnostic` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Diagnostics/DiagnosticEnvelope.cs:11-32`): `code`, `message`, `severity`, `location`, optional `declarationId`, `verification`, `suggestion`, and `fix` with `description`, `isSafeToApply` and `edits` (`:76-81`). It has no `hasFix` field; the presence of `fix` is the flag.
- Five call sites create a fix marked safe, all under `src/Calor.Compiler/`: `Parsing/Parser.cs:621`, `Parsing/Lexer.cs:398` and `:407` (conditionally), `Formatting/CalorFormatter.cs:441` and `:481`.
- Label gating. The shim sets `arm="$ARM_LABEL"` (`run-pair.sh:1025`) and then tests `"$arm" == "calor"` before attributing edits (`:1098`) and before the telemetry compile (`:1119`; the only other case admitted there is the two arm keys of the paused PP-W design). A label such as `calor+exemplar`, `calor+initialized` or any `--arm-label` fails the test, and its records carry `diagnostics: []`, `envelope_valid: null` and `edit_target_ids: []`. The archive agrees: all 94 journal records of `epochs/w-rows-dry-001` and `w-rows-dry-002` (labels `calor+v0.15.0` and `calor+v0.14.3-pre-rows`) have `envelope_valid: null` although each run's `result.json` names a compiler, while the earlier `epochs/e1-rows-parity-001` has it populated for labelled arms (measured with `jq` over the `journal.jsonl` files). All three Calor arms of #1605 pass `--exemplar` or `--arm-label`.
- The telemetry compile is commented "calor arm only" (`:1112`). In both arms the shim rebuilds `$ws/src/Src.csproj` after every observed `build`, `test` or `run` and writes the log to `.src_build.txt`, overwritten each time (`:1079`). Nothing parses it.
- `calor ids index` synchronises the identity sidecar for the files it is given before it exports (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/IdsCommand.cs:184`), and the shim passes the workspace file (`run-pair.sh:1102`). Whether this creates `.calor/ids.json` in the agent's workspace on 0.30 was not verified by execution.
- `validate-telemetry.sh` is called by no test and no workflow. Its entry check also restricts `code` to `Calor` plus four digits (`:201`), which is narrower than the JSON schema.
- `extract_metrics` filters journal records on the literal `loop-telemetry/2` when it computes `meanFeedbackLatencyMs` and `envelopeValidAll` (`run-pair.sh:1598-1602`). Three epoch drivers pin the same literal (`run-epoch.sh:46`, `run-m5-epoch.sh:207`, `run-guarantees-epoch.sh:150`); the last two serve a paused and a closed programme. `ppw-run-observer.py:421` writes its own `/2` records for the paused PP-W design and is not touched.

#### Why

The 0.30 evaluation has to say, per arm, which kinds of error cost the agent iterations and how many of the diagnostics it hit offered a fix. Archived runs answer neither question, and the missing fields cannot be recomputed later. An entry records only a code and a declaration id, the source of each iteration is not kept, and whether a fix is safe depends on the instance and not on the code: the tab-indentation fix is marked safe only when the file has no mixed indentation (`src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Lexer.cs:398`). No diagnostic at all is recorded for the C# arm, so a per-arm comparison of failure classes has one empty side. These fields must exist before the first paid run of #1605. The report that reads them (#1760) can follow later.

#### Done when

- Unit tests under `bench/phase0-agent-native/tests/` (run by `.github/workflows/test.yml:124`) cover:
  - a compiler JSON fixture with one diagnostic carrying a safe fix, one carrying an unsafe fix and one with none, yielding the three flag combinations;
  - an MSBuild log fixture with a `CS` error in a hand-written file, a `CS` error in a `.g.cs` file, a warning and a `Calor` error, yielding two error entries (one with `generated: true`), one warning entry and no `Calor` entry;
  - `validate-telemetry.sh` accepting a `/3` record and an archived `/2` record, and rejecting a `/3` record without the new fields.
- With a built 0.30 product, `run-pair.sh --null-agent --exemplar <file>` on one pair writes journal records whose `schema` is `loop-telemetry/3`, whose `arm` is `calor+exemplar` and whose `envelope_valid` is `true`, and a `result.json` whose `meanFeedbackLatencyMs` is a number and whose `envelopeValidAll` is `true`. This is a product-backed test, skipped without a product like `NullAgentEndToEnd` (`tests/test_harness_capture.py:1541`); the pull request records where it ran.
- A null epoch written by `run-epoch.sh` after the change has `telemetrySchema: "loop-telemetry/3"` in its `pins.json`, and `git grep -n 'telemetrySchema:"loop-telemetry/2"' -- bench/phase0-agent-native` prints nothing.
- A run of the C# arm with a local `claude` stub in place of the agent (the precedent is `tests/test_initialized_workspace.py:336-404`; the stub writes a compile error, builds, fixes it, builds) yields a first record with a `CS` code in `build_diagnostics` and a second record with none. The test is skipped where no .NET SDK is installed.
- A product-backed test asserts, before cleanup, that a Calor-arm workspace contains no `.calor/` directory that the starter did not ship.
- `envelopes/` and `build-logs/` in a run directory hold one file per distinct `src_tree_hash` in the journal.
- `loop-telemetry-schema.md` documents every new field and states whether `declarationId` is populated on 0.30, where declaration ids live in a sidecar and the telemetry compile sees copies without one.

#### Not in scope

- Any metric computed from these fields: #1760.
- A target for the share of diagnostics with a safe fix. It is reported descriptively. That no effect-row or contract fix is ever marked safe is pinned by #1801.

Part of epic #1761. Depends on #1766 and #1762. Blocks #1767, #1760 and #1605.

### #1767 · [0.31] Harness support for delivery variants: variant selection, CLI-only switch, primer-read log, starter-less fixtures

**Action:** new · **Labels:** tests · **Depends on:** #1766, #1773, #1771, #1770, #1764 · **Displaces:** #1733

#### Change

Extend `bench/phase0-agent-native/run-pair.sh --initialized-workspace`, the mode that runs the real `calor init` in a fresh workspace, so that stage 2 of #1605 can run. Stage 2 compares how the syntax primer reaches the agent, on the two pairs where Calor cost extra iterations in July (`N1-003-csv-row`, `W3-003-kv-journal`). Its arms and n are fixed in #1770. No paid agent run is part of this issue. Paths below without a directory are under `bench/phase0-agent-native/`.

Terms: a *pair* is one task with a specification, a hidden test suite and a starting fixture (the *starter*) per arm; an *arm* is one configuration under test; the *primer* is the syntax sheet served as the MCP resource `calor://primer`; a *delivery variant* is one way that sheet reaches the agent; an *iteration* is one journalled build or test invocation that follows an edit; `--null-agent` applies the reference solution in place of an agent and costs nothing.

Gate. This issue is built only if the design entry of #1770 registers stage 2 (its option with stage 1 and stage 2). The gate's value is that entry, an entry in Annex A of `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md`; it has no numeric threshold.

- *Open:* the entry is merged and registers stage 2. Work starts; the spend record of #1770 may follow later.
- *Shut:* the entry is merged and registers no stage 2 (stage 1 only, the reduced design, or no paid run). Close this issue as not planned and link the entry.
- *Held:* the entry is not merged. No part starts and the issue stays open. It gates stage 2 only, which does not hold the 0.31 release; stage 2 must publish, or be recorded on #1605 as not run, before #1823 is decided.

1. **Variant selection.** A `--delivery <variant>` option whose values are the `calor init` options #1773 adds: the pointer (as shipped), the primer inlined in the generated agent file, and the primer inlined plus the starter `.calr` file that `calor init` writes. The harness passes the option to the real `init` command and never rewrites a generated file. The variant goes into `initialization.json` and into the arm label, so two variants cannot share an output directory.
2. **CLI-only switch.** An option under which no MCP server is offered to the agent. Pass an empty server list with `--strict-mcp-config`, so that neither the registration `calor init` generated nor the operator's own servers load. Initialization must not fail for lack of an MCP primer read in this arm; the pre-run primer check goes through the CLI command #1771 adds. The `calor` command stays on `PATH`.
3. **Primer-read log.** `result.json` gains `primerRead`: a count per channel (the MCP resource `calor://primer`, `calor_help`, the CLI command) and whether the first read precedes the first edit of a `.calr` file. Take it from the traffic itself, journalled outside the agent. For the two MCP channels that is either a journal the server writes when an environment variable is set (the precedent is `CALOR_MCP_WRITE_LOG`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/Tools/FileWriteTool.cs:65`) or a stdio proxy the harness places in front of the server; the implementer chooses and the README says which. For the CLI channel it is a journal written by the `calor` wrapper the harness already puts on `PATH`. The transcript is a cross-check only: no archived transcript in the repository contains a resource read, so its event shape in the client's stream is unverified.
4. **Starter-less fixtures.** For each pair #1770 registers for stage 2, add a new pair directory (a new id, valid under `task-spec-schema.json`) whose starting fixture holds no source file in either arm. Its `spec.md` states the whole public surface, including the operations the original starter already implemented, and names for each arm the file, the namespace and the container the hidden suite's shim binds to. The hidden tests, shims and reference solutions are those of the original pair, with the Calor reference in 0.30 syntax. `materialize` must accept a fixture without a source file.
5. **`calor check` is an iteration.** In an initialized workspace the `calor` wrapper runs the CLI directly, so a `calor check` (build, test and verify in one command) leaves no journal record and is not counted, while every `dotnet build` of the C# arm is. Journal each `calor check` through the same observation as `dotnet build` (edit detection, iteration ordinal, silent hidden-suite run), as a record with `cmd: "check"` in the schema #1764 leaves; add that value to the `cmd` enum of `loop-telemetry.schema.json` and to `validate-telemetry.sh`. Other `calor` subcommands stay unjournalled.
6. **MCP compiles are counted.** The generated agent file tells the agent to compile with the MCP tool `calor_compile` after each edit, and those calls leave no journal record either. Record in `result.json` the number of `calor_compile` calls per run and how many followed an edit of a `.calr` file, from the step-3 journal or proxy. Whether such a call is an iteration is a rule of the #1770 design entry. If the entry says yes, journal each one as step 5 does, with `cmd: "compile"`; if it says no or is silent, `iterations` is unchanged and the README states that MCP compiles are reported beside it.

Deviation to state, not to fix: initialized-workspace arms run without the harness's `.g.cs` write block, and the product hook they run with allows `.g.cs` writes. The #1770 design entry records this deviation; the README section for this mode repeats it.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- `--initialized-workspace` accepts only the ordinary strict Calor arm with raw edits and rejects `--arm-label` and `--canary-only` (`run-pair.sh:294-310`). It rejects pairs that have a `smoke/` directory or a `defect.json` (`:364-367`). The label gains the suffix `+initialized` (`:359`): `calor+initialized`, or `calor+exemplar+initialized` with `--exemplar`.
- `harness-capture.py initialize_workspace` always runs `init --ai claude --project src/Src.csproj` (`:610-611`), fails unless init generated the `calor` MCP registration with arguments `mcp --stdio` (`:634-638`), reads `calor://primer` from the selected compiler over MCP as a control (`:640-658`), and always writes `mcp-config.json` (`:676-678`). Its docstring forbids reconstructing generated files (`:564-567`). `run-pair.sh:1331` passes `--mcp-config … --strict-mcp-config` whenever that file exists, so no arm runs without the server.
- `calor init` has four options: `--ai`, `--project`, `--solution`, `--force` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/InitCommand.cs:16-33`). The generated agent file says "Read `calor://primer`" (`src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Templates/CLAUDE.md.template:9`) and "Compile with `calor_compile` after each edit" (`:13`), and init writes no copy of the primer. The primer is registered only as an MCP resource (`src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/McpMessageHandler.cs:124-130`).
- `--exemplar` only appends text to the prompt (`run-pair.sh:1261-1263`). In this mode its bytes are archived with the initialized inputs (`:752-756`).
- In this mode the harness writes a `calor` wrapper into the shim directory (`run-pair.sh:982-985`). The wrapper execs the real `dotnet` with the CLI, bypassing the journalling `dotnet` shim, so no `calor` invocation produces a journal record. `calor check` is the CLI's "Build, test and verify one source snapshot" command (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/TestCommand.cs:65`). `iterations` in `result.json` counts journal records with `edited: true` (`run-pair.sh:1496`), and the journal's `cmd` enum is `build`, `test`, `run` (`loop-telemetry.schema.json:16`).
- In this mode the harness does not install its `.g.cs` write block (`run-pair.sh:899`), and the product hook that `calor init` installs allows `.g.cs` writes (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/HookCommand.cs:283-287`).
- Every two-arm pair ships a starter in both arms, and the specifications rely on it. `pairs/N1-003-csv-row/spec.md` has a section "Existing behavior (already implemented in the starting fixture)" and names functions only. The names the hidden suite binds to are in the shims: `CsvRow.CsvRowModule` for Calor and `CsvRowLib.CsvRow` for C# (`pairs/N1-003-csv-row/tests/shims/TestShim.calor.cs`, `TestShim.csharp.cs`). `materialize` requires the fixture directory to exist (`run-pair.sh:735`).
- A no-provider test already drives this mode with a local `claude` stub (`tests/test_initialized_workspace.py:336-404`).

#### Why

The only lever with a measured effect on the cost of writing new Calor is the in-context syntax reference: in July a 60-line exemplar appended to the prompt removed 55% of the excess iterations (`epochs/e1a-attribution/VERDICT.md:11-28`, pre-0.30 syntax, unpinned model). `calor init` ships a pointer to that reference, and whether agents follow the pointer has never been measured. Stage 2 measures it. Three things stand in the way: the harness can produce no variant but the pointer, it cannot run without the MCP server, and every pair hands the agent a starter file, which #1605 itself calls "the main exemplar" and which would mask the difference between variants. A fourth distorts the reading: iterations are counted from journalled `dotnet` invocations, so a Calor agent that checks its work with `calor check` appears to iterate less than a C# agent that runs `dotnet build`. An agent that compiles through the MCP tool is undercounted the same way. The same count is read again for the per-agent Calor arms in 0.33 (#1811, through the drivers of #1651).

#### Done when

- A product-backed test (skipped without a built compiler) initializes a workspace for each variant. It asserts that the archived agent file differs between pointer and inline as #1773 specifies, that the starter variant contains the `.calr` file `calor init` wrote, and that `initialization.json` records the variant.
- A stub-agent test under the CLI-only switch asserts that the client's argument list offers no `calor` server, and that `calor` on `PATH` prints the primer through the #1771 command.
- A no-provider test with four sessions (one reads the MCP resource by speaking MCP to the journalled server, as `harness-capture.py:640-658` already does; one calls `calor_help` for the primer; one runs the CLI command through the wrapper; one reads nothing) yields `primerRead` counts for resource, help and CLI of 1/0/0, 0/1/0, 0/0/1 and 0/0/0.
- For each registered stage-2 pair, the starter-less pair passes `--null-agent` in both arms (ordinary mode for C#, initialized mode for Calor) with `escapedBugs: 0`, and its `pair.json` validates against `task-spec-schema.json`.
- Two variants run back to back write to different run directories.
- A no-provider test in an initialized workspace whose stub edits a `.calr` file, runs `calor check`, edits again and runs `calor check` again: `journal.jsonl` holds two records with `cmd: "check"` and `edited: true`, `result.json` has `iterations: 2`, and `validate-telemetry.sh` accepts the journal. A `calor --version` call by the same stub adds no record.
- A no-provider test whose session edits a `.calr` file and then calls `calor_compile` on the journalled server yields a `result.json` that reports one `calor_compile` call, made after an edit. If the #1770 design entry counts such a call as an iteration, the journal also holds a record with `cmd: "compile"` and `iterations` is 1; otherwise `iterations` is 0.
- If the primer-read log is a journal written by the MCP server, the product change has a test under `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/`, and `expectedTotal` for that project in `eng/test-manifest.json` is raised by the number of tests added.
- The product-backed tests are skipped in CI, which builds no compiler in the job that runs the harness tests; the pull request records the machine and the output of the run.
- `bench/phase0-agent-native/README.md` documents the options, the `check` record, how MCP compiles are counted and the `.g.cs` deviation.
- Or, with the gate shut: the issue is closed as not planned with a link to the #1770 design entry.

#### Not in scope

- The `calor init` options themselves (#1773), and the primer command and `calor_help` action (#1771).
- Which variants run, n per variant and the decision rule: #1770.
- Resource-read support in other agent clients: #1820, which cites this issue's primer-read log for Claude Code.
- Blocking `.g.cs` writes in `calor init` workspaces: the deviation is recorded in #1770, not removed here.
- What an ordinary (non-initialized) arm sees.

Part of epic #1761. Depends on #1766, #1764, #1770 (its design entry), #1773 and #1771. Blocks stage 2 of #1605.

### #1760 · [0.31] Report script for the 0.30 evaluation: failure classes, opportunity counts, dead-end rate, repeat rate, blocked test writes

**Action:** new · **Labels:** tests · **Depends on:** #1764 · **Blocks:** #1811, #1835 · **Displaces:** #1747 · **May slip:** yes

#### Change

Add a report script under `bench/phase0-agent-native/` (for example `eval-report.py`) that reads an archived epoch directory and prints one JSON document and one Markdown table per arm and pair. It runs after collection, on archives, and invokes no agent. Inputs per run: `result.json`, `journal.jsonl`, `transcript.jsonl`, `agent-builds.jsonl`, `final-src/`, and `initialization.json` where present. Paths below without a directory are under `bench/phase0-agent-native/`.

Terms: a *pair* is one task with a specification, a hidden test suite and a starting fixture per arm; an *epoch* is one archived collection in which all arms ran interleaved; an *arm* is one configuration under test; an *iteration* is a journal record with `edited: true`; an *escaped defect* is a hidden test that still fails when the agent declares the task done; an *opportunity* is a place in a task where a defect of a given class could occur.

Three things are checked in with the script:

- **The class table**, `bench/phase0-agent-native/diagnostic-classes.json` (the name is the implementer's choice): one entry per diagnostic code, `Calor` and `CS`, giving its class. A code that is not in the table is reported as `unclassified`.
- **The definitions**, in a section of `bench/phase0-agent-native/README.md` that other issues can cite. This issue owns the definition of *first-build pass rate*: `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md` does not define it, and #1832 and #1835 use the definition written here.
- **No thresholds.** They are read from the thresholds file that #1770 registers (proposed there as `bench/phase0-agent-native/registrations/eval-0.30.json`), passed by path. Given none, the script prints "threshold not registered" for every outcome.

Each column is defined in that README section, repeated in the script's docstring and pinned by a test:

| Column | Definition |
|---|---|
| Failure class of each failed iteration | A failed iteration is a journal record with `edited: true` that is not green (`heldout_fail > 0`; the harness counts a state that does not build as all hidden tests failing). When the record carries an error diagnostic, its class comes from the class table (the `diagnostics` and `build_diagnostics` arrays of #1764). When it carries none (the build succeeds and hidden tests fail), the class is `logic`. Classes: unknown or removed syntax; type error; null; missing case; resource leak; effect; contract; logic; unclassified. |
| Attempts to write removed forms | Write and Edit tool calls on `.calr` files in the transcript whose content contains a retired `§` marker or an `Option` or `Result` construction. Counted under "unknown or removed syntax" whether or not a build followed. |
| Failure class of each escaped defect | An escaped defect is a hidden test that fails when the agent declares the task done (`heldoutFinal.failedTests`). Its class comes from an optional per-pair map of test name to class, and is `logic` when the pair has no map. |
| Opportunity count per class | Per task, counted on the pair's reference solutions in both languages by a documented rule: nullable-typed values (null), matches or switches over a non-bool type (missing case), `IDisposable` locals or `using` (resource leak). |
| Outcome per class | Above threshold, below threshold, held (between the two) or not measurable (too few opportunities or runs), each as the thresholds file defines it; these are the four readings #1770 registers. Neither held nor not measurable is ever reported as below. |
| C#-habit events per form | Per form in the rubric committed with the registration (#1770 item 9): the number of valid arm runs with at least one failed iteration that the rubric maps to the form, over the valid runs on pairs whose reference uses the form, and the number of pairs. The outcome (admissible, not admissible, not measurable) is read from the thresholds file. |
| First-build pass rate | Share of valid runs whose first edited build-or-test invocation exits 0. The invocation is the first journal record with `edited: true`, whatever its `cmd` (`build`, `test`, `run`, or `check` once #1767 journals it), and the exit code is the agent-visible one (`exit`). A run with no such record is counted in the denominator and not in the numerator. First-try green (`iterationsToGreen == 1`) is reported beside it. |
| `.g.cs` dead-end rate | The definition at `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md:42`, with one stated difference: the share of Calor-arm runs with (a) an attempted Write or Edit on a `.g.cs` path, blocked or not, or (b) at least one wasted iteration on an error located in a `.g.cs` file. Wasted means two consecutive compiles with identical errors (code plus message, with paths and line numbers stripped) and no `.calr` edit between them; for a runtime error, the agent's next action must also reference the `.g.cs` frame. The gates document counts only blocked writes in (a); initialized-workspace arms have no block, so every attempt is counted and the two kinds are reported separately. |
| Calor0006 hits | Per run, the number of iterations whose diagnostics include `Calor0006`. |
| Repeat rate per diagnostic code | Per arm and code: among runs that hit the code, the share that hit it in two or more iterations, and the mean number of iterations. |
| Turns before the first build | Distinct top-level assistant messages before the first build, test or `calor` invocation. |
| Fix coverage | Share of agent-hit diagnostic entries with `hasFix`, and with `isSafeToApply`. Descriptive, not a target. |
| Blocked test writes | In initialized-workspace arms: Write calls on a test-like `.cs` path that the product hook refused. In other arms: reported as not observable, with the number of attempted test-file creations for information. |

A field that an archive lacks (older telemetry, no transcript) prints as "not available", never as zero.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- No script computes any of these. The existing analyzers are specific to one epoch each (`ppe1-analyze.py`, `w5-analyze.py`, `guarantees-analyze.py`, `ppe1-turn-attribution.py`).
- Per run the harness archives `transcript.jsonl` (every event), `agent-builds.jsonl` (each `dotnet build`, `dotnet test` or `calor` call by the agent, with output truncated at 4,000 characters: `harness-capture.py:123`, `:257-329`), `journal.jsonl`, `final-src/` and `result.json`.
- `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md:42` defines the `.g.cs` dead-end rate. The gates document defines no first-build pass rate (its metric list is `:35-43`), so this issue defines one.
- Ordinary Calor arms install a `.g.cs` write block (`run-pair.sh:899`, `:916-919`), and every ordinary arm gets a prompt rule against test files (`:1248`), which nothing enforces. Initialized-workspace arms run the product hook instead. It blocks new `.cs` files with "BLOCKED: Cannot create C# file" (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/HookCommand.cs:297-312`) and allows `.g.cs` writes (`:283-287`); the harness block is not installed there (`run-pair.sh:899`).
- The 50 translated pair files contain no `match`, `enum`, `using` statement, `null` or nullable type, and the C# references contain no `switch`, `using` statement or `null` (grep over `tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs` and `bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs/*/reference/csharp`). On the current pair set the null, missing-case and resource-leak classes will read "not measurable".
- The July epoch recorded zero escaped defects in 180 runs, one invalid slot aside (`epochs/e1a-attribution/VERDICT.md:62-64`, pre-0.30 syntax).
- Five call sites create a fix marked safe, all under `src/Calor.Compiler/`: `Parsing/Parser.cs:621`, `Parsing/Lexer.cs:398` and `:407` (conditionally), `Formatting/CalorFormatter.cs:441` and `:481`.
- `epochs/ws5-probe-001` holds `loop-telemetry/2` journals for a `calor` and a `csharp` arm and no `transcript.jsonl` (54 runs), which makes it a smoke input for the "not available" path.

#### Why

Later milestones build a new guarantee (null gaps, enum exhaustiveness, disposal) only for a defect class that agents are measured to produce, and choose which diagnostics get a fix by how often agents hit and repeat them. Those decisions need a base rate per class with a denominator, and a third outcome for a class the tasks cannot exhibit. Without it every gate reads zero by construction and the item is dropped on no evidence. The same script gives #1605 its descriptive columns and is run unchanged on the per-agent archives in 0.33 (#1811). It works on archives, so it does not hold the run. This issue may slip to 0.32 without holding the 0.31 release.

#### Done when

- The script and a test module exist under `bench/phase0-agent-native/`, and the tests run at `.github/workflows/test.yml:124`.
- The class table is committed. A test asserts that every code in it has exactly one class from the list in the first row of the table above, and that it covers every diagnostic code the fixture epoch contains except one left out on purpose.
- That code, present in the fixture and absent from the table, is reported as `unclassified` and listed by code; a test asserts the list.
- A synthetic fixture epoch committed under `bench/phase0-agent-native/tests/fixtures/` exercises every column, including: an iteration with `Calor0006`; a repeated code; a `.g.cs` dead end of each kind; a blocked test write; a class with zero opportunities (expected output "not measurable"); a failing hidden test with and without a class map; a run whose first edited invocation exits 0 and one whose first exits 1 (first-build pass rate 0.5).
- `python3 <script> bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/ws5-probe-001` exits 0 and prints "not available" for the fields that archive lacks.
- Given no thresholds file the script prints "threshold not registered" for every class outcome; given a fixture thresholds file it prints, for four fixture classes, "above threshold", "below threshold", "held" and "not measurable". A test asserts both.
- The fixture epoch has one form above and one below the fixture C#-habit threshold, and a test asserts both outcomes.
- Opportunity counts for the 25 translated pairs are printed, and are zero for null, missing case and resource leak, matching the baseline.
- `bench/phase0-agent-native/README.md` names the script, its inputs and each definition, with the definition of first-build pass rate under a heading that #1832 and #1835 can link.

#### Not in scope

- Thresholds, minimum opportunity counts and the reading of each outcome: #1770.
- Cost, token, turn and iteration ratios to the C# arm: defined in #1770 and reported by #1605 from `result.json`.
- The unknown-effect share: #1802.
- Tasks that can exhibit null, missing-case or resource-leak defects: #1789.

Part of epic #1761. Depends on #1764. Blocks #1811.

### #1763 · [0.31] Legacy-dialect allowlist and self-check

**Action:** new · **Labels:** tests · **Depends on:** #1648 · **Blocks:** #1803, #1839 · **Displaces:** #1741 · **May slip:** yes

#### Change

0.30 retired the `§` syntax with no rewriter, and 853 `.calr` files in that syntax remain in the tree on purpose. Record which they are and why, and stop new ones from appearing unnoticed. Most are benchmark material: pair fixtures (one task with a starting program per language), and the archived outputs of past benchmark collections (epochs).

1. **Allowlist.** `tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/inputs.json` already lists every original that has a current-syntax copy (`originalPaths`). Add, in that file under a new key or in a file beside it, a second list of `.calr` paths or directory prefixes that are kept as they are and have no copy, and a map from each directory prefix of `originalPaths` to the same fields (`originalPaths` itself stays a list of strings). Every entry carries three fields:
   - `dialect`: `pre-0.30` for the retired `§` syntax, or `0.30` for 0.30-syntax files that must stay frozen. No `0.30` entry is needed today. The value exists so that 0.30-syntax evidence, for example the archived run outputs of #1605, can be listed before the 0.32 rewrite (#1803) changes the current syntax again.
   - `class`: `frozen-evidence` (archived epoch output and pinned fixtures, never edited), `retained-original` (kept byte-identical behind a translated copy), `legacy-arm-input` (read by the pre-0.30 arm A of #1605) or `pending-1648`.
   - `reason`: one line.
2. **Classification table.** Commit the table from the baseline below, with current counts, where the allowlist lives or in `bench/phase0-agent-native/README.md`. The classes in the table are a proposal. The maintainer accepts or changes them by approving the pull request; that approval is part of the 0.31 tracker sign-off (#1759).
3. **Notes.** In each directory group of the table, add a short note (`LEGACY-DIALECT.md` or a README section): the files here are in the pre-0.30 syntax, are kept byte-identical, belong to the stated class, have their current copy at the stated path if one exists, and are not syntax examples.
4. **Self-check.** A check over tracked `.calr` files that fails when a file contains a retired marker and is on neither list; when a listed path no longer exists; and when a path listed with `dialect: pre-0.30` no longer contains a marker. A retired marker is `§` followed by a letter or `/`, the definition `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/SourceLiteralSyntaxGuardTests.cs:10-12` uses. Implement it as an xUnit test beside `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/ActiveSyntaxInputTests.cs`, or as a script in the `calor-first-guard` job of `.github/workflows/test.yml`; either runs on every pull request.

No retired file is translated, moved or edited by this issue.

Order.

- #1648 records the fate of the six `W-00x` pair directories. If it has done so when this lands, their 52 files carry the class it recorded; if not, they are listed as `pending-1648` and #1648 owns the follow-up edit. The check passes either way, so the dependency on #1648 is an ordering preference: an open #1648 holds neither this issue nor, through it, #1803.
- The check reads `.calr` files only, and no `.calr` file under `tests/E2E` holds a retired marker, so it does not wait for #1736.
- It must be merged before #1803. That change applies the 0.32 rewrite across the repository, skips every path on this allowlist (the rewriter is #1806), and begins by adding the 0.30-syntax evidence that must stay frozen to it.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

853 of 1,823 tracked `.calr` files contain a retired marker (`git ls-files '*.calr'`, then `grep -lE '§[A-Za-z/]'`). 746 of them are in `originalPaths`; 107 have no copy.

| Group | Files | Current copy | Proposed class |
|---|---|---|---|
| `bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/` (`e1-rows-parity-001` 40, `guarantees-probe-001` 90, `w5-parity-001` 40, `w5-parity-002` 40) | 210 | yes | frozen-evidence |
| `bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/` (`w-rows-dry-001` 36, `w-rows-dry-002` 18) | 54 | no | frozen-evidence |
| `bench/phase0-agent-native/latency/` | 106 | yes | retained-original |
| `bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs/`, the 25 two-arm pairs | 50 | yes | legacy-arm-input |
| `bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs/W-00*` | 52 | no | pending-1648 |
| `bench/phase0-agent-native/fixtures/d-s1.5/` | 24 | yes | retained-original |
| `bench/phase0-agent-native/templates/calor-arm/permissive-canary.calr` | 1 | no | legacy-arm-input |
| `tests/TestData/Benchmarks/` | 226 | yes | retained-original |
| `tests/TestData/EditScripts/` | 60 | yes | retained-original |
| `tests/Calor.Conversion.Tests/Snapshots/` | 48 | yes | retained-original |
| `docs/design/spikes/effect-rows/` | 15 | yes | frozen-evidence (pinned by `blobSha`, for example `bench/phase0-agent-native/effect-rows-probe-ledger.json:32-33`) |
| `benchmarks/` | 7 | yes | retained-original |

- `originalPaths` has 767 entries; the 21 that carry no retired marker are 10 under `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/SelfTest/` and 11 under `samples/`. The test helper reads the file by named property (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/ActiveSyntaxInputs.cs:21-30`, `:73`), so a new top-level key does not disturb it; `scripts/registered_ledger_historical_replay.py:469` also names the file.
- `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/ActiveSyntaxInputTests.cs:133` parses every registered copy with the current compiler. No test asserts the reverse: that a retired-syntax `.calr` file is registered or listed.
- None of the directory groups has a note that its `.calr` files are retired syntax. `tests/TestData/EditScripts/`, `benchmarks/` and `bench/phase0-agent-native/fixtures/d-s1.5/` have a README, without such a note; the other groups have none.
- No `.calr` file under `tests/E2E` contains a retired marker (58 files, 0 matches). The retired syntax #1736 removes there is in `.json`, `.md`, `.py` and `.sh` files, which this check does not read.
- `eng/test-manifest.json` pins the test count of each test project (`expectedTotal`; 22,559 for `Calor.Compiler.Tests` at `:8`), and CI fails on a different count.

#### Why

Agents that could read the checkout in July learned the syntax from whatever `.calr` files they found, and nearly half of those files now teach a dialect the compiler rejects. #1762 keeps benchmark agents out of the checkout, so #1605 does not wait for this issue. The files remain a hazard for people and agents working in the repository, and for the mechanical rewriter planned in 0.32 (#1806), which must skip them: 264 archived epoch outputs and 15 hash-pinned spike fixtures are evidence, and the originals behind translated copies are kept so that historical byte and hash checks keep reading them. One list with a reason per entry, and a check that fails on anything outside it, turns that from convention into something CI enforces. The `dialect` field keeps the list usable after 0.32, when 0.30-syntax evidence becomes legacy in its turn. This issue may slip to 0.32 without holding the 0.31 release, provided it lands before #1803.

#### Done when

- The self-check passes on `main`. Its own tests show it failing when a retired-syntax `.calr` file is added outside both lists, when a listed file is deleted, and when a file listed as `pre-0.30` no longer contains a marker; and passing when a marker-free file is listed with `dialect: 0.30`.
- The two lists together cover exactly the files the self-check finds: 746 and 107 at HEAD, with any difference explained in the pull request.
- Every entry and every directory prefix has a `dialect`, a `class` and a `reason`.
- The classification table is committed and the maintainer's approval of it is on the pull request.
- Every directory group in the table has the note.
- `git diff --diff-filter=MD --name-only <base> -- '*.calr'` prints nothing.
- The 52 `W-00x` files carry the class #1648 recorded. If #1648 is still open they are listed as `pending-1648`, and the pull request names #1648 as the owner of the follow-up edit.
- If the check is an xUnit test, `expectedTotal` for `Calor.Compiler.Tests` in `eng/test-manifest.json` is raised by the number of tests added.

#### Not in scope

- Wiring the translated pair copies into the harness (#1766) and the non-`.calr` files under `tests/E2E` (#1736, whose own done-when greps them).
- Retired syntax in Markdown and in `CHANGELOG.md`.
- The 220 tracked `.calr.inc` fragments of the paused PP-W research (`git ls-files '*.calr.inc'`; all hold retired markers). The check reads `.calr` files only. They are under `tasks/ppw-redesign` (52), `epochs/w-rows-pilot-001` (52), `epochs/w-rows-001` (52), `task-candidates/1256` (36) and `buildability/1255` (28), all in `bench/phase0-agent-native/`. #1648 classifies them in its closing record. Extending the check to `*.calr.inc` is a follow-up with no issue.
- Keeping benchmark agents out of the checkout: #1762.
- Listing files that do not exist yet. #1605 adds its arm-A outputs, which are pre-0.30 syntax, when it commits them; #1803 adds the 0.30-syntax evidence as its first step.
- Calor0006 guidance (#1788) and a retired-to-current rewriter (#1806 records that decision).

## Epic #1775 · [0.31] Epic: measure the 0.30 language once, including how the reference reaches the agent

New epic. Labels: epic.

#### Goal

Publish one agent measurement of the 0.30 language: the pre-0.30 compiler against the 0.30 compiler, each against a C# arm run in the same epoch on the same pinned model, under a registration fixed before any money is spent. A second stage measures how the syntax primer reaches the agent inside a `calor init` workspace. Alongside it, publish a ledger of what the project's agent results do and do not establish, and correct the README to match.

#### Why

- **Nothing has been measured with an agent on 0.30.** The release notes say so: "Diagnostic token budgets and before/after iterations-to-green are not measured yet" (`CHANGELOG.md:182`). The last measurement is from July 2026, on the pre-0.30 syntax with an unpinned model: 2.38 iterations to green against 1.00 for C#, pooled over two pairs at 30 runs per arm (`bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/e1a-attribution/VERDICT.md:13-17`).
- **The one lever with a measured effect ships behind a pointer nobody has tested.** In that epoch a syntax sheet of about 60 lines appended to the prompt removed 55% of the excess iterations (2.38 to 1.62) and cut the mean cost per run from $3.65 to $2.23 on one pair and from $4.20 to $1.17 on the other (summed from `total_cost_usd` in the archived `agent.json` files). In 0.30 the successor of that sheet, the primer (`src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/agent-syntax-exemplar.md`, 191 lines), is reachable only as the MCP resource `calor://primer`, and the file `calor init` generates tells the agent to read it. Whether agents do has never been measured.
- **#1605 as filed cannot be the exit gate.** It says it uses "no registration or freeze process", has no C# arm, requires "B is not worse than A" to close, tells the reader to revisit forms "before 0.30 ships", and is "Part of #1606", the 0.30 epic, which closed on 2026-11-22. The amended design is about 300 runs where the issue priced 180, and this program has recorded cost overruns of 2.8 times and about 4 times its estimates.
- **The measured arm would read guidance the compiler contradicts.** The teaching reference and the primer say `Contains(string)`, `Substring`, trimming, `Enumerable.Where` and `ToArray` are unknown to the effect checker and show hand-written loops; the effect manifests declare them pure, and the reference solution of one of the two penalised pairs calls `Contains(string)` from a pure function and `Substring` from one that declares only mutation and allocation.
- **The README claims what the results page disclaims.** `README.md:95` says Calor "shows measurable advantages"; `docs/benchmarking/results.md:20-23` says the same static outputs "do not establish a language, coding-agent productivity, correctness, or safety advantage".

Every sizing decision in 0.32 reads this measurement: which constructs to fix (#1645), which diagnostics get a fix (#1790), what `calor init` writes by default (#1823), the MCP budget (#1824) and which rejecting guarantees may land (#1800).

#### Work

| Issue | Item |
|---|---|
| #1770 | Decision record: register the evaluation (arms, pairs, n, pins, close rule, class thresholds, delivery rule, MCP rule) and authorise or decline the spend |
| #1605 | The evaluation itself, amended: concurrent C# arm, two stages, registered readings, report venue |
| #1773 | `calor init` options to inline the primer in the agent file and to write a starter file; default unchanged |
| #1771 | The primer through `calor_help` and through a CLI command, for clients and arms without MCP resources |
| #1772 | Correct the teaching statements about effects that the manifests contradict, and the workflow steps that name an option `calor_compile` does not have |
| #1745 | Execute the teaching-reference examples in CI, not only compile them (amended: a definition of "runnable" that is not empty) |
| #1774 | Evidence ledger, README correction, and a claims check that reads `README.md` |

#### Sequence

1. #1770, design half (arms, pairs, stages, thresholds). #1766, #1762 and #1767 in epic #1761 build only what it registers.
2. #1772, #1745 and #1771 merge before the 0.30 commit that #1605 pins for stage 1. Each changes what a measured arm reads or can reach, so none may land between the pin and the run. #1773 merges before the stage 2 pin.
3. #1770, spend half, before any paid run.
4. #1605 stage 1: zero-cost dry pass, collection, report. Its publication is the first 0.31 exit criterion.
5. #1605 stage 2 after #1767; it publishes, or is recorded on #1605 as not run, before #1823 is decided. It does not hold the release.
6. #1774 at any point. The #1605 rows are added to its ledger at the 0.31 close-out.
7. After the run, in 0.32, because each rewrites text the run measures: #1734, #1788, #1822.
