### #1770 · [0.31] Decision: register and authorise the 0.30 evaluation

**Action:** new · **Labels:** tests, p1 · **Blocks:** #1767, #1605, #1785 · **Displaces:** decision-only

#### Change

Record, before any paid run, what the 0.30 agent evaluation (#1605) runs, the most it may cost, and how each possible result will be read. These are maintainer decisions. This issue lists the options, their cost and the plan's recommendation; it does not take them. Filing or merging anything under this issue authorises no spend until the spend record carries a dated authorisation.

Terms. A *pair* is one task with a specification, a hidden test suite and a starting fixture per language. An *arm* is one configuration under test. An *epoch* is one complete collection in which all arms run interleaved on one pinned model; numbers are compared only within an epoch (`docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md:12`). The *primer* is the syntax sheet served as `calor://primer`. A *null pass* runs the harness with `--null-agent`, which applies the reference solution in place of an agent and costs nothing. The *penalised pairs* are the two where Calor cost extra iterations in July.

**What is produced**

1. **The design entry**: a new entry in §A.3 of `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md` (the next free `A-1.N`; `A-1.13` if nothing has been added since), with the `Annex version` pointer at `:102` updated. It holds decision 1 and items 1 to 13 below. No row is added to the §A.2 table: these are planning rules, not proof-point gates, and the entry body is what the guard freezes. CI keeps existing entries byte-identical, so every later change is a new entry. The pull request must not touch `scripts/check-annex-freeze.py` or `.github/workflows/experiment-registry-tamper-check.yml`; the guard refuses a pull request that changes the annex together with either.
2. **The thresholds file**: a machine-readable copy of items 5 to 11, proposed at `bench/phase0-agent-native/registrations/eval-0.30.json` (the directory exists and holds the paused PP-W registrations, which this issue does not touch). The design entry cites its SHA-256, and the report script (#1760) reads it. The entry is the registration; where the file differs from it, the entry governs.
3. **The spend record**, proposed at `docs/plans/0.31-evaluation-spend-authorisation.md` and modelled on `docs/plans/pp-w5-spend-authorisation.md`: decision 2. #1605 appends realised spend to it.

Three values do not exist when the design is signed: the hash of the 0.30 commit, the agent client version and the SHA-256 of the primer files. #1605 records them, under the rule in item 4, in one sub-entry per stage (`A-1.N.1`, `A-1.N.2`; the guard allows a sub-version under an existing parent) before the first paid run of that stage.

**Order.** The design entry and the thresholds file are merged first, so that epic #1761 builds only what is registered: #1767 and the arm-A parts of #1766 and #1762 wait for them and for nothing else. The spend record may follow later and exists before any paid run. This issue closes when all three exist.

**Decision 1: the design**

Projected cost uses the July per-run means (pre-0.30 syntax, unpinned model; see Baseline). The low figure assumes the primer helps as it did in July; the high figure assumes it does not. Stage 2 has no cost precedent: its fixtures have no starter and four of its arms run in `calor init` workspaces, three of them with the always-on MCP surface, so its range is the least reliable figure here.

| Option | Runs | Projected | What is lost |
|---|---|---|---|
| A. Stage 1 (arms A, B′, B and C# on five pairs) then stage 2 (four delivery arms and C# on two pairs), 10 runs per cell | 200 + 100 | $270 to $400, plus $150 to $325 | Nothing |
| B. Stage 1 only | 200 | $270 to $400 | The delivery rule and the MCP rule are never measured. #1767 is closed as not planned, #1823 records "not measured, default unchanged", #1824 is closed as not planned with this record linked |
| C. Reduced: arm B and C# on the five pairs | 100 | $105 to $150 | Also the before/after comparison: no arm A, no arm B′, and no regression against the old syntax can be filed. #1766 skips its arm-A part |
| D. No paid run | 0 | $0 | The first 0.31 exit criterion is met by its third branch: the spend record carries "Declined: no paid run authorised" and lists the rows that then read "not measured": #1645 steps 1, 4 and 5, #1790, #1788, the #1605 inputs of #1785 and #1800, #1823 and #1824 (their not-run branches), Part B of #1816, part 2 of #1802, and the cost recomputation in #1816 and #1832. The same list is entered in the plan of record (`docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39.md`) |

The plan recommends A at 10 runs per cell. Reasons: the before/after question is the one #1645 and the 0.32 breaking window are sized from, and stage 2 tests the only lever with a measured effect. Fifteen runs per cell in stage 1 would project to $400 to $600. Thirty runs per variant in stage 2, the July design, would project to $440 to $975; with stage 1 that is $710 to $1,375, which leaves no room under the $1,500 ceiling for an overrun, and this program has recorded two (2.8 times and about 4 times the estimate, see Baseline).

**Contents of the registration.** Each value below is the plan's recommendation; the maintainer confirms or replaces it before signing, and the entry states the final value. Items 6 to 11 are planning judgements with no measurement behind them and are to be labelled as such.

1. **Arms.** Stage 1: A is the pinned pre-0.30 compiler through `--arm-repo-root` and `--calor-dll` from the same checkout (the first does not bind the CLI the journal uses, see Baseline), with that release's primer through `--exemplar` and the original starters; B′ is the 0.30 compiler with that primer mechanically translated and the translated starters; B is the 0.30 compiler with the shipped primer and the same starters; C# has no primer. Stage 2, in `calor init` workspaces on the 0.30 compiler: pointer (as shipped), primer inlined in the agent file, inlined plus starter file, and CLI only (inlined, no MCP server); plus C#.
2. **Pairs.** Stage 1: `N1-003-csv-row` and `W3-003-kv-journal` (penalised), `W1-002-usage-billing`, `W2-003-window-stats` and `W2-004-interval-clip` (at parity in July). Stage 2: the starter-less versions of the two penalised pairs (#1767).
3. **n.** 10 valid runs per arm per pair in both stages. An invalid run is re-run at most twice per slot (`agent-native-gates.md:13`).
4. **Platform and pins.** The design entry names the operating system and the read sandbox the paid runs use, because #1762 builds for it. The plan recommends macOS with `sandbox-exec`, the only sandbox precedent in the tree; choosing Linux adds a mechanism to #1762. All arms of an epoch run on one machine. It names one full versioned model id, passed through `CLAUDE_MODEL` (never `default`), and the tag and commit of the last release before the rewrite, for arm A. It states the rule for the 0.30 commit: it contains #1766, #1762, #1764, #1772, #1745 and #1771, and for stage 2 also #1767 and #1773. Stage 1 arms load no template and no MCP server and read only the primer, so the maintainer may instead require #1771 and part 2 of #1745 only in the stage 2 commit; the entry says which. The commit hash, the client version and the SHA-256 of the three primer files go in the sub-entry #1605 writes.
5. **Measures.** Per pair and arm: mean iterations-to-green (never-green runs count 11, `agent-native-gates.md:39`), mean `total_cost_usd`, input tokens (uncached, cache-write, cache-read), output tokens, `num_turns`. Each as a ratio to the C# arm; pooled as the median of per-pair ratios (`:40`) over the two penalised pairs and over all five. Intervals: 90% percentile bootstrap over runs within pair and arm, 10,000 resamples, seed recorded. In stage 2 a `calor check` that follows an edit counts as an iteration, and so does a call of the MCP tool `calor_compile` that follows an edit, which is how the generated agent file tells the agent to compile; #1767 journals both. Without them the Calor arms would be understated against C# (see Baseline). If the maintainer decides that MCP compiles are not iterations, the entry says so and they are reported beside the count. These are planning rules, not gates under §6.1 of that document: its test is a cluster bootstrap over pairs (`:86`), which two penalised pairs cannot support.
6. **Close rule**, arm B against C#, pooled over the two penalised pairs, thresholds 1.3 on iterations-to-green and 1.5 on cost. *Closed*: both upper bounds are below their thresholds; #1645 opens no green-field follow-up. *Open*: either lower bound is above its threshold; #1645 ranks the cost drivers from the failure-class table and green-field work beyond #1734 may be funded. *Not resolved*: anything else; reported with intervals, nothing new is funded. The #1645 re-run reads the rule again only if both penalised pairs are in its pair set (#1816 Part B); otherwise the reading stays not resolved and no later run in this plan re-reads it.
7. **Before/after readings**, for B against A, B′ against A and B against B′, per pair and pooled, on iterations-to-green and on cost. *Regression*: the interval's lower bound is above 1.0; filed against #1645, attributed to the language change when it shows in B′ against A and to the rewritten primer when it shows in B against B′. *Improvement*: the upper bound is below 1.0. *No difference resolved*: otherwise. All three are published and none holds the release.
8. **Defect-class base rates** for null, missing case and resource leak, on the 0.30 Calor arm (arm B here), counting runs with at least one failed iteration or escaped defect of the class. *Not measurable*: fewer than 5 static opportunities for the class across the pair set, or fewer than 30 valid runs on pairs that have one. *Above threshold*: such events in at least 5 distinct runs and at least 10% of those runs. *Below threshold*: at least 30 runs and events in at most 1. *Held*: anything between. The same rule is applied unchanged to the Calor arm of each later registered run whose pairs have opportunities (the #1645 re-run under #1816 Part B; #1811), within one epoch and never pooled across epochs. Not measurable and held both keep the dependent item (#1800, and #1869, #1871 and #1870 in epic #1872) waiting for the next measurement; neither is a drop. The minimum of 5 opportunities per class is also the floor #1789 authors its pairs to. On the 0.31 pair set all three classes will read not measurable (see Baseline), and the entry says so in advance.
9. **C#-habit threshold**, the count #1785 calls the wrong-form error count. For a Calor form with a different C# spelling (`var`, a header `=>`, `set`, `type:name`, `each`), an event is a failed iteration in arm B whose edit wrote the C# spelling in place of the form. The rubric that maps spellings and diagnostic codes to forms is committed with the entry. A respelling toward C# becomes admissible under #1785 when events occur in at least 20% of the valid arm-B runs on pairs whose reference uses the form, in at least 5 runs, on at least two pairs. Fewer than 20 such runs reads not measurable. This is not the compiler's own wrong-form class (short or mis-cased visibility and modifier words, `CHANGELOG.md:52-55`), which #1790 handles.
10. **Delivery rule** (stage 2), pointer against inlined. #1823 makes the inlined primer the default if the primer is read before the first `.calr` edit in fewer than 90% of pointer-arm runs, or if the pointer arm's mean cost exceeds the inlined arm's by at least 10% and by at least two standard errors of the difference. At 10 runs per cell two standard errors is 9% to 13% of the mean if spread is as in July with the primer and 27% to 29% without, so the read rate is the primary trigger. Otherwise the reading is "pointer sufficient at this n", with the smallest resolvable difference stated. A starter file becomes default only if inlined-plus-starter costs at least 10% and two standard errors less than inlined. A client that cannot read MCP resources (#1820) gets the inlined primer regardless.
11. **MCP rule** (stage 2), inlined with the MCP server against CLI only, on cost per run, with input tokens and iterations-to-green reported beside it. *Does not earn its cost*: the MCP arm costs at least 10% and two standard errors more, with no lower iterations-to-green; #1824 merges or lazy-loads tools. *Earns it*: at least 10% and two standard errors less; #1824 records the present ratchet as the budget. *Not resolved*: otherwise; #1824 records the ratchet and changes no tool. No later run in this plan compares an MCP arm with a CLI-only arm; a second reading needs its own line under Part C of #1832.
12. **Deviations and limits**, stated in the entry: `calor init` arms run without the harness `.g.cs` write block that `agent-native-gates.md:26` requires; the CLI-only arm reads an agent file whose rules name MCP tools it does not have; stage 1 arms load no template and no MCP server; five fixture pairs, one agent, one model; the three parity pairs were at the C# floor in July; nothing is compared across epochs.
13. **End-to-end leg.** Not authorised by the first signature: this registration covers the paid leg of #1736 only through a later entry under it. If wanted, that entry is signed after #1736 closes, stating the task subset, the same model pin, a ceiling no larger than the unspent remainder of the ceilings below, #1736's no-agent dry run as its dry pass, and its reading: a before/after pass rate, excluded from any advantage claim.

**Decision 2: spend**

The maintainer authorises or declines, and sets the final figures; the values below are the plan's recommendation. The run uses the one model pinned in item 4, and the reading of every outcome is fixed by items 6 to 11 before the first run.

- *Ceilings.* Stage 1 $800; stage 2 $600; $1,400 in total, under the $1,500 epoch ceiling. Option C: $300. Costs are read from each run's `agent.json`, never priced by hand.
- *Abort triggers*. `run-epoch.sh` has no spend stop, so the operator sums `total_cost_usd` over the finished runs and evaluates the triggers after every interleaved round; the record says so. (a) After the first two valid runs of every cell, the cost extrapolated to the registered n exceeds the stage ceiling: stop; the maintainer records a smaller n for all cells before any outcome is tabulated, or closes the stage as not run. (b) Realised spend reaches 90% of the ceiling with cells incomplete: stop; no top-up without a new dated entry. (c) An arm canary or isolation canary fails, a cell has more than two invalid slots after re-attempts, or arm A's `compilerHash` equals arm B's: stop and repair; money spent counts against the ceiling. (d) One run costs more than $20, about five times the highest 30-run cell mean in July ($4.20): pause and inspect.
- *Dry pass.* A null pass for every registered arm and pair, with the same options and isolation mode, committed as `<epoch>-null` before the first paid run, every run with `taskSuccess: true`.
- *Not authorised by this record:* the #1645 re-run (#1816), the end-to-end leg unless added, any repeat of a voided epoch beyond the ceiling.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- #1605 says "Use the existing harnesses, with no registration or freeze process" and estimates "2 pairs × 3 arms × n=30 ≈ $300–550".
- The precedent record is `docs/plans/pp-w5-spend-authorisation.md`: authorised before the epoch (`:3-4`), shape and pins (`:8-16`), projection (`:18`), null pre-flight (`:75-79`). It projected about $33 and realised $92.83, 2.8 times, because the first attempt was void: both arms had been built by the same compiler (`:84-99`). `bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/guarantees-probe-001/VERDICT.md:20-24` records $79.39 against an estimate under $20.
- `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md:81` sets an epoch API ceiling of $1,500. Annex A starts at `:100`; its newest entry is A-1.12 (`:349`). `scripts/check-annex-freeze.py` keeps every entry that starts `**A-1.` byte-identical to the base branch, requires a new entry for any change to the annex, and requires the version pointer to match; it runs as the `annex-freeze` job (`.github/workflows/experiment-registry-tamper-check.yml:98`) on every pull request to main.
- `epochs/e1a-attribution` (2026-07-16, 180 runs, `modelPin: "default"` in its `pins.json`) cost $370.52, summed from `total_cost_usd` in its 180 `agent.json` files. Mean cost per run: `N1-003-csv-row` Calor $3.65, Calor with exemplar $2.23, C# $0.60; `W3-003-kv-journal` $4.20, $1.17, $0.50. Standard deviation over mean of cost within a cell: 0.30 and 0.32 without the exemplar, 0.10 and 0.14 with it, 0.14 and 0.13 for C#.
- `epochs/hardness-check-001` (2026-07-13, three runs per cell, no exemplar, `modelPin: "default"`) is the only epoch that ran all three parity pairs and the only one with a C# arm on them: Calor $2.06, $1.24 and $1.37 per run; C# $0.54, $0.49 and $0.44 (means of `total_cost_usd`). Its `rollup.json` shows iterations-to-green of 2.67 for Calor on the two penalised pairs and 1.0 on the other three.
- The model pin is passed only when `CLAUDE_MODEL` is set and not `default` (`bench/phase0-agent-native/run-pair.sh:1321-1325`).
- The 50 translated pair files under `tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs` contain no `match`, `enum`, `using` or `null` (word grep, zero files).
- In `--initialized-workspace` mode the harness does not install its `.g.cs` write block (`run-pair.sh:899`), and the product hook allows `.g.cs` writes (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/HookCommand.cs:283-287`). In the same mode the `calor` wrapper on the agent's path execs the compiler directly (`run-pair.sh:982-985`), so a `calor` build or check is not journaled as an iteration, while `dotnet build` and `dotnet test` are. The generated agent file tells the agent to compile with the MCP tool `calor_compile` after each edit (`src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Templates/CLAUDE.md.template:13`); those calls are not journaled either.
- `--arm-repo-root` binds the arm template, `Calor.Tasks` and the canary build to another checkout (`run-pair.sh:267-281`). The CLI the journal uses is taken from the harness checkout unless `--calor-dll` names another (`run-pair.sh:434-455`), so arm A needs both options or its journal records come from the 0.30 CLI.
- The only read sandbox in the harness directory is macOS `sandbox-exec` (`bench/phase0-agent-native/ppw-gateway-client.py:239-240`, `:314`), part of the paused PP-W gateway.
- `bench/phase0-agent-native/run-epoch.sh` has no spend stop; its header only says to get authorisation before a live epoch (`:10-11`). `--max-budget-usd` is passed to the client only on the paused PP-W ticket path (`run-pair.sh:1368-1376`).
- No per-task cost is recorded for the end-to-end suite (grep for cost fields under `tests/E2E/agent-tasks/benchmark-results`, no match).

#### Why

The 0.31 exit gate is a paid run that has grown from the three arms on two pairs #1605 priced to nine arms over two stages, in a program whose realised spend has twice exceeded its estimate (2.8 times and about 4 times), and #1605 as filed asks nobody to authorise it. Several later decisions read thresholds from this run that are written nowhere: the close rule for green-field work (#1645), the base rates that gate new guarantees (#1800, epic #1872), the respelling threshold (#1785), the delivery rule (#1823) and the MCP rule (#1824). Three of them fail if left to be written later: a 10% cost trigger cannot be resolved at ten runs with the spread measured in July, the MCP comparison has no margin, and a base rate reads zero on tasks that cannot exhibit the defect unless "not measurable" is a separate outcome. A threshold written after the report has been read is not a registration. This issue is a decision record and takes no engineering capacity.

#### Done when

- The design entry is merged with the `annex-freeze` job green. It states the option chosen in decision 1 with its date, keeps the item numbers above (#1823 and #1824 cite the delivery rule as item 10 and the MCP rule as item 11), gives a value for each of items 1 to 13 marked "as recommended" or "changed to", and states that it was written before any paid run of the evaluation.
- The thresholds file exists at the path the design entry names, and its SHA-256 matches the one the entry cites.
- The spend record exists and carries either "Authorised: <date> by the maintainer" with a ceiling per stage and the abort triggers, or "Declined: <date>" with the option taken instead (for option D, "Declined: no paid run authorised"), and lists what it does not authorise.
- No live epoch of the evaluation exists whose `pins.json` has a `startedAt` earlier than the merge of the design entry or the date on the spend record.
- If option B, C or D was chosen: #1767 is closed as not planned with a link to the record; for C or D the arm-A part of #1766 is marked skipped; for D the spend record lists the rows that read "not measured", the plan of record carries the same list, and #1605 is closed on that record.

#### Not in scope

- Running anything: #1605. Building the arms, the isolation or the delivery variants: epic #1761.
- The 0.32 and 0.33 registrations: #1816 and #1832, which append to the same annex.
- The token cap per respelling and the tokenizer set: #1785.

Part of epic #1775. Blocks #1767 and #1605.

### #1605 · [0.31] Before/after agent evaluation of the simplified language

**Action:** amend · **Labels:** tests, p1 · **Depends on:** #1766, #1762, #1764, #1770, #1772, #1745, #1771 · **Blocks:** #1645, #1790, #1788, #1800, #1816, #1823, #1824, #1651, #1832, #1642

**Current**

The issue asks for a before/after agent evaluation of the 0.30 syntax with the pair harness: three Calor arms (A: old compiler and primer; B′: new compiler with the primer mechanically translated; B: new compiler with the new primer), an end-to-end leg, and one small arm inside a `calor init` workspace, "with no registration or freeze process". It is done when results are published, B is not worse than A, and any regressed form is revisited "before 0.30 ships". C# appears only as a report line, and the body ends "Part of #1606".

**Amendment**

Retitle to `[0.31] Before/after agent evaluation of the simplified language`. Replace label `p2` with `p1`. Keep arms A, B′ and B. Make the six replacements below; leave the rest of the body as it is.

1. In `## Goal`, replace the last sentence ("Use the existing harnesses, with no registration or freeze process.") with:

```markdown
Use the existing pair harness. Arms, pairs, n, the model and commit pins, the spend ceiling, the abort triggers and every threshold are fixed in the registration (#1770) before the first paid run. Filing this issue authorises no spend.
```

2. Under `## What the existing harnesses do and don't cover`, in the `run-pair.sh` bullet, replace the sentence "It doesn't load a CLAUDE.md, template or primer, so it doesn't exercise the `calor init` path users run." with:

```markdown
In its ordinary mode it loads no CLAUDE.md, template or primer. Its `--initialized-workspace` mode runs the real `calor init` and always registers the generated MCP server. The agent file `init` writes points at `calor://primer` and does not contain it; the other delivery variants of stage 2 need #1773 and #1767.
```

3. In the same section, replace the last two sentences of the `tests/E2E/agent-tasks` bullet ("Its `verify.sh` scripts grep for specific forms ... The runner also skips `calor init`.") with:

```markdown
At bc80daeb all 129 `tasks/*/*/verify.sh` scripts delegate to the compiler-backed `tests/E2E/agent-tasks/lib/verify-logic.py` and none matches a retired marker. What still names the retired syntax is 16 `task.json` prompts under `tasks/refactoring-benchmark`, six fixture `CLAUDE.md` files and the counting functions in `lib/helpers.sh`; #1736 migrates them. The runner skips `calor init` (`tests/E2E/agent-tasks/lib/helpers.sh:281`).
```

4. Replace the whole `## Design` section with:

```markdown
## Design

This issue runs what the registration (#1770, an entry in Annex A of `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md`) registers and nothing else. If that entry records the reduced design, read "arm B and the C# arm" for stage 1 and skip stage 2.

1. **One epoch per stage, one pinned model.** All arms of a stage run interleaved in one epoch on one machine, with the same model id (`CLAUDE_MODEL`, never `default`) and agent client version. Numbers are compared within an epoch only (`docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md:12`); nothing is compared with the July figures. Each stage is run through the arm list of `run-epoch.sh` (#1766).
2. **Stage 1, the before/after question.** Four arms on the five green-field pairs (`N1-003-csv-row`, `W3-003-kv-journal`, `W1-002-usage-billing`, `W2-003-window-stats`, `W2-004-interval-clip`):
   - **A:** the pinned pre-0.30 compiler through `--arm-repo-root` and `--calor-dll` from the same checkout, that release's primer through `--exemplar`, the original starters.
   - **B′:** the 0.30 compiler, the same primer mechanically translated to 0.30 syntax, starters translated one for one.
   - **B:** the 0.30 compiler, the shipped primer, the same translated starters.
   - **C#:** the C# starters, in the same epoch.

   A against B′ isolates the language change; B′ against B isolates the rewritten primer.
3. **Stage 2, how the primer reaches the agent.** In `calor init` workspaces (`--initialized-workspace`) on the starter-less versions of the two pairs that cost extra iterations in July: pointer (as shipped), primer inlined in the agent file, inlined plus a starter file, and CLI only (primer inlined, no MCP server), with a C# arm in the same epoch. In this stage a `calor check` after an edit counts as an iteration, and an MCP `calor_compile` call after an edit is counted as the registration states (#1767 journals both). The delivery rule and the MCP rule of the registration are applied to the result; #1823 and #1824 act on it.
4. **End-to-end leg.** `tests/E2E/agent-tasks`, after #1736, and only if the registration has a signed end-to-end part. Its results are excluded from any advantage claim.
5. **Dry pass first.** Before the first paid run of a stage, a `--null-agent` epoch (the reference solution applied in place of the agent, at no API cost) passes for every arm and pair of that stage under the same options and isolation mode, and is committed as `<epoch>-null`.
6. **Spend.** Collection stops at the ceiling and on the abort triggers written in the spend record of the registration. `run-epoch.sh` has no spend stop, so the operator sums `total_cost_usd` over the finished runs after every interleaved round and the spend record says so. Nothing is topped up without a new dated entry there.
7. **Report.** Per pair and arm: valid, invalid and censored runs; mean iterations and iterations-to-green; `total_cost_usd`; input tokens (uncached, cache-write and cache-read separately); output tokens; turns. Each also as a ratio to the C# arm of the same epoch. B against A, B′ against A and B against B′ with the registered intervals and readings, and the reading of the close rule. The columns of the report script (#1760), which include the class readings of registration item 8 and the C#-habit counts of item 9. If the script has not landed when stage 1 is published, the report says "pending #1760" for those columns and an addendum is committed beside it when the script lands; #1785 and #1800 read the addendum. A defect class the pair set cannot exhibit is reported "not measurable", never as zero. Teaching-token counts from `TokenBudgetTests`. Commit, model, client version, platform and command lines. Realised spend against the ceiling.
8. **Venue.** The report is committed with the epoch directory and summarised in the 0.31 release notes. Its row in the evidence ledger (#1774) is added at the 0.31 close-out under the checklist in the plan of record; stage 1 counts as published when the report and the release-notes section are merged. `docs/benchmarking/results.md` is generated from `website/public/data/benchmark-results.json` and is not edited. Epoch directories are committed under `bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/`; arm A's archived `.calr` outputs are pre-0.30 syntax and go on the legacy-dialect allowlist (#1763) if that check has merged.

Not in this run: modify-a-converted-module tasks (authored in #1789 and measured in the #1645 re-run) and any Rust arm (#1831).
```

5. Replace the three bullets of `## Done when` with:

```markdown
- **Stage 1 is published** at the venue above with a C# arm run in the same epoch on the same pinned model, or the reduced design is published if the registration records it, or, if the spend record of #1770 carries "Declined: no paid run authorised", this issue records "not run" with a link to that record and closes. This is the 0.31 exit criterion; the bullets below do not hold the release.
- The B-versus-A and Calor-versus-C# results are reported with the readings registered in #1770, whatever they are. Every regression, as that entry defines one, is filed against #1645 with the pair, the arms compared, the numbers and the most frequent diagnostic codes in the failed iterations.
- **Stage 2 is published**, or this issue records "stage 2 not run" with a link to the #1770 record. One or the other happens before #1823 is decided.
- **The end-to-end leg is published**, or this issue records "end-to-end leg not run" with the reason.
- For every epoch: a sub-entry under the registration entry (`A-1.N.1` for stage 1, `A-1.N.2` for stage 2) records the 0.30 commit, the agent client version and the SHA-256 of the primer files, and `git merge-base --is-ancestor` shows that the commit contains the changes the registration requires; the sub-entry is merged before the earliest `startedAt`; the `-null` companion and the live epoch directory are committed with `pins.json`; realised spend is appended to the spend record.
- The issue closes when each of the three legs is published or recorded as not run (all three under a declined spend record). #1645, #1790, #1788, #1800, #1651 and #1832 wait only for the stage 1 report; #1823 and #1824 wait for the stage 2 report or its not-run record.
```

6. Replace the last line ("Part of #1606.") with:

```markdown
Part of epic #1775. Blocked by #1770 (the registration) and by #1766, #1762, #1764, #1772, #1745 and #1771, which must be in the pinned 0.30 commit as the registration's pin rule states. Stage 2 is also blocked by #1767 and #1773; the end-to-end leg by #1736.
```

**Why**

Every arm of the issue as filed is Calor, so it cannot say what the 0.30 language costs against C#, and the July cost figures describe a syntax that no longer exists. As the exit gate of 0.31 the body also contradicts itself: it disclaims registration although its thresholds must be fixed before the data, its done-when stays open on an unfavourable result, its deadline has passed, its parent epic is closed, and it names no blocker although the 0.30 arm, the arm isolation, the telemetry fields and the registration must exist before the first paid run. Two sentences about the harnesses are stale at bc80daeb: all 129 `verify.sh` scripts delegate to `lib/verify-logic.py` and none greps for a marker, and `run-pair.sh` has an `--initialized-workspace` mode (`bench/phase0-agent-native/run-pair.sh:223`).

### #1773 · [0.31] calor init options for primer delivery (pointer or inline) and an optional starter file; default unchanged

**Action:** new · **Labels:** enhancement · **Blocks:** #1767, #1823, #1650, #1835 · **Displaces:** #1742

#### Change

Give `calor init` two options, so that stage 2 of the 0.30 evaluation (#1605) can compare how the syntax primer reaches the agent. The *primer* is the text served as the MCP resource `calor://primer`. With neither option, every file `calor init` writes is byte-identical to today.

Stage 2 is the first consumer. #1823 is the second: a client that cannot read MCP resources gets the inlined primer whatever stage 2 shows, so this issue is needed even if the registration (#1770) records no stage 2.

1. **Primer delivery.** An option with two values, proposed as `--primer pointer|inline` (the final spelling is set in this issue's pull request; #1767 uses what ships). `pointer` is the default and is today's output. With `inline`, the Calor section of the generated agent file contains the primer text verbatim, as `McpMessageHandler.GetPrimerContentPublic()` returns it, inside the existing `BEGIN CalorC SECTION` / `END CalorC SECTION` markers, and Rule 0 points at the inlined text instead of telling the agent to read `calor://primer`. Compose the text when `init` runs. Do not change the four embedded template files: they are generated, and `calor self-check docs` fails when one drifts from its source.
2. **Starter file.** An option, proposed as `--starter`, that writes one `.calr` file into the project `init` configures. Its content is one complete program from `programs[]` in the teaching source (`src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Teaching/reference.json`), so it is already compiled in CI; the pull request names the program id. It never overwrites an existing file without `--force`. In solution mode the pull request states which project receives the file, and a test pins that choice.
3. **All four agents.** `claude`, `codex`, `gemini` and `github` accept both options. Their templates are identical today and stay so.
4. **Switching back.** Running `init --force` with `pointer` on a workspace initialized with `inline` restores the pointer section byte for byte.
5. **Token statement.** The pull request states, for each combination, the size of the generated Calor section in o200k tokens (the `ProxyTokenizer` in `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/TokenBudgetTests.cs`), and what was added and removed against the pointer form. No budget constant in that file changes; changing the default or a budget is #1823.
6. **Docs.** The options table in `docs/cli/init.md:106-113` and `website/content/cli/init.mdx`.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- `calor init` has four options: `--ai`, `--project`, `--solution`, `--force` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/InitCommand.cs:16-41`). Supported agents are `claude`, `codex`, `gemini`, `github` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Init/AiInitializerFactory.cs:11`).
- The four templates under `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Templates/` (`CLAUDE.md.template`, `AGENTS.md.template`, `GEMINI.md.template`, `copilot-instructions.md.template`) are byte-identical (same MD5). Line 9 reads "Rule 0: Read `calor://primer` before writing Calor; use `calor_help` for other constructs."
- They are generated from the `agentInstructions` field of `reference.json` (`src/Calor.Compiler/SelfCheck/ExemplarCompileChecker.cs:158-162`), and `calor self-check docs` fails when a template drifts from it.
- Each initializer reads its template and substitutes `{{VERSION}}`, nothing else (`src/Calor.Compiler/Init/ClaudeInitializer.cs:66-68`, `CodexInitializer.cs:34`, `GeminiInitializer.cs:31`, `GitHubCopilotInitializer.cs:37`).
- `init` writes no `.calr` file; its closing message tells the user to create one (`InitCommand.cs:333`, `:533`).
- The primer is the embedded `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/agent-syntax-exemplar.md` (191 lines). `McpMessageHandler.GetPrimerContent()` returns it with the authoring-only `<!-- drift:ignore -->` lines removed (`src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/McpMessageHandler.cs:512-533`); that method is private, and `GetPrimerContentPublic()` (`:535`, internal, visible to `Calor.Compiler.Tests`) wraps it.
- Budgets, in o200k tokens, with the measurements the test file records for 2026-11-20 (`TokenBudgetTests.cs:41-54`; not re-measured here): each template plus the MCP server instructions measures 317 + 275 = 592 against a budget of 600; the primer measures 1,569 in a band of 800 to 1,600. `InitTemplatePlusServerInstructions_FitsBudget` (`:84-103`) reads the embedded templates, so text composed at `init` time does not enter it. An inlined section is about 317 + 1,569 tokens (a sum, not measured).
- The harness mode that will use these options runs the real `init` and by design never rewrites a generated file (`bench/phase0-agent-native/harness-capture.py:564-567`).

#### Why

A syntax sheet in the prompt removed 55% of the excess iterations of writing new Calor in July (`bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/e1a-attribution/VERDICT.md:13-28`, pre-0.30 syntax). The product delivers the successor of that sheet as a pointer, and whether agents follow the pointer has never been measured. The harness mode for `calor init` workspaces runs the real `init` and does not rewrite what it generates, so the inlined and starter variants have to exist as product behaviour before stage 2 can compare them. They ship as options with the default unchanged, because choosing the default is the decision the measurement informs. This issue takes the 0.31 capacity of #1742, which moves to 0.34.

#### Done when

- Tests in `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/InitCommandTests.cs`, for each of the four agents:
  - with no new option, the generated agent file equals the embedded template with the version substituted;
  - with the inline option, the section contains `GetPrimerContentPublic()` exactly once, between the markers, and does not contain "Read `calor://primer`";
  - `init --force` with `pointer` after `inline` yields the default file.
- A test that the starter file compiles without errors through `Program.Compile` with default `CompilationOptions`, that an existing file of the same name is left untouched without `--force`, and that in solution mode the file lands in the project the pull request names.
- A test pins the token count of the inlined section at no more than the template count plus the primer count plus 50.
- `TokenBudgetTests` passes with no constant changed, and `calor self-check docs` reports no drift.
- `eng/test-manifest.json` has `expectedTotal` for `Calor.Compiler.Tests` raised by the number of tests added.
- The pull request carries the token statement, and both documentation pages list the options.

#### Not in scope

- Changing the default, removing rules duplicated between the template and the MCP instructions, or any budget constant: #1823.
- Harness selection of a variant and the starter-less fixtures: #1767.
- An agent file for clients with no MCP server at all. The CLI-only arm of stage 2 uses the inlined file as generated; #1770 records that its rules name MCP tools.

Part of epic #1775. Blocks #1767.

### #1771 · [0.31] Serve the primer outside MCP resources: a calor_help action and a CLI command

**Action:** new · **Labels:** enhancement · **Blocks:** #1767, #1605 · **Displaces:** #1748

#### Change

Make the syntax primer reachable by an agent that cannot read MCP resources, and by one that has no MCP server. The primer is the text served today as `calor://primer`.

1. **`calor_help` action.** Add `primer` to the `action` enum of the `calor_help` tool (`src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/Tools/HelpTool.cs:48-53`), to the dispatch in `ExecuteAsync` and to the list of valid actions in its error message (`:89-97`). It takes no other argument and returns the primer text.
2. **CLI command.** Add a command that prints the same text to standard output and exits 0, proposed as `calor primer`, registered with the other subcommands in `src/Calor.Compiler/Program.cs:310-338`. It needs no project, no configuration and no network. The spelling is set in this issue's pull request; #1767 uses what ships.
3. **One source.** The resource, the action and the command all return the text of `McpMessageHandler.GetPrimerContent()`. That method is private; call the internal `GetPrimerContentPublic()` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/McpMessageHandler.cs:535`), as `McpResourceValidator.GetPrimer()` does. Do not read the embedded file a second way.
4. **Token statement.** The `tools/list` result grows by the new enum value and the words that describe it. State the total before and after, as `AlwaysOnMcpSurface_FitsRatchet` prints it. The total must stay at or under 8,000 with `McpSurfaceRatchet` unchanged; if it does not fit, shorten another description in the same pull request.
5. **Docs.** The `calor_help` row in `docs/cli/mcp.md:43` and `website/content/cli/mcp.mdx`, and an entry for the command under `docs/cli/` and `website/content/cli/`.

The templates and the MCP server instructions do not change in this issue: together they are at 592 of 600 tokens.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- The primer is registered only as an MCP resource (`src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/McpMessageHandler.cs:124-130`) and served by `LoadResourceContent` (`:465-470`) from `GetPrimerContent()` (`:512-533`).
- `calor_help` has four actions, `syntax`, `features`, `error` and `example` (`HelpTool.cs:50`, `:89-97`). None returns the primer; `query=overview` and `query=reference` return other texts (`:112-113`).
- No CLI command prints it: the root command registers 29 subcommands (`src/Calor.Compiler/Program.cs:310-338`) and no file under `src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/` contains the word "primer".
- The always-on MCP surface measured 7,900 o200k tokens for 19 tools on 2026-11-20 (tools/list 7,625 plus instructions 275; the figure the test file records, not re-measured here) against a ratchet of 8,000 (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/TokenBudgetTests.cs:65-71`); the test's failure message says "Merge or shorten tools rather than raising the ratchet" (`:141`). One tool description may not exceed 200 tokens (`:78`).
- The harness mode for `calor init` workspaces always registers the MCP server and reads `calor://primer` over MCP as its own pre-run check (`bench/phase0-agent-native/harness-capture.py:634-658`).
- A manual Codex session on 2026-07-22 recorded that Codex read `calor://primer` (`bench/phase0-agent-native/CODEX-SMOKE.md`). Resource support in the other clients is unverified; #1820 checks it.

#### Why

The primer is the one piece of teaching text with a measured effect, and today only an MCP resource read reaches it. Stage 2 of the 0.30 evaluation (#1605) has an arm with no MCP server, which measures whether the always-on tool surface earns its cost per turn; as recommended in #1770 that arm reads the primer inlined in its agent file, and the command is its only other route to the text. A client that has the server but cannot read resources (#1820 checks which) has no route at all until `calor_help` serves it. The change alters what a measured arm can reach, so it lands before the 0.30 commit #1605 pins and not between the pin and the run. The tool surface is 100 tokens under its ratchet, so the addition has to be counted. This issue takes the 0.31 capacity of #1748, which moves to 0.33.

#### Done when

- A test under `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/Mcp/` asserts that the `calor://primer` resource text, the result of `calor_help` with `action=primer`, and the standard output of the CLI command are equal, allowing one trailing newline.
- The CLI command exits 0 when run in an empty directory.
- `AlwaysOnMcpSurface_FitsRatchet` and `EveryToolDescription_FitsPerToolCeiling` pass with no constant changed, and the pull request states the token total before and after.
- `eng/test-manifest.json` has `expectedTotal` for `Calor.Compiler.Tests` raised by the number of tests added.
- `calor self-check docs` reports no drift, and the documentation pages list the action and the command.
- The merge is an ancestor of the 0.30 commit that #1605 records for the first stage whose pin rule names this issue (stage 1 as #1770 recommends; the sub-entry under its annex entry); `git merge-base --is-ancestor` exits 0.

#### Not in scope

- Text in the `calor init` templates or the server instructions that mentions the command.
- The harness switch that runs without an MCP server and the primer-read log: #1767.
- Checking which clients can read MCP resources: #1820.

Part of epic #1775. Blocks #1767 and #1605.

### #1772 · [0.31] Correct the "remains unknown" statements in the teaching reference against the effect manifests

**Action:** new · **Labels:** documentation · **Blocks:** #1605, #1798 · **Displaces:** text-only

#### Change

Correct the agent-facing teaching text where it contradicts the compiler. All of it has one source, `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Teaching/reference.json`; `calor self-check docs --fix` regenerates the mirrors (the full reference, the primer, `docs/syntax-reference/current-language.md`, the website page, the lookup JSON and the workflow catalog). Edit the source, regenerate, change no example program and no effect manifest.

**A. Statements about the effects of .NET members.** For each row, write one probe: a complete module whose single function declares the empty effect row and calls the member (for the interpolation row, it interpolates an `int`, a `long` and a `double`). Compile it twice: with default `CompilationOptions`, and with `StrictEffects = true`, the setting the teaching tests use (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/ExemplarCompilesTests.cs:253-260`). For example:

```calor
module EffectProbe
  func Has (string:s, string:sub) → bool
    !
    return s.Contains(sub)
```

If both compiles finish with no error and no `Calor0411`, `Calor0419` or `Calor0425` diagnostic, the member is not unknown and the statement is rewritten to say so. If either reports `Calor0410` for `unknown` or one of those three codes, the statement stays, reworded to name the setting under which it holds. Commit the probes as tests so that #1798 can start from them.

The fields below are in `constructs[]` (by construct `id`) and in `primer`.

| Field (line in `reference.json`) | Statement | What the manifest says |
|---|---|---|
| `linq_query.description` (`:655`) | `Enumerable.Where` and `Enumerable.ToArray` "currently remain unknown under strict checking"; "its row is alloc,mut where LINQ's is alloc" | Both listed with no effects (`src/Calor.Compiler/Manifests/bcl-system.calor-effects.json:464`, `:506`) |
| `string_interpolation.description` (`:673`) | "neither ${value} nor a format clause supplies an authoritative effect contract" | `Int32`, `Int64` and `Double` `ToString` are pinned pure (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/StringInterpolationTests.cs:270-290`) |
| `string_operations.calorSyntax` (`:1374`) | `string.Concat(a, b)` and `text.IndexOf(value, StringComparison.Ordinal)`: "strict effect contract unavailable" | `System.String` methods are pure through a wildcard (`bcl-system.calor-effects.json:43-47`) |
| `string_operations.description` (`:1375`) | `Contains(string)`, `Substring` and trimming "remain unknown under strict checking" | Same wildcard |
| `primer.introduction` (`:2432`; rendered at `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/agent-syntax-exemplar.md:62-63`) | "Contains(string), Substring, trimming and comparison-mode searches are unknown under strict checking; scan ordinally into a buffer as FirstField does" | Same wildcard |

Where a pure contract is one #1731 lists as unsound (LINQ operators, culture-sensitive formatting), say what the compiler does today and no more. The hand-written examples (`StringContains`, `FirstField`, `Positive`) stay as programs; their descriptions stop presenting them as the only permitted route.

**B. The workflow catalog** (`catalogs` → `workflows.json`, `reference.json:2689-2789`, served as `calor://workflows`).

- Four steps pass `includeEffectSummary` to `calor_compile` (`:2697`, `:2732`, `:2763`, `:2782`) and one tells the agent to read `effectSummary.missing` (`:2768`). The tool defines neither. Remove them; where a step needs the missing effects, point it at the `Calor0410` diagnostics of an ordinary compile, which name them.
- Two steps say effect errors are fixed automatically (`:2710` "Compile and auto-fix syntax/effect errors", `:2722` "Auto-fix all high-confidence errors (parser, effects)"). Only fixes marked safe are applied, and none of those is an effect fix. Say so.
- Every `autoFix` argument is written at the top level of `args` (`:2708`, `:2720`, `:2751`, `:2781`); the tool reads it inside `options`. Move it.
- The `calor_convert` step passes `includeEffects` (`:2744`), which that tool does not define. Remove it.

**C. The `calor_compile` tool description** (`src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/Tools/CompileTool.cs:21-22`) says "Auto-fixes parser, ID, and effect errors by default", which its own option text contradicts (`:85-88`). Correct it. It is part of the always-on MCP surface, so state the token count before and after.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- The generated copies carry the same statements: `docs/syntax-reference/current-language.md:580` and `:1153`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Teaching/Generated/current-language-reference.md:572` and `:1145`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/calor-syntax-documentation.json:887` and `:1830`, `website/content/syntax-reference/current-language.mdx:577` and `:1150`. No other line of `reference.json` carries one (grep for "unknown under strict", "contract unavailable", "contracts are unavailable" and "authoritative effect contract").
- `tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs/N1-003-csv-row/reference/calor/CsvRow.calr` calls `Contains(",")` at line 5 and `Replace` at line 12 inside functions whose row is `!`, and `Substring` at lines 34 and 37 under `! mut, alloc`. `PairProgram_CompilesWithoutErrors` (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/BenchCorpusCompilesTests.cs:59-71`) compiles it with default options and asserts no errors. Under those options an undeclared `unknown` charge is a `Calor0410` error (`src/Calor.Compiler/Effects/EffectEnforcementPass.cs:1053-1056`; `CHANGELOG.md:69`). So under default options those members are not charged `unknown` at HEAD. This was established by reading; no .NET SDK was available to run a probe. `StrictEffects` raises `Calor0411`, `Calor0419` and `Calor0425` from warnings to errors (`src/Calor.Compiler/Program.cs:1392-1395`, `EffectEnforcementPass.cs:1137-1139`, `:1989`); whether any of the three is reported for these members was not verified. `N1-003-csv-row` is one of the two penalised pairs of #1605.
- Probable cause (the commit history was not available to verify it): #1731 records under "History" that these contracts were withdrawn during 0.30 and restored on 2026-11-14, and the teaching text still describes the withdrawn state.
- The examples the text points to are pinned by tests and must keep passing unchanged: `TeachingStringContains_PreservesOrdinalSubstringBehavior`, `TeachingFirstField_PreservesOrdinalPrefixBehavior`, `TeachingArrayFilter_PreservesValuesOrderDuplicatesAndInput`, and the `complete_linq` assertions (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/ExemplarCompilesTests.cs:115-127`, `:159-245`).
- `calor_compile` has no `includeEffectSummary` property (`CompileTool.cs:29-130`); the string occurs in no `.cs` file under `src/`. `includeEffects` is an option of `calor_structure` only (`src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/Tools/StructureTool.cs:70`). The catalog is mirrored at `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Teaching/Generated/workflows.json`. `autoFix` is a member of `options` (`CompileTool.cs:85-89`) and is read from there (`:189`). Automatic repair applies only fixes with `IsSafeToApply` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Diagnostics/Diagnostic.cs:1633`), which is set at five sites, all in the lexer, parser and formatter (`Parsing/Parser.cs:621`, `Parsing/Lexer.cs:398`, `:407`, `Formatting/CalorFormatter.cs:441`, `:481`); the fix attached to `Calor0410` (`EffectEnforcementPass.cs:1061-1088`) is not marked safe. `docs/cli/mcp.md:37` and `:93` already describe the fixes as safe patches and the option as `options.autoFix`.
- Budgets in o200k tokens, as the test file records them for 2026-11-20 (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/TokenBudgetTests.cs:47-71`; not re-measured here): primer 1,569 in a band of 800 to 1,600; full reference 16,750 against a ratchet of 17,000; MCP surface 7,900 against 8,000. The primer has 31 tokens of room and the reference 250, so a correction that adds words must remove as many.
- CI runs `calor self-check docs` on every pull request (`.github/workflows/test.yml:267`); it compiles example programs and does not check prose.

#### Why

Arm B of the 0.30 evaluation (#1605), the 0.30 compiler with the shipped primer, reads this text. On the CSV pair it would be told that the calls its own reference solution uses are unavailable and be steered to hand-written loops, so the run would measure the cost of wrong guidance and attribute it to the language. The workflow catalog sends an agent to an option that does not exist; that defect was tracked in #673, which is closed as superseded in 0.31, and this issue takes it over. The correction is text only, displaces no issue, and must be in the commit #1605 pins. #1798 adds the CI check that keeps the statements true afterwards.

#### Done when

- The pull request lists every statement in part A with its probe and the outcome, and each statement is rewritten or confirmed accordingly. The probes are tests in `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/` and pass in CI.
- `git grep -n -e includeEffectSummary -e effectSummary -e '"includeEffects"' -- src/Calor.Compiler/Resources website/content docs/cli docs/syntax-reference` prints nothing. At bc80daeb it prints 12 lines, all in `reference.json` and its generated `workflows.json`.
- `git grep -n -i -e 'auto-fix syntax/effect' -e 'errors (parser, effects)' -e 'and effect errors by default' -- src/Calor.Compiler` prints nothing. At bc80daeb it prints 5 lines (`CompileTool.cs:22` and two each in `reference.json` and `workflows.json`).
- `jq '[.tasks[].steps[] | select(.args.autoFix != null)] | length' src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Teaching/Generated/workflows.json` prints 0. At bc80daeb it prints 4.
- `calor self-check docs` reports no drift, and the tests named in the baseline pass with no example program changed (`git diff` shows no change to any `calor` field under `programs` in `reference.json`).
- `TokenBudgetTests` passes with no constant changed, and the pull request states the token counts of the primer, the full reference and the MCP surface before and after.
- `eng/test-manifest.json` has `expectedTotal` for `Calor.Compiler.Tests` raised by the number of tests added.
- The merge is an ancestor of the 0.30 commit that #1605 records for stage 1 (the sub-entry under the #1770 annex entry); `git merge-base --is-ancestor` exits 0.

#### Not in scope

- A check that fails CI when a statement goes stale: #1798. A check that every workflow step argument exists in the tool's input schema: #1652.
- Changing any effect contract: #1731.
- The effects reference (hand-written in `website/content/syntax-reference/effects.mdx`; `docs/syntax-reference/effects.md` is generated from it) and the `[needs ...]` annotations in the primer: #1798.

### #1745 · [0.31] Run every runnable teaching-reference example in CI, not only compile it

**Action:** amend · **Labels:** tests · **Blocks:** #1605, #1798, #1734, #1835

**Current**

The issue says every teaching-reference example compiles in CI (`calor self-check`, check 11) and that only `AgentDocsSyntaxGuardTests` runs any of them. It is done when every runnable example, defined as one with an entry point or an expected-output block, is compiled and executed in CI with its output compared to the expected output written next to it, and examples that cannot run say so.

**Amendment**

Title and milestone are unchanged. Add label `tests`. Replace the second paragraph and the done-when paragraph, and add the closing lines.

Replace the second paragraph ("Every example in the teaching reference compiles in CI ...") with:

```markdown
Every example in the teaching reference compiles in CI (`calor self-check docs`, check 11; `.github/workflows/test.yml:267`). At bc80daeb the source, `src/Calor.Compiler/Resources/Teaching/reference.json`, holds 87 programs (`programs[]`, each with `id`, `title` and `calor`). Fifteen are also executed by hand-written tests (found by searching `tests/` for reads of that file and of the generated reference): 13 in `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/ExemplarCompilesTests.cs:130-245` (`complete_records`, `copilot_smoke`, `complete_arrays`, `task_8`, `task_14`, `task_39`, `complete_linq`, `complete_ranges`, `complete_reevaluated_for`, `complete_enum_extensions`, `task_23`, `task_37`, `primer_strings`) and 2 in `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/Mcp/AgentDocsSyntaxGuardTests.cs:35-58` (`nested_arrays`, `complete_postfix`). The other 72 are only compiled: one that returns the wrong value, prints the wrong text or throws still passes. No program has an entry point (`func Main` occurs in none) and none carries an expected result, so a done-when written in those terms is met by doing nothing.
```

Replace the "Done when:" paragraph with:

```markdown
## Done when

- Every program in `programs[]` carries, in a field that no generated teaching output renders, either an expectation (a call with its arguments and the expected return value, exception type or console output) or the marker "cannot run" with a one-line reason.
- CI compiles and executes every program that has an expectation and compares the result. The 15 programs executed today keep at least the checks they have.
- All nine primer programs (`primer_contracts`, `primer_branching`, `primer_files`, `primer_strings`, `primer_collections`, `primer_effects`, `primer_loops`, `primer_bindings`, `primer_classes`) have an expectation. None is marked "cannot run".
- A test fails when a program has neither field. The ids of the programs that carry an expectation are pinned in that test (a list, or a count per kind), so deleting a program, or replacing its expectation with "cannot run", fails CI until the pin is edited in the same pull request.
- `TokenBudgetTests` passes with no constant changed: the test file records the full reference at 16,750 of its 17,000-token ratchet (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/TokenBudgetTests.cs:57-63`), so expectations must not be rendered into it. `calor self-check docs` reports no drift.
- `eng/test-manifest.json` has `expectedTotal` for `Calor.Compiler.Tests` raised by the number of tests added.

## Order

Two ordered parts, each one pull request. Part 1: the expectation field, the runner, the test that fails when a program has neither field, the pin, and expectations for the nine primer programs and the 15 programs executed today. Part 2: an expectation or "cannot run" for every other program. Both merge before the 0.30 commit that the 0.30 agent evaluation (#1605) pins for stage 1, unless the registration (#1770, pin rule) records that stage 1 needs part 1 only; part 2 then merges before the stage 2 pin. Until part 2 merges, the test of part 1 lists the programs still without a field and fails if that list grows. Merge both before #1734, which shortens the reference in 0.32. #1734 may not remove an example program; with the pin above, a removed example fails CI and an altered one fails its expectation.

Part of epic #1775. Blocks #1605, #1734, #1798 and #1835.
```

**Why**

The primer is the only lever with a measured effect on the cost of writing new Calor (a syntax sheet in the prompt removed 55% of the excess iterations in July, on the pre-0.30 syntax), and the reference is about to be measured (#1605) and then shortened (#1734). An example that compiles and returns the wrong value would be measured and then kept, and a trim could delete an example with nothing failing. As filed, the done-when is met by doing nothing, because no example has an entry point or an expected output, and the baseline sentence undercounts the tests that already execute examples.

### #1774 · [0.31] Evidence ledger, README correction, and a claims check that reads README.md

**Action:** new · **Labels:** documentation · **Displaces:** text-only

#### Change

1. **Evidence ledger.** Add `docs/benchmarking/evidence-ledger.md` (hand-written; `docs/benchmarking/results.md` beside it is generated and is not edited). Give the file the front matter its neighbours use (`docs/benchmarking/index.md:1-7`, with `parent: Benchmarking`). Write versions with two parts: `calor self-check docs` rejects the current three-part compiler version in any Markdown file under `docs/` outside `docs/plans`, `docs/experiments`, `docs/design` and `docs/process` (`src/Calor.Compiler/SelfCheck/DocDriftChecker.cs:265-271`). Every record in the ledger is a repository-relative path; the table below drops the prefix `bench/phase0-agent-native/` after its first use. The first version has at least these rows:

   | Result | Record |
   |---|---|
   | Seeded-defect probe, July 2026: Calor removed 9 of 9 seeded defects, C# 4 of 9; adjudicated a hit. Nine fixture pairs, three runs per arm, pinned model, pre-0.30 syntax. The C# arm held each contract only as a doc comment. Measures detection of maintainer-seeded defects | `bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/ws5-probe-001/VERDICT.md:21-26`; `epochs/m5-compare-001/VERDICT.md:79-85`; `pairs/W5B-001-shipping-quote/csharp/Quote.cs:11-14` |
   | Real-scale venue, August 2026: adjudicated a miss and retired; its Calor arm never invoked the Calor compiler | `docs/plans/call-s-adjudication.md:3-4`, `:112-116`, `:134-136` |

A measured cost, not an advantage | `epochs/e1a-attribution/VERDICT.md:11-28`; `epochs/hardness-check-001/VERDICT.md`; `epochs/hardness-check-002/VERDICT.md` |
   | Calor-against-Calor results, marked as such: loop tooling (PP-L5), build-time proof (PP-G3), toolchain parity (PP-W5) | `epochs/m5-compare-001/VERDICT.md:3-5`, `:27-36`; `epochs/guarantees-probe-001/VERDICT.md:50`; `epochs/w5-parity-002/VERDICT.md:1` |
   | Static evaluation (217 paired programs, 8 calculated metrics): excluded from advantage claims by its own text; 17 of its 226 registered pairs were equivalent under the registered oracle | `docs/benchmarking/results.md:20-23`; `docs/plans/v0.24-b1-results.md:26-39` |
   | End-to-end agent-task benchmark: one snapshot of 2026-02-16 (77 of 89 tasks, compiler 0.2.3, model not recorded); excluded from advantage claims until it has a neutral design (#1736) | `website/content/benchmarking/agent-tasks.mdx:9-19` |

2. **README.** Replace the paragraph under "### Benchmark Results" (`README.md:95`) and the list "This tradeoff pays off when:" (`README.md:87-91`) with a short statement of what is and is not established, consistent with `docs/benchmarking/results.md:20-23`, and a link to the ledger. It says at least: the static benchmark outputs do not establish a language, coding-agent productivity, correctness, or safety advantage (the wording of the results page); one catch-rate advantage was shown once, at fixture scale, on seeded defects, against C# without executable specifications, on the pre-0.30 syntax; writing new Calor cost more agent iterations than C# when last measured (July 2026, pre-0.30 syntax); no agent measurement of the 0.30 syntax has been published. Date the last statement, so that it cannot read as current after #1605 publishes; the 0.31 close-out replaces it.
3. **Claims check.** Add a check that reads `README.md` and the ledger and fails when the README contains "measurable advantages" or "tradeoff pays off", lacks the link to the ledger, or lacks the results page's sentence that the static outputs "do not establish a language, coding-agent productivity, correctness, or safety advantage" (compared after collapsing whitespace, as `website/tests/public-claims.spec.ts:378-380` does for the results page); and when a ledger row cites a record path that does not exist. Implement it as a test in `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/` or as a check in `calor self-check docs`, both of which run on every pull request, or extend `website/tests/public-claims.spec.ts`. With the website route, add `README.md` and `docs/benchmarking/**` to both `paths:` lists in `.github/workflows/website-tests.yml` (`:6`, `:9`), which today would not start the job for a README-only change.
4. **Link** to the ledger from `website/content/benchmarking/evidence-status.mdx`.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- `README.md:95`: "Calor shows measurable advantages in AI agent comprehension, error detection, edit precision, and refactoring stability. C# wins on token efficiency ...". `README.md:87-91`: "This tradeoff pays off when: Agents need to **reason** about code behavior ...".
- `docs/benchmarking/results.md:1-2` marks the file auto-generated from `website/public/data/benchmark-results.json`; `:20-23` says the outputs "do not establish a language, coding-agent productivity, correctness, or safety advantage".
- `website/tests/public-claims.spec.ts` reads four files outside `website/`: `../CHANGELOG.md` (`:174`), `../bench/phase0-agent-native/effect-rows-benefit-ledger.json` (`:215`), `../docs/benchmarking/results.md` (`:366`) and `../Directory.Build.props` (`:386`). It never reads `README.md` (no match for "README" in the file). It already forbids "tradeoff pays off" on the website's trade-offs page (`:616`) and "Overall Advantage" in the generated results (`:383-385`).
- `calor self-check docs` covers `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/syntax-reference`, `docs/cli`, `docs/semantics`, the website content, and `docs/**/*.md` for the version scan only (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/SelfCheckCommand.cs:45-47`). It does not read the root `README.md`.
- `website/content/benchmarking/evidence-status.mdx` exists and covers the paused effect-rows study and the unadjudicated M0 review. It lists no adjudicated agent result.
- `docs/plans/evidence/` is closed to new files (`docs/plans/evidence/README.md`, enforced by `EvidenceTreeGuardTests`), so the ledger does not go there.
- The seeded-defect result: `ws5-probe-001/VERDICT.md:21-24` (9/9 against 4/9, model `claude-opus-4-8`, 54 runs), adjudicated "HIT" at `m5-compare-001/VERDICT.md:83-85`. The gates document describes that measure as detection of seeded defects on a frozen set of nine (`docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md:323`).

#### Why

The repository holds one adjudicated Calor-against-C# hit and one adjudicated miss, and no single place lists them with their limits. Without that list the results are easy to misstate in both directions: "every attempt ended undecided" is false, and the README claims advantages that the project's own results page disclaims. Two published instruments, the end-to-end task benchmark and the static evaluation, are excluded from advantage claims until the first has a neutral design (#1736) and the second is restricted to oracle-equivalent pairs (17 of 226 today), and the ledger is where that is recorded. Later milestones publish more results (#1605, the 0.32 replication, the per-agent runs), and each needs a place to be entered with its comparison type and syntax version; the release close-out in the plan of record appends to this ledger. The change is text and one check, and displaces no issue. Publication of the ledger and the corrected README is a 0.31 exit criterion.

#### Done when

- `docs/benchmarking/evidence-ledger.md` exists with the columns above and at least the eight rows listed, and every record path it cites exists.
- `README.md` no longer contains "measurable advantages" or "tradeoff pays off", links to the ledger, and contains the sentence that the static outputs "do not establish a language, coding-agent productivity, correctness, or safety advantage".
- The claims check passes on `main`, and its own tests show it failing on a README with the old paragraph restored and on a ledger row whose record path is missing.
- A pull request that changes only `README.md` runs the check (shown by the workflow's path filter or by the job it lives in).
- `calor self-check docs` reports no drift. If the check is an xUnit test, `eng/test-manifest.json` has `expectedTotal` raised by the number of tests added.
- `website/content/benchmarking/evidence-status.mdx` links to the ledger.

#### Not in scope

- Entering the #1605 result: done at the 0.31 close-out under the checklist in the plan of record (#1765).
- Rewriting the website's benchmarking pages or `docs/benchmarking/index.md`.
- The external anchor on published per-language agent results: #1829.
