## Epic #1768 · [0.31] Epic: the proof-soundness instrument is restored

New epic. Labels: epic.

#### Goal

The soundness sweep judges programs written in the 0.30 language again. A successor packet of generated cases in 0.30 syntax is registered, its expected verdicts are frozen from a macOS run with every changed verdict explained, and the daily job on `main` has been green for the number of consecutive runs the release rule sets for the 0.31 exit.

#### Why

The soundness sweep (`bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep`, run daily by `.github/workflows/soundness-sweep.yml`) is the only instrument that checks a "proven" result of the verifier against something independent. It generates 710 single-function cases from the packet in `tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep/`, compiles each with the compiler under test, and executes a hand-written C# rendering of the same property over a registered input domain. A *false proof* is a property the verifier reports proven while that oracle finds an input that violates it.

At HEAD `bc80daeb` the instrument does not work:

- **It is failing.** The scheduled job opened #1694 with 10 gate failures (issue body; the tracker dates it 2026-10-28). The issue has no milestone and no priority label.
- **It cannot judge 0.30 at all.** All 136 templates are in the retired syntax: 799 lines of `templates.json` contain a `§` marker (`grep -c '§'`), and so do four string literals in the generator and the harness. 0.30 makes every such marker the lexer error `Calor0006` (`CHANGELOG.md:31-32`). Reading the harness, a 0.30 compiler fails the solver-availability controls and the run stops with exit code 5 before any sweep case (`bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep/Program.cs:91-94`). No run was inspected to confirm this.
- **Pull-request CI does not notice.** The one compiler test that checks the cases still lex and parse returns early when the front-end files differ from the v0.22.0 blobs the packet pins (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/SoundnessRegistration/SoundnessRegistrationTests.cs:134-137`). At HEAD 29 of the 31 pinned files differ, including all eight front-end files (`git hash-object` against `tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep/manifest-B1.json`).
- **Later milestones are measured by it.** #1660, #1661 and #1662 (0.35) state their results in terms of the sweep, each 0.35 verifier step on the contract and obligation engines adds controls to it, and #1697 (0.36) requires #1694 resolved before a release claim rests on it.

The work has a calendar floor that effort does not remove. The sweep runs once a day (`.github/workflows/soundness-sweep.yml:26`), so each required green run costs a day after the freeze merges. That is why this epic starts first in 0.31. It spends nothing on agent runs, and macOS runner minutes are free for this public repository (`:6-7`).

The instrument checks contract and obligation proofs. It says nothing about effect checking or null safety.

#### Work

| Issue | Item |
|---|---|
| #1694 | The tracker for the failing sweep. Step 0: failure reports keep landing on it even if another issue is later given its label. Step 1: record what the scheduled runs say, before the artifact of the run named in the issue expires. Step 2: every failure fixed or dispositioned on the ported cases, until a run is fit to freeze. |
| #1769 | Decision record for the successor packet: where it lives, its slots and channels, how preconditions map now that they are parameter refinements, which rows are retired, the procedure for adding rows later, the disposition of the frozen whitelist and of the baseline manifests, and the state of the fixture between port and freeze. |
| #1781 | The port: templates, generator and harness literals in 0.30 syntax. The validity test runs on every pull request again. |
| #1778 | The registration: expected verdicts frozen from a `macos-15` run, in a reviewed pull request that classifies every changed verdict as port-only or real. |
| #1783 | The release rule: how many consecutive green daily runs the 0.31 exit needs, and the larger count for 0.35 entry, 0.36 entry and any release note that says "proven". Then the record that the 0.31 count was met. |

#### Sequence

1. #1694 steps 0 and 1, immediately. The new issues of this epic do not carry the `soundness-sweep` label, which is reserved for #1694, so failure reports keep landing on #1694; step 0 makes the lookup robust by title. The artifact of the run named in #1694 is kept 30 days; for a run of 2026-10-28 that is until about 2026-11-27.
2. #1769, and with it part 1 of #1783, whose restart rule cites a command the design record names. Both are maintainer decisions recorded in `docs/plans/0.31-toolchain-and-sweep-record.md`, in the same session as the toolchain decisions of #1639.
3. #1781. The port needs the design.
4. #1694 step 2. The failures can be fixed and verified only on cases a 0.30 compiler can read.
5. #1778. The freeze command refuses a run that fails a check, so #1694 comes first.
6. #1783 part 2. Daily green runs are counted from the #1778 merge. The 0.31 release cannot be earlier than that merge plus the exit count in days.

#### Done when

- The successor packet is registered and frozen (#1778 closed), and `EveryCaseIsValidCalorBeforeVerification` runs without an early return in the `tests (compiler)` check.
- #1694 is closed with a disposition for each of its failures, and no issue titled "Soundness sweep failing on main" is open.
- `docs/release-policy.md` states both run counts and the rule for when a count restarts.
- No issue other than #1694 carries the `soundness-sweep` label.
- The scheduled sweep has passed its gate on `main` for the 0.31 exit count, counted from the #1778 merge, and the counted runs are listed in `docs/plans/0.31-toolchain-and-sweep-record.md` (#1783 closed). This is the second exit criterion of 0.31.

#### Not in this epic

- Re-registering the packet after the 0.32 rewrite: #1803 and #1807. The count for 0.35 entry restarts there if a case changes.
- New controls for new verifier steps: #875, #1660, #1661 and the other 0.35 issues, which use the row-addition procedure #1769 defines.
- Controls for the bug-pattern and taint analyses: #1723 and #1858 (0.35). #1769 only decides which instrument holds them.
- The end-to-end agent-task suite, the other stale instrument: #1736.
- The pilot evidence-readiness check that consumes #1694's resolution: #1704 (0.36).
- Running the harness anywhere but macOS ARM64.
- The `net10.0` output paths in `scripts/run-soundness-sweep.sh:18` and `:31`, which change only if #1639 changes the tool framework.

### #1694 · Soundness sweep failing on main

**Action:** move-in (from none) · **Labels:** soundness-sweep, p1 · **Depends on:** #1781 (step 2 only) · **Blocks:** #1778, #1697, #1704

**Current**

The scheduled job opened the issue, and its body is that job's report: the sweep failed on `main` at commit `3c04f111` (run 40691314916) with 10 gate failures, the first a changed verdict (`R1-POST-METHOD-RECEIVER-001` expected `refusal-validated/Unsupported`, got `harness-invalid/None`). It points to the README section "When the scheduled sweep fails". It has the label `soundness-sweep` and no milestone, priority, plan or done-when. The body carries no date; the tracker dates the issue 2026-10-28 (not verified from the repository).

**Moved from** no milestone because the only proof-soundness instrument is failing and unscheduled, and #1697 and #1704 already say its fix must not be postponed.

**Amendment**

Milestone 0.31. Labels: keep `soundness-sweep`, add `p1`. Title unchanged: the notify job creates its tracker with exactly this title (`.github/workflows/soundness-sweep.yml:187`), and step 0 below makes the job find the tracker by it. Leave the generated body in place and append:

```markdown
## Plan (0.31)

The failures above were recorded before the 0.30 rewrite reached `main`, against the retired `§` syntax: that run still judged cases. Since v0.30 every case in the packet is a lexer error (`Calor0006`), so the sweep judges nothing until the packet is ported to 0.30 syntax (#1781). This issue stays the tracker for the failures. A *case* is one generated single-function program with its oracle. A *verdict* is the pair (class, token) the harness records for a case. The *fixture* is `tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep/expected-verdicts.json`, the expected verdicts.

**Step 0: keep failure reports landing here.** The notify job comments on whichever open issue with the `soundness-sweep` label `gh issue list --limit 1` returns, the most recently created one (`.github/workflows/soundness-sweep.yml:183`). It does not read the title. No other issue of this plan carries the label (it is reserved for this tracker), but any issue that is given it later would capture the reports. Change the lookup to list the open issues with the label (`--json number,title`, without `--limit 1`) and take the one whose title is exactly `Soundness sweep failing on main`, and assert the new lookup in `test_workflow_runs_the_gate_and_notifies_with_issues_write_only` (`scripts/test_soundness_sweep_gate.py:226-238`), which reads the workflow text. A pull request that changes the workflow file also runs the full sweep (`.github/workflows/soundness-sweep.yml:78`), which fails as `main` does. Say so in the pull request. Whether `sweep` is a required status check on `main` was not verified; if it is, the merge needs the maintainer's override.

**Step 1: record what the runs say.** No code. One comment with:

1. The most recent scheduled run on `main`: URL, commit, conclusion of the `sweep` job, the gate headline, and whether the commit is before or after v0.30. Reading the harness predicts that a 0.30 compiler fails the solver-availability controls and `run` exits 5 before any sweep case (`bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep/Program.cs:91-94`). Confirm or correct that.
2. All ten failures of run 40691314916, copied from its job summary (case, expected, got), and the date of that run. Do this item first: artifacts are retained 30 days (`.github/workflows/soundness-sweep.yml:146`), so the artifact of a run of 2026-10-28 expires about 2026-11-27. Download it, attach or store a copy that outlives the retention, and link the copy.
3. The last green scheduled run before that run and the first failing one, with their commits. The sweep is daily, so the two commits bound the cause to one day of merges.
4. Whether the list of failures changed between that run and the last scheduled run on a pre-0.30 commit. Keep that run's artifact in the same way.

**Step 2: a disposition for every failure, on the ported cases** (after #1781). Diagnosis may start sooner: the retired-syntax packet still runs against pre-0.30 commits on macOS ARM64, and item 3 of step 1 gives the range to bisect. Record exactly one disposition for each failure from item 2 of step 1, and for each failed control, invalid run or finding in the first run of the ported packet:

| Disposition | Evidence to link |
|---|---|
| Compiler defect, fixed | The fixing pull request and the case's verdict after it |
| Compiler defect, claim withdrawn | The pull request after which the compiler reports `Assumed` or `Unsupported` for the form and keeps its runtime check |
| Harness or packet defect, fixed | The pull request |
| Intended verdict change | The commit that intended it. #1778 lists the case as *real* |
| Not reproduced | The ported case gives its pre-port expected verdict |
| Case retired | The row and the reason in the #1769 record |

A finding is never accepted as expected: "Do not edit the fixture to make the job green" (`bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep/README.md:69`). File each compiler defect as its own issue with the `p0` or `p1` label, as that README prescribes (`:68-69`), and link it here. If a fix adds or tightens a compile error, its pull request carries the standing rule for new hard errors of the plan of record (`docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39.md`): the conversion scorecard's native count and the density ratio do not regress, and the number of corpus files that newly fall to whole-file preservation is reported.

## Done when

- Step 0 is merged and the step 1 comment is posted.
- Every failure has one disposition from the table, with its evidence linked.
- One macOS ARM64 run of the ported packet is fit to freeze. The run is of a commit of `main`, or of #1781's pull request if the #1769 record chose to port and freeze in one pull request. In a scratch checkout of the commit the run swept, `python3 scripts/soundness_sweep_gate.py freeze --out <artifact directory> --commit <sha> --runner <text>` exits 0 on the artifact of that run's `sweep` job. `freeze` applies every gate check except the comparison with the fixture and refuses on any failure (`scripts/soundness_sweep_gate.py:302-324`). The closing comment links the run.

Close the issue then. The gate on `main` stays red on unfrozen verdicts until #1778 merges. #1778 closes any tracker the scheduled job opens in that interval.

Part of epic #1768. Step 2 depends on #1781. Blocks #1778, #1697, #1704.
```

**Why**

The issue is the only record of the failures and had no milestone and no owner. The failures predate 0.30 but can be fixed and verified only on cases a 0.30 compiler can read, and the successor packet cannot be frozen while they stand. Splitting the issue into steps around the port removes that circle. Steps 0 and 1 are in the amendment because of two things found in the repository: the notify job selects its tracker by label alone, and the artifact that holds the other nine failures expires within days.

### #1769 · [0.31] Design record for the successor soundness packet

**Action:** new · **Labels:** tests, p1 · **Blocks:** #1781, #1783 · **Displaces:** decision-only

#### Change

Write the section `## Successor soundness packet: design` of `docs/plans/0.31-toolchain-and-sweep-record.md`. Create the file if it does not exist; #1639, #1778 and #1783 add their own sections to it. Nothing else changes in this issue: no template, generator, harness or fixture is edited.

This is a maintainer decision. The pull request proposes an option with its reason for each row of the table below, and the maintainer decides by approving or amending it. Filing this issue decides nothing.

Terms. The soundness sweep (`bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep`, run daily by `.github/workflows/soundness-sweep.yml`) compares each "proven" claim of the verifier with an independent oracle. The *packet* is the set of files in `tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep/` from which the sweep generates its cases. A *template* is Calor text with holes plus a hand-written C# oracle for the same property, and a *case* is one filled template. A *row* is one verifier form in the packet's denominator, classified by what the verifier may claim for it. A *channel* is one way of invoking the compiler and reading its claim. A *verdict* is the (class, token) pair recorded for a case, and the *fixture* (`expected-verdicts.json`) holds the expected ones. A *slot* is one compiler binary the harness loads. *Freezing* rewrites the fixture from a run. An *obligation* is a condition the verifier must discharge at one program point; its *kind* names where it comes from.

**Part A: ten decisions**

| # | Decision | Options and what each costs | Plan recommends |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Where the successor lives | (a) It takes the existing file names in `tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep/` under a new registration version, and the 1.0.0 files move unchanged into a subdirectory as retained evidence in the retired dialect. No path literal changes, and later issues that name the directory stay correct. (b) A new directory: six path literals change (Baseline) and later issues are reworded. (c) Rewrite in place and keep the old packet only in git history: cheapest, but the 1.0.0 rule that nothing changes except by amendment is then broken with no trace in the tree. | (a) |
| 2 | Slots and the execution ceiling | The literal ceiling leaves room for 30 more cases (Baseline), and decision 6 and the 0.35 controls add cases. (a) Keep the three slots and compute the ceiling from the case count. Smallest harness change; every case still runs twice. (b) Also drop the third slot (`P845`) and turn the six `CTRL-RETRO-845` cases into ordinary retrospective controls. More harness work, because the slot names are literals in the harness, the report and the gate; it can be done later without touching a hash. (c) One slot: half the run time, and no agreement check for `freeze`. | (a) |
| 3 | Channel definitions | State what `VerifyRefinements = false` means for the contract channel now that a precondition is a parameter refinement, backed by one probe compiled with those options and quoted in the record. (a) Keep the five channels and their option sets and rewrite their text. (b) Compile every case with obligation solving on: one channel fewer, but each case then yields extra obligations and the one-claim-per-case rule needs harness work. | (a) |
| 4 | Bug-pattern and taint diagnostics | (a) No channel. Those analyses keep their own instruments, `BenchmarkSubjectRegressionTests` and `BugPatternRuntimeOracleTests`, which run in the `tests (compiler)` check, and the record says the sweep's scope excludes them, as row `XCL-BUGPATTERNS` already did. (b) A diagnostics channel: a new claim site, an oracle meaning for "a finding exists", harness and gate changes on the 0.31 exit chain, and more cases against the ceiling. | (a). Post the answer on #1723 |
| 5 | Preconditions | 0.30 allows one refinement per parameter. For the 21 templates with two precondition lines: (a) conjoin them, and put a condition over several parameters on the last parameter it names (the documented rule). The oracle's hypothesis text does not change. The compiler then reports one precondition result per refined parameter, not one per line. (b) Redesign those templates around one refined parameter: new oracles, so their verdicts cannot be compared with the old ones. | (a) |
| 6 | Rows whose source form is retired: named refinement types (4 templates, 20 cases) and indexed types (1 template, 4 cases) | (a) Retire them. The obligation kinds they exercised lose coverage. (b) Retire them and add templates, with new oracles, for the 0.30 forms that still produce a claim-bearing obligation of each kind (for example a same-module call to a function with a refined parameter, or a plain array access). Each added case counts against the ceiling of decision 2. | (b) |
| 7 | Retiring and adding rows without moving a hash | (a) Append-only. A retired template stays in the file, marked retired with its reason; it still consumes its case ids and emits no case. The record says whether a retired entry keeps its retired-syntax text or only its id, row and instance count. New templates go at the end, and `instances` never changes on an existing template. Retained cases keep their id, seed and oracle hash, and "additive" becomes checkable: every id, hash and expected verdict of the previous index and fixture is unchanged. Costs a small generator change. (b) Delete and renumber. No generator change, but ids, seeds and hashes shift in every affected row, old and new verdicts cannot be compared case by case, and every later addition is a full re-registration. | (a) |
| 8 | Template form for the 0.32 rewrite | (a) Keep templates. Every hole stands for a whole token or a whole expression, so every fill parses, and the record gives the procedure #1803 follows to apply a respelling: run the rewriter over generated cases and carry the diff back into the template. (b) Materialise the cases as `.calr` files and drop the generator. The rewriter applies directly; seeded generation and review at template level are lost. | (a) |
| 9 | The fixture between port and freeze | (a) The port keeps the pre-port expected verdict of every retained case, removes retired ids, and gives new ids a placeholder no result can equal. The gate's "verdict changed" list is then exactly the worklist for #1694 step 2 and for the classification in #1778. `main` and the port's pull request stay red until the freeze. (b) Port and freeze in one pull request: no unfrozen fixture on `main`, but the validity test stays unarmed until every failure is fixed and classified. | (a). First check whether the `sweep` job is a required status check on `main` (not verified here); if it is, (a) needs a merge over a red check |
| 10 | The two baseline manifests (`manifest-B1.json`, `manifest-N1.json`) | They list the case ids of every row, and the report and the test `CaseIdsAreUniqueAndMatchEachManifest` read them (Baseline), so they must follow every retired or added case. (a) Keep both and regenerate them with `derive-manifests.py`, once its copy of the id rule follows decision 7. No harness change. The script needs the v0.22.0 and 0.21.0 commits in the clone, and the pinned blobs go on describing two baselines that no slot runs. (b) Replace them with one row list for the successor, without blob pins. The report, three tests and the script change, on the 0.31 exit chain. | (a) |

The record also states:

- For decision 1: the successor's `registrationVersion` and `status`, and what each later freeze writes into `registration.json`. The 1.0.0 file requires an amendment to carry a version, a UTC timestamp, a justification and a reviewed pull request (`registration.json:3059`), and its `amendmentLog` is empty (`:3068`). #1778 and #1807 fill these fields.
- For decision 6: for each of the five obligation kinds, the 0.30 source form that produces it and whether its status can carry a proof claim.
- For decision 7: the command that tells an additive change from a re-registration. #1783 and the 0.35 issues rely on it.
- For the frozen whitelist (`denominator.frozenWhitelist` in `registration.json` and the test that compares it with `ModeledForms.RenderWhitelist()`): whether the successor re-pins it to the 0.30 translator and re-arms the test, or retires both; and, if it is kept, whether a later change to the live whitelist (#875, #1862, #1660, #1860) is an additive change or a re-registration.
- The procedure a later issue follows to add a control, and what an issue does when it changes the classification or the expected verdict of an existing row (Done when).

**Part B: the row table**

One line per denominator row and per template: kept (respelled only), remapped (by which decision) or retired (with the reason), with case counts that reconcile with today's 710.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- The packet is `registration.json` (version 1.0.0, status `FROZEN-AT-MERGE`, `:8-9`; registered for the 0.24 sweep of the v0.22.0 and 0.21.0 compilers), `templates.json` (136 templates, 13 oracle controls), `cases-index.json` (710 cases, each with the SHA-256 of its Calor text, prime text and oracle text), `expected-verdicts.json` and two baseline manifests. The denominator has 100 rows: 54 modeled, 24 unsupported-refused, 15 assumed and 7 not-investigated.
- Its rules forbid editing in place. "No row, template, case, classification, oracle rule, or result class changes after the freeze except by a versioned amendment … An amendment cannot remove a row or case" (`registration.json:3059`). The README says "Do not edit the fixture to make the job green" (`bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep/README.md:69`). `CaseIndexMatchesTheGenerator` fails on any byte difference between the index and the generator's output (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/SoundnessRegistration/SoundnessRegistrationTests.cs:33-62`).
- Counts in the templates' Calor text (the `calor` and `calorPrime` fields), from a Python count over `templates.json`: 112 precondition lines in 86 templates, which hold 493 of the 710 cases (107 lines are in `calor` texts, where 65 templates have one and 21 have two, and 5 are in `calorPrime` texts); 11 proof-statement lines in 11 templates (49 cases); 5 named-refinement-type lines in 4 templates (20 cases); 1 indexed-type template (4 cases); 10 templates set unchecked overflow; 223 typed-literal prefixes. Of the 90 options of `pairs` and `cyclePairs` holes, 77 have a retired Calor member (63 prefix-operator expressions and 14 literals with a type prefix) and 13 are plain. Claim sites of the 136 templates: 112 postcondition, 17 obligation, 4 implication, 2 precondition, 1 guard-emission.
- In 0.30 a precondition is an inline refinement on the parameter it constrains, a condition on several parameters goes on the last one, and there is no separate clause (`docs/syntax-reference/current-language.md:388`). The compiler projects one precondition per refined parameter into the contract pipeline (`src/Calor.Compiler/Ast/ContractNodes.cs:158-190`), and the contract pass reads them whether or not obligation solving is on (`src/Calor.Compiler/Verification/ContractVerificationPass.cs:84`; `src/Calor.Compiler/Program.cs:1074`, `:1149`). This is from reading the code, not from a run.
- "Named refinement types and indexed type aliases are retired" (`docs/syntax-reference/refinement-types.md:15`). The proof statement is `proof condition` (`:115`). Unchecked overflow is a module header field (`src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Parser.cs:1169`). Typed-literal prefixes are gone, and widths use the suffixes `L`, `U` and `UL` (`current-language.md:1219`).
- By reading `src/Calor.Compiler/Verification/Obligations/ObligationGenerator.cs`, not from a run: the obligation for an inline-refined parameter that is not `out` always gets the status `Boundary`, which carries no proof claim (`:639-666`). `Subtype` obligations are generated for calls between a module's functions when the callee has a refined parameter (`:106-206`) and for an assignment to an inline-refined parameter (`:410`, `:586-605`). A `RefinementReturn` obligation is generated only when the return type names a refinement type (`:271-286`), which 0.30 source cannot declare: `src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/Parser.cs` constructs no refinement-type or indexed-type node. An `IndexBounds` obligation is also generated for an access to a plain array, against the array's length (`:855-880`).
- The 24 cases of decision 6 are the templates `T-OBL-ENTRY`, `T-OBL-RETURN`, `T-OBL-SUBTYPE` and `T-OBL-SELFREF` (5 cases each) and `T-OBL-INDEX` (4). The only obligation template that already uses an inline refinement is `T-OBL-INLINE` (3 cases, row `OBL-REFINEMENT-ENTRY`).
- The contract channel is defined with `VerifyContracts = true, VerifyRefinements = false`, and the obligation channel adds `VerifyRefinements = true` (`registration.json:2805-2806`).
- The scheduled job points all three slots at one build of `main` (`scripts/run-soundness-sweep.sh:51-52`) and a run is 1,448 case executions (`bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep/README.md:24`): every case on two slots, plus the controls of the third slot and retries. The harness stops at `ExecutionCeiling = 1508` (`bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep/Program.cs:14`, `:110`), and the gate fails on any unexecuted case (`scripts/soundness_sweep_gate.py:195-198`). Sixty executions of headroom are 30 cases.
- Case ids are `R1-<row>-<nnn>`, numbered within a row in template file order, and each case's random seed is derived from its id (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/SoundnessRegistration/SweepCaseGenerator.cs:125-134`, `:101-105`).
- `manifest-B1.json` and `manifest-N1.json` list the case ids of each row and pin git blobs of the v0.22.0 and 0.21.0 trees. `tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep/derive-manifests.py` writes them from `registration.json`, `templates.json` and those two commits, with its own copy of the id rule (`:34-40`). The report iterates rows and cases from them (`bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep/Report.cs:34`, `:42`, `:47`), and `CaseIdsAreUniqueAndMatchEachManifest` fails when their ids differ from the generator's (`SoundnessRegistrationTests.cs:64-74`). No workflow runs `derive-manifests.py`.
- `freeze` refuses a run with a finding, a failed control, an invalid run, an unexecuted case or a slot disagreement (`scripts/soundness_sweep_gate.py:302-324`). It does not refuse the classes `harness-invalid`, `flaky`, `crashed`, `timed-out` and `unconfirmed-claim` (`bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep/Report.cs:17`). The fixture at HEAD holds 5 `harness-invalid` and 4 `timed-out` expected verdicts. A fixture entry needs only a non-empty class that is not a finding class (`scripts/soundness_sweep_gate.py:65-92`).
- Row `XCL-BUGPATTERNS` is excluded with the reason "Bug-pattern analyses use Z3 for findings, not proof outcomes" (`registration.json:2722-2730`). Both test classes named in decision 4 exist under `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/Analysis/`.
- The packet path is a literal at `bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep/Program.cs:39` and `:61`, `bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep/Report.cs:34`, `scripts/soundness_sweep_gate.py:36`, `SoundnessRegistrationTests.cs:22` and `.github/workflows/soundness-sweep.yml:78`.
- The frozen whitelist is eight lines at `registration.json:108-117`, described as the rendering of `ModeledForms.RenderWhitelist()` at the v0.22.0 baseline (`:118`). `FrozenWhitelistMatchesTheLiveWhitelistWhileTheTranslatorIsUnchangedSinceB1` compares it with the live rendering only while `src/Calor.Compiler/Verification/Z3/ContractTranslator.cs` equals the blob pinned in `manifest-B1.json`, and returns early otherwise (`SoundnessRegistrationTests.cs:171-184`).
- `docs/plans/0.31-toolchain-and-sweep-record.md` does not exist.

#### Why

The packet cannot be ported by respelling alone. 0.30 made preconditions parameter refinements (#1618), retired named refinement types and indexed types, and changed which obligations carry a claim. Some rows no longer have a source form, and others need a mapping rule. Without written rules the port would settle them one template at a time, and the registration in #1778 could not tell an effect of the port from a compiler regression.

Two later milestones constrain the design. 0.32 may respell forms the packet uses (#1803), and 0.35 adds controls for each verifier step, which the per-case hashes in `cases-index.json` and the 30-case headroom do not allow today.

This is a decision record and takes no implementation capacity.

#### Done when

- `docs/plans/0.31-toolchain-and-sweep-record.md` has the design section with the option chosen, the reason and the date for each of the ten decisions, in a pull request the maintainer approved.
- The section's row table lists all 100 rows and all 136 templates, each as kept, remapped or retired, and its case counts add up to 710.
- For each obligation kind (`RefinementEntry`, `RefinementReturn`, `ProofObligation`, `IndexBounds`, `Subtype`) the section names the 0.30 source form that produces it, or says that none does.
- The section quotes the probe of decision 3: its source, the compilation options and the precondition results the contract channel reported.
- The section gives the successor's registration version and the fields each freeze writes into `registration.json`, the command that tells an additive change from a re-registration, and the procedure for applying a 0.32 respelling to the templates.
- The section states the disposition of the frozen whitelist: re-pinned to the 0.30 translator with its test re-armed, or both retired; and, if it is kept, whether a later change to the live whitelist is an additive change or a re-registration.
- The section gives the procedure a later issue follows to add a control: the new template (decision 7), its denominator row and classification, the amendment entry in `registration.json`, regenerating the manifests (decision 10), the ceiling (decision 2), the README counts, and the command that shows the change is additive. It also says what an issue does when it changes the classification or the expected verdict of an existing row, and that this is a re-registration.
- The answer to decision 4 is posted as a comment on #1723.

#### Not in scope

- Editing the packet, the generator or the harness: #1781.
- Freezing verdicts (#1778) and the run counts (#1783).
- Adding controls for new verifier steps: the 0.35 issues.

Part of epic #1768. Blocks #1781 and part 1 of #1783.

### #1781 · [0.31] Port the sweep templates, generator and harness literals to 0.30 syntax

**Action:** new · **Labels:** tests, p1 · **Depends on:** #1769 · **Blocks:** #1694, #1778 · **Displaces:** #1643

#### Change

Port the inputs of the soundness sweep to 0.30 syntax under the rules of the design record (#1769: the section `## Successor soundness packet: design` of `docs/plans/0.31-toolchain-and-sweep-record.md`), so that every generated case lexes and parses and the harness reads 0.30 source and 0.30 emissions. The sweep is the daily job that compares the verifier's "proven" claims with an independent oracle. A *template* is Calor text with holes plus a hand-written C# oracle, and a *case* is one filled template. This issue freezes no verdict (#1778) and changes no compiler source.

1. **Templates** (`templates.json` of the packet). Rewrite the Calor side of every template the record keeps or remaps: the `calor` and `calorPrime` texts, and the Calor member of each `pairs` and `cyclePairs` hole option. Do not touch the oracle side (`oracle.*` and the C# member of each pair), the order of a template's holes or the number of options in a hole: the seeded draws depend on them. Retire templates as the record prescribes. Reword the `conventions` and `note` prose that names retired markers. A ported case has this shape (the first template with its holes filled; the form is that of `docs/syntax-reference/current-language.md:2015-2025` and was not compiled here):

   ```calor
   module R1Case
     func Probe (int:x | (# >= 0), int:y | (# <= 5)) → int
       !
       => x + y >= 3
       return 0
   ```

2. **Generator** (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/SoundnessRegistration/SweepCaseGenerator.cs`). Three things are retired: the module header (`:152`; an unchecked module uses the header field `overflow unchecked`), the literal prefixes `INT:`, `LONG:`, `UINT:` and `ULONG:` written by `RenderLiteral` (`:250-262`), and the short type names used as hole tokens. Keep those tokens as the keys of `Numeric` and `FixedTables` (`:30-59`): the oracle's domain is filled from them (`:160`, `:271`, `:295`). Map them to C# type names only where Calor text is written. Add what the record's rule for retired templates needs.
3. **Harness** (`bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep`). Each site in the table below. Also write the located claim line into the CLI cross-check record, so that a failed lookup is visible in the run output.
4. **Tests** (`SoundnessRegistrationTests.cs`, in the generator's directory). Remove the early return of `EveryCaseIsValidCalorBeforeVerification` (`:134-137`), so that it checks the working tree on every pull request. Re-derive `frontEndRefusalCodes` of the refused rows from the 0.30 front end (error codes with verification off, which are front-end facts and not verifier outcomes), and list each changed code in the pull request. Treat `FrozenWhitelistMatchesTheLiveWhitelistWhileTheTranslatorIsUnchangedSinceB1` (`:171-184`), which is dormant in the same way, as the record says. Regenerate the case index by running `CaseIndexMatchesTheGenerator` with `CALOR_UPDATE_R1_CASE_INDEX=1` set (`:23`, `:57-58`).
5. **Manifests.** Apply the record's decision on `manifest-B1.json` and `manifest-N1.json`. They list the case ids of each row, `CaseIdsAreUniqueAndMatchEachManifest` (`SoundnessRegistrationTests.cs:64-74`) compares them with the generator's ids, and the report iterates them (`bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep/Report.cs:34`). `tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep/derive-manifests.py` writes them and repeats the id rule (`:34-40`), so it changes with the generator.
6. **Fixture and constants.** Leave `expected-verdicts.json` in the state the record chose for the interval before the freeze. Apply the record's decision on slots and the execution ceiling. Update the counts in `bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep/README.md`.

| Site | What it does | If left as it is |
|---|---|---|
| `Program.cs:418` | Finds the postcondition line of a control case for the CLI cross-check | No line matches and the comparison is skipped |
| `CaseExecutor.cs:172` | Reads the direction of an implication case from a postcondition marker in the source | Every case is read as precondition weakening, so postcondition cases are judged on the wrong diagnostic |
| `OracleRuntime.cs:172` | Inserts the replay driver into the emitted class `public static class R1CaseModule` | 0.30 emits `public static partial class`, named `GlobalModule` when the module has no namespace (`src/Calor.Compiler/CodeGen/CSharpEmitter.cs:2384-2387`). The driver does not compile, and the replay of the emitted code records `not-run-compile-error` |
| `CaseExecutor.cs:206-218` | Guard markers: substrings of emitted C# that show a runtime check is present | The emitter composes refinement messages as `"Violation of <description>"` and `"Value violates <description>"` (`src/Calor.Compiler/CodeGen/CSharpEmitter.cs:6496-6497`, `:6523-6524`). For an inline refinement the description is `inline refinement for parameter '<name>'` (`:348`), so the entry marker `"Violation of inline refinement` still matches. The `Subtype` marker `"Value violates refinement type` and the other entry marker match only a named refinement type (`:491`), which 0.30 source cannot declare. A marker absent from the emission that keeps every guard is reported as the finding `guard-elided-without-proof` (`CaseExecutor.cs:227`, `:297`). Set each marker from the emission of the 0.30 form the record assigns to its obligation kind |
| `OracleRuntime.cs:204-212` | Names the exception that counts as "the guard fired" for each claim site | Review it: a failed precondition now throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` and no longer `ContractViolationException` (`CHANGELOG.md:47-49`). That is the type this site already reads as a fired refinement or subtype guard, so for those obligation kinds the type alone no longer tells a failed precondition from a fired guard |
| `Program.cs:301` | Probe program of the `native-check` subcommand | Lexer error. The daily script does not call this subcommand |
| `oracle.replay` in 8 templates | A C# call into the emitted code | Review it against the emitted member names |

The first three sites fail without failing the gate. The done-when below therefore checks them in the run output.

One pull request is preferred, because the templates, the generator and the case index are hashed together. If it is split, the order is (1) templates, generator, tests, index, manifests and fixture state, then (2) the harness sites.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- `tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep/templates.json` has 136 templates and 799 lines with a `§` marker (`grep -c '§'`). `grep -n '§'` over `bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep/*.cs` and `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/SoundnessRegistration/*.cs` prints four lines: `SweepCaseGenerator.cs:152`, `Program.cs:301`, `Program.cs:418` and `CaseExecutor.cs:172`.
- The reference documents only C# type names and suffixed literals (`docs/syntax-reference/current-language.md:1219-1221`). Whether the short names (`i32`, `u64`) are still accepted is not verified: the parser helpers and the binder still map them (`src/Calor.Compiler/Parsing/AttributeHelper.cs:429`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Binding/Scope.cs:576`), but nothing was compiled here.
- The six tests of the packet are in `SoundnessRegistrationTests.cs` and run with `dotnet test tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests --filter FullyQualifiedName~SoundnessRegistrationTests` (not run here).
- `EveryCaseIsValidCalorBeforeVerification` (`SoundnessRegistrationTests.cs:125-168`) returns at `:137` when any pinned front-end or entry-point file differs from its v0.22.0 blob. At HEAD 29 of the 31 pinned files differ, including all eight front-end files (`git hash-object` against `manifest-B1.json`), so the test checks nothing. It belongs to `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests`, whose pinned total is 22559 (`eng/test-manifest.json:8`).
- The harness builds on Linux in the `harness-check` job and runs only on macOS ARM64 (`.github/workflows/soundness-sweep.yml:38-69`, `:88-91`). A pull request that touches the packet, the generator, the oracle, the harness, the gate or the workflow runs the full sweep on `macos-15` (`:78`, `:83-91`) and uploads the run directory even when the gate fails (`:137-146`). That happens only if `harness-check` passed, and `harness-check` requires the fixture to cover exactly the ids of the case index (`scripts/soundness_sweep_gate.py:65-92`).
- Oracle text is produced only from the case id, a template's `oracle` fields, the C# member of each hole, the type token of each domain entry and seeded draws (`SweepCaseGenerator.cs:139-201`). The draws depend on the case id, the order of the holes and the number of options in each (`:101-105`, `:144-145`, `:210`, `:221`). A port that leaves those alone leaves every `oracleSha256` unchanged.
- The harness project file must stay byte-identical: its exemption from the lock-file rule rests on that (`scripts/test_supply_chain.py:86-87`).

#### Why

Since 0.30 the sweep has judged nothing, and no pull-request check notices a case that stops parsing. The port is the first point at which CI can notice again, and the first at which the failures in #1694 can be reproduced on a compiler that exists. It is a hand rewrite of 136 templates. The comparison of oracle hashes shows that what each case claims did not move, and #1778 then compares the verdicts. The 0.32 rewrite (#1803) passes over these templates again, so they follow the form the record fixed for it.

This issue takes the 0.31 capacity of #1643, which this plan moves to 0.34.

#### Done when

- The `grep -n '§'` of the Baseline prints nothing. `grep -c '§'` on the successor `templates.json` prints 0, or the number of lines the record lets retired entries keep, stated in the pull request.
- `EveryCaseIsValidCalorBeforeVerification` has no early return and passes in the `tests (compiler)` check. Every generated case lexes and parses without error and, outside the parse-only rows, compiles with verification off to exactly the refusal codes its row registers.
- `CaseIndexMatchesTheGenerator` and `CaseIdsAreUniqueAndMatchEachManifest` pass on the regenerated index and manifests. The pull request attaches the output of a script that compares the old and the new index: the cases retained, retired and added, and identical `oracleSha256`, `claim` and `exhaustive` for every retained id. Any exception is one the record names.
- The `harness-check` job passes.
- The pull request's `macos-15` sweep run is linked. On the cases it executed, no attempt has the status `harness-crash`, no claim token starts with `UNMAPPED`, no replay record (`o2`) has the status `not-run-compile-error`, every `CTRL-*` cross-check record (`cliCrosscheck`) with the status `compared` has a claim line, and every case of row `IMPL-POSTCONDITION-STRENGTHENING` has `claim.direction` equal to `Postcondition`. The gate itself may fail. A non-zero `run` exit, a failed control or a finding is reported on #1694 with the artifact and is not fixed here.
- If a test is added or removed, the pinned total in `eng/test-manifest.json` is updated in the same pull request.

#### Not in scope

- Freezing or classifying verdicts: #1778.
- Fixing a compiler defect that the ported cases expose: #1694.
- New controls: the 0.35 issues.

Part of epic #1768. Depends on #1769. Blocks #1778 and step 2 of #1694.

### #1778 · [0.31] Register the successor packet and freeze expected verdicts

**Action:** new · **Labels:** tests, p1 · **Depends on:** #1781, #1694 · **Blocks:** #1783, #1807, #1662 · **Displaces:** #1640

#### Change

Register the ported soundness packet and freeze its expected verdicts, in one reviewed pull request. The soundness sweep compares the verifier's "proven" claims with an independent oracle over a packet of generated cases. A *verdict* is the (class, token) pair the harness records for a case, and the *fixture* (`tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep/expected-verdicts.json`) holds the expected ones. *Freezing* rewrites the fixture from a run.

1. **Get a run.** Start from `main` after #1781 has merged and #1694 has met its done-when. A pull request that touches `tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep/` runs the full sweep on `macos-15` and uploads the run directory as the artifact `soundness-sweep-<run id>-<attempt>`, even when the gate fails. Open the pull request with the registration edit of step 4, which is such a change. If the #1769 record chose to port and freeze in one pull request, steps 1 to 4 are done on #1781's pull request instead.
2. **Freeze.** Download the artifact and, in a checkout of the pull request head, run `python3 scripts/soundness_sweep_gate.py freeze --out <artifact directory> --commit <pull request head commit> --runner "macos-15, GitHub Actions run <URL>"`. A pull-request run sweeps the temporary merge commit of that head (`.github/workflows/soundness-sweep.yml:105`), which is not kept, so the head commit and the run URL are what the fixture records. The command is Python only and reads the run directory and the case index, so it runs on any machine; only the sweep needs macOS ARM64. If it refuses, #1694 is not finished: reopen it, and do not edit the run or the gate. Commit the rewritten fixture. The pull request's next sweep run must pass the gate against it. That second run shows the frozen verdicts reproduce.
3. **Classify.** Put a table in the pull request body and copy it into the record. It lists every retained case whose frozen verdict differs from the pre-port fixture (the fixture at the parent of #1781's merge) as *port-only* or *real* with a one-line reason, every retired id with the record's reason, and every new id with its verdict.
   - *Port-only*: the claim is the same, and the verdict moved only because a respelling or a mapping rule of the #1769 record changed what the compiler is given. Name the rule.
   - *Real*: the compiler answers the same claim differently than the pre-port fixture expected. Link the commit that intended it, or an issue. Until it is explained it is treated as a compiler soundness bug, as the README prescribes (`bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep/README.md:66-69`).
   - A retained case that was `validated-proof` before and is not afterwards is real unless a named mapping rule accounts for it. State how many there are, even if there are none.
   - `freeze` accepts the classes `harness-invalid`, `flaky`, `crashed`, `timed-out` and `unconfirmed-claim`. List every frozen verdict in one of them with its cause. A retained case may not enter one of them as port-only.

   Add two summaries, before and after: the distribution of verdicts, and the status of the release-critical rows from `B1/row-status.json`.
4. **Register.** In `tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep/registration.json`, set the successor's registration version and status and write the freeze entry, with the fields the #1769 record lists for a freeze. Update the counts in `bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep/README.md` (cases, executions per run, expected timeouts).
5. **Sign-off.** The classification is a maintainer decision. The pull request proposes it and the maintainer accepts it by merging. An entry the maintainer reclassifies as real blocks the merge until it has a linked issue.
6. **After the merge.** Link the first scheduled run on `main` in the record. Once that run is green, close any tracker titled "Soundness sweep failing on main" that the scheduled job opened between the closing of #1694 and this merge.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- The freeze procedure is documented in `bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep/README.md:77-92` and implemented in `scripts/soundness_sweep_gate.py:302-335`. It applies every gate check except the comparison with the fixture, requires the three slots to agree, refuses on any failure (`:314-324`), and records the commit and runner it is given (`:330`). A finding class can never be an expected verdict (`:88-89`). It needs the `exit-status.json` that `scripts/run-soundness-sweep.sh:59` writes.
- Classes that make a row incomplete without being findings are not refused (`bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep/Report.cs:17`, `:66-67`).
- The fixture at HEAD holds 710 verdicts recorded from commit `eb0864ab` on a local Apple-silicon machine. By a Python count over the file: 362 `no-claim/Failed`, 118 `refusal-validated/Unsupported`, 90 `validated-proof/Proven`, 46 `no-claim/Unsupported`, 28 `refusal-validated/Assumed`, 25 `no-claim/Assumed`, 20 `refusal-validated/ProvenVacuous`, 8 `validated-proof/Discharged`, 5 `harness-invalid`, 4 `timed-out`, and 4 `no-claim` with no token.
- The harness runs on macOS ARM64 only (`bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep/README.md:14`; `.github/workflows/soundness-sweep.yml:88-91`). Scheduled and dispatched runs always check out `main` (`:100-106`), so a branch is swept only through a pull request, and only if `harness-check` passed and a sweep input changed (`:83-87`). Artifacts are kept 30 days (`:137-146`).

#### Why

`freeze` guarantees that no finding becomes an expected verdict. It cannot tell that a mis-ported case now reports a refusal where it used to report a validated proof. That is not a finding, and it would shrink what the sweep checks while leaving it green. Only a case-by-case comparison with the pre-port verdicts shows it, and the 98 cases that are validated proofs today are where it matters most. #1704 states the same rule for the pilot: never rebaseline away a finding to turn the gate green.

The merge also starts the calendar: the 0.31 exit count (#1783) is counted from it. This issue takes the 0.31 capacity of #1640, which this plan moves to 0.34.

#### Done when

- The pull request is merged with the classification table. No entry is unclassified and no *real* entry lacks a link.
- `recordedFrom` in the fixture names the commit and the `macos-15` run it was frozen from, and the last sweep run of the pull request passed the gate.
- On that run no replay record (`o2`) has the status `not-run-compile-error`, every `CTRL-*` cross-check record (`cliCrosscheck`) with the status `compared` has a claim line, and every case of row `IMPL-POSTCONDITION-STRENGTHENING` has `claim.direction` equal to `Postcondition`. These are the checks of #1781, now on a complete run.
- `registration.json` carries the successor's version and the freeze entry of step 4.
- `docs/plans/0.31-toolchain-and-sweep-record.md` has the section `## Successor packet registration` with the table, the two summaries, the run links and the merge date.
- The first scheduled run on `main` after the merge passes its gate and is linked there, and no issue titled "Soundness sweep failing on main" is open.

#### Not in scope

- Changing what the gate or `freeze` treats as a failure.
- Adding controls (0.35), and the re-registration after the 0.32 rewrite (#1807, which repeats this procedure).
- The run counts: #1783.

Part of epic #1768. Depends on #1781, #1694. Blocks part 2 of #1783.

### #1783 · [0.31] Release rule: green-sweep run counts for the 0.31 exit, for 0.35 entry, and for any "proven" claim

**Action:** new · **Labels:** tests, documentation · **Depends on:** #1769 (part 1 cites its command), #1778 (part 2 only) · **Blocks:** #1662, #1697 · **Displaces:** text-only

#### Change

Decide how many consecutive green daily runs of the soundness sweep each use requires, publish the rule, and then record that the 0.31 count was met. The sweep is the daily job that compares the verifier's "proven" claims with an independent oracle. This issue is text only: no workflow, harness or compiler change.

**Part 1: the rule.** A maintainer decision, taken with #1769 and recorded with its options, the choice, the reason and the date in the section `## Green-sweep run counts` of `docs/plans/0.31-toolchain-and-sweep-record.md`. Filing this issue decides nothing.

| # | Decision | Options and what each costs | Plan recommends |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Exit count: consecutive green runs before 0.31 may be released | 1, 3, 5 or 7. Each run is one calendar day after the #1778 merge, so the number is also the least number of days the release waits. One run repeats what the freeze pull request already showed. Three add three days of merges on separate runner sessions. | 3 |
| 2 | Proof-claim count: before 0.35 work starts, before 0.36 is entered, and before a release note says "proven" | 7, 14 or 30. It is counted from the latest registration (#1778, or #1807 if the 0.32 rewrite changes a case), which precedes 0.35 by at least two milestones, so 0.35 entry is unlikely to wait on it. The cost falls on 0.35 itself: the last change to an existing verdict must merge that many days before a release whose notes claim a proof. The decision also settles whether a published count of obligations with a `proven` column is such a claim; if it is, the 0.35 release waits for the count after its last verdict-changing freeze. | 14; a published `proven` count is a claim |
| 3 | What breaks a streak | (a) Any failed run. Simple, but one slow runner costs the whole count, and four cases are expected timeouts today. (b) Only a failure that the compiler or the packet causes: a finding, a failed verifier control, or a changed verdict that involves no timeout. A run that never reached the gate, an invalid run, or a failure made only of timeout changes does not count. It does not break the streak if a rerun or a dispatched run on the same UTC day passes, and breaks it otherwise. | (b) |

Then write the rule into a new section `## Soundness sweep gate` of `docs/release-policy.md`:

- **Counted run.** A run of `.github/workflows/soundness-sweep.yml` on `main` whose `sweep` job passed, started by the schedule, or by a dispatch that stands in for a scheduled run under decision 3. At most one per UTC day. Pull-request runs never count.
- **The two counts and their uses**, as a table. The exit count is for the 0.31 exit. The proof-claim count is for 0.35 entry, for 0.36 entry, and for any release whose notes describe a contract or obligation in user code as proven, verified or discharged. A published count of obligations by status that has a `proven` column (the proof-yield table of #1843, the inventory of #1853 or #1703) is such a description: the release that publishes it states the streak. This sentence is the plan's recommendation; if the maintainer decides under decision 2 that a published count is not a proof claim, the section says so in its place, so that one of the two is written. Such a release states the streak in its release-health notes: the count, the date of the last run and its link.
- **Restart.** A streak is counted from the latest *re-registration*; the first is the merge of #1778. An *additive* change does not restart it: every case id that existed keeps its three hashes in `tests/TestData/SoundnessSweep/cases-index.json` and its expected verdict in `expected-verdicts.json` beside it, only new ids appear, and the pull request's own sweep run passed. Any other change to those two files restarts it, including the re-registration after the 0.32 rewrite (#1807) and a re-baseline of timeouts. So does a change to the oracle (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/SoundnessRegistration/IndependentOracle.cs`) or to what the gate fails on. A change to the harness (`bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep`) that alters how a claim, a guard marker or a replay is read for an existing case restarts it. A harness change that only adds a code path used by new case ids does not, if the pull request's sweep run passes against unchanged expected verdicts and the pull request says which existing claim sites it left untouched. A change to the packet's frozen whitelist restarts it or not as the #1769 record classifies it. Cite the command the #1769 record names for telling the two apart.
- **How to check.** `gh run list --workflow soundness-sweep.yml --branch main --event schedule --limit 30 --json conclusion,createdAt,headSha,url` (not run here), and the same with `--event workflow_dispatch` for a stand-in run.

Write milestones as `0.31`, `0.35` and `0.36` in that section and never a full version string: the documentation self-check reports the current compiler version wherever it appears in this file.

Post both counts and a link to the section as a comment on #1662. The entry criteria in the bodies of #1662 and #1697 are amended by their own epics, using these numbers.

**Part 2: the 0.31 count.** The issue stays open after part 1 until this part is recorded. After #1778 merges, append one line per counted run to the record (date, run URL, commit, result), from the first scheduled run after that merge until the streak reaches the exit count. Record any break and its cause.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- The sweep is scheduled once a day at 06:17 UTC (`.github/workflows/soundness-sweep.yml:26`). Scheduled and dispatched runs both sweep `main` (`:100-106`).
- The gate fails on any verdict that differs from the fixture, timeouts included, and its summary lists the changes that involve a timeout separately (`scripts/soundness_sweep_gate.py:226-227`, `:266-268`). `main` has 4 cases that time out by expectation, and the README says to check the runner's speed before suspecting the compiler (`bench/Calor.Soundness.Sweep/README.md:51-53`, `:72-74`).
- `docs/release-policy.md` (154 lines, hand-written) does not mention the sweep: `grep -n -i 'sweep\|soundness\|proven'` prints nothing.
- #1662's done-when says only "The soundness sweep (0.27 #1589) stays green." #1697 asks that #1694 "or successors" be "resolved and demonstrated healthy before their results support release claims", and gives no number.
- Nothing in the repository counts consecutive green runs, and nothing tells an additive fixture change from any other: `freeze` rewrites the whole fixture (`scripts/soundness_sweep_gate.py:325-333`).
- `calor self-check docs` runs in CI (`.github/workflows/test.yml:267`). It reports any line that contains the current compiler version in a document under `docs/`, except under `docs/plans`, `docs/experiments`, `docs/design` and `docs/process` (`src/Calor.Compiler/SelfCheck/DocDriftChecker.cs:265-271`, `:950-975`).
- The 0.30 release notes have a "Release health" subsection (`CHANGELOG.md:17-24`).

#### Why

"Green" was a criterion without a number: the 0.31 exit referred to a count that nothing defined, and 0.35 entry and "proven" claims had none. Each required run is a calendar day, so the numbers are inputs to the schedule and have to be decided before the freeze, not at the release.

The restart rule matters because the packet is re-registered after the 0.32 rewrite and extended in 0.35. Without the additive distinction every added control would reset the count. With no rule at all, a changed verdict would not.

Part 2 exists so that the second exit criterion of 0.31 is closed by an issue and not by memory. The issue is text only and displaces nothing.

#### Done when

Part 1:

- The record section holds the three decisions, each with the option chosen, the reason and the date, in a pull request the maintainer approved.
- `docs/release-policy.md` has the section with both counts, the definition of a counted run, what breaks a streak, the restart rule (including the harness and frozen-whitelist cases), whether a published count of `proven` obligations is a proof claim, and the check command, and `calor self-check docs` passes.
- #1662 has a comment with both counts and a link to the section.

Part 2:

- The record lists a streak of counted runs at least as long as the exit count, starting after the #1778 merge and ending at the most recent scheduled run, each with its date, URL, commit and result. This list is the evidence for the 0.31 exit criterion "Soundness sweep green on a registered 0.30-syntax packet for the 0.31 run count stated in #1783". The release close-out checklist (#1765) confirms that the streak is unbroken when the release is dispatched.

#### Not in scope

- A script or a CI job that counts runs.
- Changing the gate, including how it treats timeouts.
- The amended entry criteria of #1662 and #1697.

Part of epic #1768. Part 1 depends on #1769. Part 2 depends on #1778. Blocks #1697, whose entry criterion uses the proof-claim count.
