## Epic #1780 · [0.31] Epic: default migration yields Calor again, measured by a corpus job

New epic. Labels: epic.

#### Goal

Default project-mode `calor migrate` (the command given a `.csproj`) no longer fails its own project check, or the build of the migrated project, because a preserved C# original is compiled as generated code (CS8669). The first project-mode native file counts for the pinned corpus projects are recorded, and a scheduled job keeps reporting, per corpus project, how many files are native and how many are preserved as C#.

#### Why

Project-mode output has been measured once, by hand, in #1746 (2026-11-20, compiler 606dae6f2, o200k proxy tokenizer). MediatR and Serilog came out entirely as whole-file preserved C# (the original file kept byte for byte inside a `.calr`), which #1746 attributes to CS8669. Those two projects hold 149,211 of the 291,307 Calor tokens in that measurement (51%, summed from the #1746 table).

The documented defect is that a preserved original is compiled from a file named `*.g.cs`. The C# compiler treats such a file as generated code with nullable annotations off, so in a project with `<Nullable>enable</Nullable>` and `TreatWarningsAsErrors` every `?` annotation in it is the error CS8669 (`CHANGELOG.md:120-124`, `docs/cli/migrate.md:121-126`). At HEAD the migrator marks every file of such a project unverified and the migrated project does not build. Whether it also still drops every native file is not known; #1782 establishes that first.

Three things read migrated corpus output and are empty or wrong until this is fixed:

- **Density.** Half of the Calor tokens measured in #1746 are preserved C# from these two projects, so its ratio says little about Calor.
- **Adoption.** Per-module migration is the one workflow where agents needed no more iterations in Calor than in C# (1.0x on modifying existing Calor, against 2.7x on new code), because existing Calor is the agent's reference (`docs/plans/agent-native-strategy.md:370-373`, measured on pre-0.30 syntax). A project migrated to whole-file C# gives the agent no Calor to read.
- **Later reports.** The modify-a-converted-module pairs (#1789), the unknown-effect share (#1802), the before-and-after measurement of the 0.32 breaking-change batch (#1784), the opportunity count for the rejecting-guarantee decision (#1800), the per-project native fraction that 0.34 exits on (#1659), the guard census (#1848), the nullable-oblivious count (#1834) and the proof-status inventory (#1853) all need migrated corpus projects.

No CI job produces project-mode output. The one CI measurement of default migrate (`scripts/default_migrate_report.py`, a step of `roundtrip-verification (default)`, `.github/workflows/test.yml:1028-1034`) copies only the `*.cs` files and runs directory mode, with no project file and no references.

#### Work

| Issue | Item |
|---|---|
| #1782 | Reproduce the #1746 result at HEAD, then make preserved C# originals compile under the nullable context their source file had: the project check stops reporting CS8669, the migrated MediatR and Serilog projects build, regression tests cover it, the first project-mode native counts are recorded for all three corpus projects, and what remains is handed to #1647 |
| #1779 | Scheduled corpus report job: restored working copies of MediatR, Serilog and FluentValidation migrated by default project-mode `calor migrate`, one JSON report per project, with a place for later issues to add columns |

#### Sequence

1. #1782: reproduce at HEAD, fix, record the first project-mode baseline. #1779 automates the hand run it records, and #1789 takes its starter files from a project migrated after the fix.
2. #1779: the scheduled job that keeps that baseline measured. The 0.32 entry criterion reads: the #1782 baseline is recorded, and the corpus report job (#1779) is running before #1802, #1784 and #1800 start.

Neither issue is named by a 0.31 exit criterion. #1782 may follow in a 0.31.x point release and #1779 may slip into 0.32; the 0.31 release waits for neither. In 0.32, #1789 and #1647 wait for #1782, and #1647, #1746, #1802, #1784 and #1800 wait for #1779.

#### Done when

- #1782 is closed. Its record in `bench/corpus/README.md` shows, for default project-mode migrate on MediatR and Serilog, no CS8669 and the native, partly preserved and whole-file preserved file counts per project, with the same counts recorded for FluentValidation. Its handover comment is posted on #1647.
- #1779 is closed: one run of the corpus report workflow on `main` has published the three reports.
- An issue that is still open when 0.31 is released carries a comment naming where it moved (a 0.31.x point release or 0.32), and this epic stays open until both are closed.

#### Not in this epic

- Raising the round-trip harness floors back to their pre-0.30 values, and "give the harness project context": #1647 (0.32). Those floors fell for a different recorded cause.
- Token-ratio columns on the corpus report: #1746 (0.32). The below-1.0x target: #1844 (0.34).
- What an agent should do with a preserved file: #1822 (0.32).
- Modify-a-converted-module benchmark pairs: #1789 (0.32).
- Native conversion of constructs the converter does not support: #1659 (0.34).
- Follow-up with no issue: running each subject's own test suite against its project-mode migrated copy. #1647 judges tests on the round-trip harness legs and judges the migrated copies by their build, so the report's `tests` field stays `not-run`.

### #1782 · [0.31] Preserved C# originals compile under their own nullable context: project-mode migrate verifies and the migrated project builds (CS8669)

**Action:** new · **Labels:** bug, p1 · **Blocks:** #1779, #1647, #1789 · **Displaces:** #1746 · **May slip:** yes

#### Change

A *whole-file preserved* file is a `.calr` holding the original C# file byte for byte, the last rung when native conversion or a check fails (`docs/cli/migrate.md:80-88`). *Project mode* is `calor migrate <file.csproj>`, which checks all generated C# together against the project's references and options (`docs/cli/migrate.md:57-67`). In the migrate report a *native* file is a written file with an empty `losses` list, and a *partly preserved* file is a written file with losses, none of them whole-file.

Make a preserved original compile, everywhere Calor compiles it, under the nullable context its source file had in the original project. The work may be delivered as two ordered pull requests: step 1 with the corpus-check recipe committed to `bench/corpus/README.md`, then steps 2 to 5.

1. **Reproduce at HEAD first.** Run the corpus check below on MediatR, Serilog and FluentValidation (the criteria below bind for the first two; FluentValidation is recorded and handed over). From the report's `data.fileResults` (fields as read by `scripts/default_migrate_report.py:34-70`) record per file: native, partly preserved, or whole-file preserved (loss `whole-file-preservation`), and the diagnostic ids in its `roundtrip-csharp-validation` issues. If a project has no native file, record which branch dropped them: an error located in each native file (`src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/Project/ProjectMigrator.cs:1183-1187`) or the drop of all native files (`:1200-1202`). The fix is needed whichever way this comes out: if HEAD already keeps the native files, CS8669 still marks every file unverified and still fails the build.
2. **Fix the three sites** that compile a preserved original from a `*.g.cs` path: the migrator's project check (`ComputeErrors`, `ProjectMigrator.cs:1067-1167`, path set at `:1086`); the `CompileCalor` task's validation (`src/Calor.Tasks/CompileCalor.cs:947-976`); and the consumer's C# compile (`src/Calor.Sdk/Sdk/Sdk.targets:123-134`, and the targets `calor init` writes, `src/Calor.Compiler/Init/CsprojInitializer.cs:219-245`). The mechanism is the implementer's choice, stated in the pull request, within these constraints:
   - the payload stored in the `.calr` stays byte-identical;
   - if the emitted C# gains a `#nullable` line, the checks that require emitted text to equal the original are changed on purpose (`src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/CSharpToCalorConverter.cs:997-1003`, `ProjectMigrator.cs:1243-1247`, `tests/Calor.Tasks.Tests/ImplicitSystemContextTests.cs:56`, `:80`, `:101`);
   - a file's own `#nullable` directives keep working, and the project values `enable`, `warnings` and `annotations` (`ProjectMigrator.cs:1985-1993`) are each handled or listed as not covered.
3. **Look for the same kind of error from suppressed warnings.** With `TreatWarningsAsErrors`, the check counts every C# warning as an error and never reads the project's `NoWarn` or `WarningsNotAsErrors` (`ProjectMigrator.cs:1851`; `src/Calor.Compiler/CodeGen/CSharpEmitter.cs:10757-10759`, `:10792-10797`). If step 1 shows a file preserved or unverified for a warning the project suppresses, pass those properties through in the pull request that makes the fix. If the pass-through cannot be made at all three sites of step 2 in that pull request, the affected diagnostic ids go into the handover to #1647 (see Done when) and the pull request says so.
4. **Record the first project-mode baseline** in a new section of `bench/corpus/README.md`: for MediatR, Serilog and FluentValidation, the pinned commit, the Calor commit, the exact commands, and the files discovered, native, partly preserved, whole-file preserved and verified. As "before", give the step 1 numbers and the #1746 result (MediatR and Serilog all whole-file, so 0 native; #1746 gives token ratios only (FluentValidation 0.972 overall, 0.859 on native files) and no file counts).
5. Replace the known-limitation text (`docs/cli/migrate.md:121-126`, `website/content/cli/migrate.mdx:170-174`) and add an entry under `## [Unreleased]` in `CHANGELOG.md`.

**Corpus check** (by hand here; #1779 automates it). Work in a throwaway copy; the submodule is never edited.

- Fetch the corpus with `git submodule update --init --recursive` (`bench/corpus/README.md:98-100`). Copy the pinned submodule without `.git` and `global.json` (as `tools/Calor.RoundTrip.Harness/RoundTripPipeline.cs:344-357` does) and `dotnet restore` the library project with `-p:NuGetAudit=false`.
- Run `calor migrate <library .csproj> --report <file>.json` with the built compiler (`src/Calor.Compiler/bin/Release/net10.0/calor.dll`, as at `.github/workflows/test.yml:1032`). Pass no other option, except `--framework <tfm>` where the library declares several target frameworks: without a selection, migrate preserves configuration-sensitive files exactly (`docs/cli/migrate.md:221`), which lowers the native count. Choose a framework the library declares and record the full command.
- Build the result. Remove each migrated original from compilation (`<Compile Remove>`, `docs/cli/convert.md:510-524`), add the written `.calr` files as `CalorCompile` items (the default glob is `src/Calor.Sdk/Sdk/Sdk.props:19`), import `src/Calor.Sdk/Sdk/Sdk.targets` with `CalorTasksAssembly` set to the built `Calor.Tasks.dll` (the pattern at `tests/Calor.Tasks.Tests/ImplicitSystemContextTests.cs:142-164`), and run `dotnet build -p:NuGetAudit=false -p:CalorEnforceEffects=false`, leaving `Nullable` and `TreatWarningsAsErrors` as the project sets them. Effect enforcement is off because the migrator checks converted Calor with it off (`ProjectMigrator.cs:853-854`).
- Run the same build once more with effect enforcement at its default (`true`, `src/Calor.Sdk/Sdk/Sdk.targets:10`). That result is not a criterion here: it goes into the handover comment, and #1647 owns making it pass.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- The emitter writes a whole-file payload verbatim with no `#nullable` line (`src/Calor.Compiler/CodeGen/CSharpEmitter.cs:1656-1665`). Native output, unless the module preserves a `#nullable` directive of its own, carries `#nullable enable`, or for a nullable-oblivious module `#nullable disable annotations`, `#nullable enable warnings` and `#pragma warning disable CS8669` (`:1674-1687`). The documented result: a preserved original that uses `?` reports CS8669 under `<Nullable>enable</Nullable>`, and with `TreatWarningsAsErrors` the project does not build (`CHANGELOG.md:120-124`, `docs/cli/migrate.md:121-126`). An existing test shows the compiler behaviour on a `.g.cs`-named source: with warnings as errors and the pragma removed, the only errors are CS8669 (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/Issue1401NullableContextConversionTests.cs:58-68`).
- The project check compiles every output under a `.g.cs` name with the project's nullable and warnings-as-errors settings (`ProjectMigrator.cs:1086`, `:1116-1117`). A native file with an error located in it drops to whole-file preservation and the check runs again (`:1183-1187`, `:1204-1239`). When no error is in a native file, the check is repeated with every file as its original, and all native files are dropped only if some error is absent from that second run (`:1188-1203`). The second run also uses `.g.cs` names, so by reading, CS8669 occurs in it too and does not drop native files. This has not been run.
- While any error remains, every file in the project is marked unverified (`ProjectMigrator.cs:1241-1264`) and its `conversionPaths.outcome` reads `unverified` whatever the file contains (`src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/ConversionContext.cs:1359-1366`). `CompileCalor` runs the same validation and fails the build (`src/Calor.Tasks/CompileCalor.cs:947-980`).
- The language server also validates generated C# under the project's nullable and warnings-as-errors settings (`src/Calor.LanguageServer/Program.cs:48-55`; `CSharpEmitter.cs:10588-10589`), but names each source by its `.calr` path (`src/Calor.LanguageServer/State/WorkspaceState.cs:1677-1684`). By reading, it is therefore not a CS8669 site; a fix that changes the emitted text reaches it too.
- #1746 reports that project-mode default migrate left MediatR and Serilog entirely whole-file preserved "because of CS8669" (hand run, 2026-11-20, compiler 606dae6f2). **Not verified at HEAD:** this issue was written by reading a shallow checkout of HEAD. Nothing was built or run, commit 606dae6f2 is not in that checkout, and the corpus submodules were not initialised. Whether the comparison run at `ProjectMigrator.cs:1188-1203` existed at 606dae6f2 is not known. The two projects' `Nullable` and `TreatWarningsAsErrors` values are not verified either; `bench/corpus/README.md:57-61` says a NuGet advisory fails Serilog's restore "under the subject's own `TreatWarningsAsErrors`", which indicates that Serilog sets it. Step 1 settles all three.
- Tests: `Migrate_ProjectErrorsThatNativeOutputDidNotCause_DoNotDragNativeFilesDown` covers a real compile error in directory mode (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/Migration/EverythingMigratesTests.cs:112-135`). `ProjectMigratorAllowsAnnotationsUnderDisabledDirectiveWithWarningsAsErrors` migrates a nullable-enabled, warnings-as-errors project whose only file converts natively (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/Issue1401NullableContextConversionTests.cs:1206-1283`). No test migrates or builds such a project with a preserved original that uses `?` (searched `tests/` for `CS8669` and `TreatWarningsAsErrors`).
- The round-trip harness (`tools/Calor.RoundTrip.Harness`, which converts each corpus file, swaps it into the project and reruns the project's own tests) cannot see the defect: it writes generated C# over the original `.cs` path (`tools/Calor.RoundTrip.Harness/RoundTripPipeline.cs:871-877`) and passes `-p:TreatWarningsAsErrors=false` (`tools/Calor.RoundTrip.Harness/ProjectConfigs.cs:131`, `:162`, `:190`).

#### Why

One preserved file with a `?` in it makes the project check fail for the whole project. Every file is then reported unverified, a report cannot tell a checked native file from an unchecked one, and the migrated project does not build. #1746 gives the same defect as the reason two of three corpus projects contained no Calor at all. The corpus report job (#1779), the density figure (#1746) and the modify-a-converted-module pairs (#1789) all start from a migrated project that has native files and builds, and #1647 starts from the list of what still fails after this fix. This issue is off the 0.31 exit chain: it may follow in a 0.31.x point release, or slip to 0.32, without holding the 0.31 release, but it must land before #1789, and its baseline being recorded is part of the 0.32 entry criterion.

#### Done when

- A test in `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests` migrates, in project mode, a project with `<Nullable>enable</Nullable>` and `<TreatWarningsAsErrors>true</TreatWarningsAsErrors>` holding one file that converts natively and one that default conversion preserves whole, both with a `?` annotation. The fixture follows `ProjectMigratorAllowsAnnotationsUnderDisabledDirectiveWithWarningsAsErrors`; the second file is a reduced copy of one that step 1 shows whole-file preserved. The test asserts that only the second file has the `whole-file-preservation` loss, that no issue on either file contains `CS8669`, and that both have `conversionPaths.roundTrip` equal to `verified`. The pull request states that it fails without the fix.
- A test in `tests/Calor.Tasks.Tests` builds that project from the two `.calr` outputs through `src/Calor.Sdk/Sdk/Sdk.targets`: exit code 0 and no `CS8669` in the output. One build only: the `tests (tasks)` shard has a 15-minute limit (`.github/workflows/test.yml:508`) and was cut off at it once during 0.30 (`docs/plans/2026-11-16-milestone-0.30-ci-triage.md:469-470`).
- `eng/test-manifest.json` has `expectedTotal` raised by the number of tests added for `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests` (22,559 at HEAD) and `tests/Calor.Tasks.Tests` (275 at HEAD); `scripts/check_trx.py` enforces both totals in CI.
- In the corpus-check reports for MediatR and Serilog, no issue message contains `CS8669` and every written file has `conversionPaths.roundTrip` equal to `verified`. If any file does not, each remaining diagnostic id is fixed here or goes into the handover comment below.
- The corpus-check build of both migrated libraries reports no CS8669 and no error located in the generated C# of a whole-file preserved file. It exits 0, or every remaining error goes into the handover comment below; the done-when of #1647 requires that no corpus project fails to build after migration.
- A comment on #1647 (linked from the pull request) lists, for MediatR, Serilog and FluentValidation: each remaining project-validation diagnostic id with its file count; each error of the corpus-check build with effect enforcement off, by code and file; and the errors of the same build with `CalorEnforceEffects` at its default, by code with file counts. An empty list is stated as empty.
- `bench/corpus/README.md` has the baseline section of step 4 and the corpus-check recipe. Expected native counts are at least 5 of 32 for MediatR and 10 of 112 for Serilog, the native floors of the round-trip harness's default leg (`blocking_floors.default` in `eng/roundtrip-baselines.json`; `ProjectConfigs.cs:139`, `:175`). That leg converts single files without project references (`bench/corpus/README.md:123`), so this is an expectation and not a threshold. If a count is lower, the pull request names the files that are native there and not in project mode, with the trigger recorded for each.
- After the fix a preserved original is checked under the project's nullable context. A nullable warning in it that appears beside native output, and not when every file is its original, drops every native file (`ProjectMigrator.cs:1200-1202`). The pull request therefore reports, for each of the three corpus projects, the native count before (step 1) and after, and names every file that newly falls to whole-file preservation with the diagnostic that caused it.
- The `conversion-scorecard` job (`.github/workflows/test.yml:672`) and `python3 scripts/roundtrip_baselines.py check` in both modes pass with no baseline or floor changed in `eng/roundtrip-baselines.json` or `ProjectConfigs.cs`.
- The known-limitation paragraphs at `docs/cli/migrate.md:121-126` and `website/content/cli/migrate.mdx:170-174` are replaced by a statement of the fixed behaviour and of any project nullable value left uncovered, and `CHANGELOG.md` has the entry under `## [Unreleased]`.

#### Not in scope

- Raising the round-trip harness floors back to 17 of 32, 96 of 139 and 54 of 112. They fell because 0.30 keeps a file with a file-scoped namespace plus usings whole as C# (`docs/plans/2026-11-16-milestone-0.30-ci-triage.md:363-367`), a different cause. That is #1647 in 0.32.
- Native conversion of constructs the converter does not support (#1659, 0.34).
- The token ratio (#1746) and the scheduled job (#1779).

Part of epic #1780. Blocks #1779, #1789 and #1647.

### #1779 · [0.31] Corpus report job: buildable copies of the three corpus projects migrated by default calor migrate, one JSON report per project

**Action:** new · **Labels:** ci, tests · **Depends on:** #1782 · **Blocks:** #1647, #1746, #1784, #1800, #1802, #1659, #1848, #1834, #1853 · **Displaces:** #1734 · **May slip:** yes

#### Change

Add a scheduled workflow that migrates restored working copies of the three corpus library projects with default project-mode `calor migrate` (the command given a `.csproj`) and publishes one JSON report per project. Later issues add their measurements to this job as columns, so the report format and the migrated tree are part of the deliverable.

In the migrate report a *whole-file preserved* file is a `.calr` that holds the original C# byte for byte, a *native* file is a written file with no recorded loss, and a *partly preserved* file is a written file in which some members or types are kept as C#.

1. **`scripts/corpus_report.py`**, with `scripts/test_corpus_report.py` added to the self-test step at `.github/workflows/test.yml:78-83`. For MediatR, Serilog and FluentValidation the script:
   - checks that the submodule is clean at its pin, as `scripts/default_migrate_report.py:114-123` does;
   - copies the whole subject tree without `.git` and `global.json` and restores the library project with `-p:NuGetAudit=false`;
   - runs the migrate command that #1782 records in `bench/corpus/README.md`: `calor migrate <library .csproj> --report <raw>.json`, with no other option except `--framework <tfm>` for a library that declares several target frameworks;
   - runs the two builds that #1782 records there, with the project's own `Nullable` and `TreatWarningsAsErrors`: first with effect enforcement off, then with `CalorEnforceEffects` at its default, and notes whether each passed;
   - writes `<Project>.json`, and one Markdown table for all three.
2. **Report fields:** `schema_version`, `project`, `source_commit`, `calor_commit`, `library_project`, `target_framework`, `command`, `migrate_exit_code`; `files` with `total`, `native`, `partly_preserved`, `whole_file_preserved`, `unwritten`, `verified`; `per_file`, a list of `path`, `class` (exactly one of `native`, `partly_preserved`, `whole_file_preserved`, `unwritten`), `round_trip`, `trigger`, `diagnostic_ids`; `build` (effect enforcement off) and `build_effects_default`, each with `status` and the first 20 errors; `tests` with `status: not-run`; `columns`, an empty object.
3. **Classify from each file's `losses` list, not from `conversionPaths.outcome`.** Whole-file preserved: a loss with feature `whole-file-preservation`. Native: written with no losses. Partly preserved: written with losses, none of them whole-file. Unwritten: a discovered input with no written output. `verified` counts written files whose `conversionPaths.roundTrip` is `verified`.
4. **Columns.** A later issue adds one function that receives the migrated working copy and the `per_file` list and returns an object stored under its own key in `columns`. Adding a key does not change `schema_version`. Say so in the script's module comment. A column function that raises, or that reports a failure of its own, is recorded as `columns.<key>.error`; the remaining columns and the report are still written, and the job then exits non-zero.
5. **`.github/workflows/corpus-report.yml`**, modelled on `.github/workflows/roundtrip-baselines.yml`:
   - triggers: a nightly `schedule`, which runs on `main` (at a different minute from that workflow's `35 5 * * *`), and `workflow_dispatch` on any branch of this repository, so that a pull request can show a before row (a dispatch on `main`) and an after row (a dispatch on its branch). Keep the `github.repository == 'juanmicrosoft/calor'` guard of `roundtrip-baselines.yml:17-20` and leave out its `refs/heads/main` condition;
   - `contents: read` only and `timeout-minutes: 60`;
   - the checkout and setup steps of `roundtrip-baselines.yml:30-40`, then the Calor build of `.github/workflows/test.yml:1015-1018`;
   - it uploads the reports, the raw migrate reports and logs, and the migrated `.calr` files as one artifact kept for 90 days, and writes the table to the step summary beside the #1782 baseline counts, which the script holds as data.
6. **Failure rule.** The job fails when a pin does not match, a restore fails, migrate exits with a code other than 0 or 1 (the crash test at `scripts/default_migrate_report.py:140-141`), a raw report's `data.fileResults` does not hold `data.summary.totalFiles` entries (`:36-37`), or any issue message contains `CS8669`, or a column function fails (item 4). It does not fail on a native count or on either build status.
7. **Docs.** Extend "Default-path CI measurement" (`docs/cli/migrate.md:328-344`) to describe both measurements and say that native counts come from this job. The directory-mode step in `test.yml` stays; it enforces that every discovered input is written (`scripts/default_migrate_report.py:188-200`).

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- The only CI measurement of default migrate is the step "Measure default directory-mode migrate (#1604)" in the pull-request job `roundtrip-verification (default)` (`.github/workflows/test.yml:1028-1034`; job limit 40 minutes, `:976`).
- Its script copies only `*.cs` files (`scripts/default_migrate_report.py:126-129`) and passes the directory to migrate (`:135-137`). A directory selects loose-directory mode (`docs/cli/migrate.md:61-63`), which validates with no references, nullable off and warnings-as-errors off (`src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/Project/ProjectMigrator.cs:1306-1334`). The script counts a file as native only when it has no losses, its `outcome` is `native` and its round trip is `verified` or `deferred` (`scripts/default_migrate_report.py:60-62`), and records build and tests as `not-run` (`:103-104`). #1746 says this report shows 0 native files by construction; that figure was not re-run.
- When the project check leaves any error, the migrator sets every file's round trip to `failed` (`ProjectMigrator.cs:1241-1264`), and `conversionPaths.outcome` then reads `unverified`, native or not (`src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/ConversionContext.cs:1359-1366`).
- `roundtrip-baselines.yml` is the existing scheduled corpus job: nightly, `main` only, 60-minute limit, recursive submodules, .NET 10 and .NET 8, Z3 bootstrap (`.github/workflows/roundtrip-baselines.yml:5`, `:17-22`, `:30-40`).
- Pins: MediatR `v12.4.1`, Serilog `v4.3.1`, FluentValidation `12.1.1` (`bench/corpus/README.md:13-17`). Library projects, relative to the submodules `bench/corpus/serilog` and `bench/corpus/FluentValidation`: `src/Serilog/Serilog.csproj` and `src/FluentValidation/FluentValidation.csproj` (`tools/Calor.RoundTrip.Harness/ProjectConfigs.cs:159`, `:187`); in `bench/corpus/MediatR` the library sources are under `src/MediatR` (`:125`). At their pins FluentValidation's library targets `net8.0` and Serilog's library targets `net10.0` (`bench/corpus/README.md:30-35`). Not verified, because the corpus submodules were not initialised when this issue was written: the MediatR library project file name, the frameworks MediatR's library declares, and whether Serilog's library declares frameworks besides `net10.0`.
- Working-copy rules already in use: drop `.git` and `global.json` (`tools/Calor.RoundTrip.Harness/RoundTripPipeline.cs:344-357`; FluentValidation pins SDK 9.0.0, `bench/corpus/README.md:53-56`), and pass `NuGetAudit=false` so an advisory does not fail restore (`bench/corpus/README.md:57-61`).
- Pull-request CI has no room for this job: `tests (compiler)` was cancelled at its 40-minute limit on three recorded occasions during 0.30 (`docs/plans/2026-11-16-milestone-0.30-ci-triage.md:444-445`, `:469`, `:558`; the limit is set at `.github/workflows/test.yml:508`).

#### Why

Later issues read migrated corpus projects and none of them owns the job that produces them. Five add a column: the token ratio (#1746), the unknown-effect share (#1802, a 0.32 exit criterion), the guard census (#1848), the nullable-oblivious count (#1834) and the proof-status inventory (#1853). Three read the job's output: the 0.32 breaking-change record for its before-and-after measurement (#1784), the rejecting-guarantee decision for a static count of opportunities (#1800), and the native-coverage epic for the per-project native fraction that is a 0.34 exit criterion (#1659). #1647 reads the native counts and the build status and decides whether the job gets blocking floors; #1844 later adds per-project reference ratios the script compares against; #1866 later adds a dispatch input for a switch-on migration. None of these changes `schema_version`. The existing report cannot serve, because it measures directory mode on copies that have no project file. Without this job each of those issues would build its own copy of the corpus, or read a report that shows zero native files. This issue is off the 0.31 exit chain: it may slip to 0.32 without holding the 0.31 release, but the 0.32 entry criterion requires the job to be running before #1802, #1784 and #1800 start.

#### Done when

- `python3 scripts/test_corpus_report.py` passes in the `calor-first-guard` job (the step at `.github/workflows/test.yml:78-83`). From synthetic migrate reports it classifies a native file, a partly preserved file, a whole-file preserved file, a native file whose `outcome` is `unverified`, and an unwritten file. It shows that a report with an issue message containing `CS8669` makes the script exit non-zero, and that a column function that raises leaves `columns.<key>.error` in a written report and a non-zero exit.
- One run of `corpus-report.yml` on `main` finishes inside the 60-minute limit. The pull request or this issue links the run and states its wall time. This is the observable for the 0.32 entry criterion.
- That run's artifact holds three reports. Each has both build statuses. In each, `files.total` equals `data.summary.totalFiles` of the raw migrate report, and `native + partly_preserved + whole_file_preserved + unwritten` equals `total`.
- For MediatR and Serilog the counts equal the #1782 baseline in `bench/corpus/README.md` when run at the same Calor commit, or the pull request explains each difference.
- Once the workflow is on `main`, one `workflow_dispatch` run on another branch completes and is linked from this issue.
- Each report has an empty `columns` object, and the script's module comment says how a later issue adds one.
- `.github/workflows/test.yml` changes by one line only, the self-test added to the step at `:78-83`.
- The "Default-path CI measurement" section of `docs/cli/migrate.md` names `corpus-report.yml` and says that native counts come from it.

#### Not in scope

- The columns themselves: token ratio (#1746), unknown-effect share (#1802), guard census (#1848), nullable-oblivious count (#1834), proof-status inventory (#1853). The opportunity count that #1800 takes from the job's output is that issue's work.
- A blocking floor on native counts. #1647 decides whether to add one.
- Running the subjects' own test suites against the project-mode migrated copies: no issue in this plan does it. #1647 judges tests on the round-trip harness legs and judges the migrated copies by their build. The `tests` field stays `not-run` until an issue adds it.

Part of epic #1780. Depends on #1782. Blocks #1647, #1746, #1784, #1800, #1802, #1659, #1848, #1834 and #1853.

## Epic #1644 · [0.31] Epic: .NET 11 minimum (first part of #1644)

Existing epic #1644. The body below replaces or amends it as marked.

#### Goal

A user whose machine has only the .NET 11 SDK can install `calor`, run `calor init` and `calor check`, and build a project that uses `Calor.Sdk`. The converter parses C# 15 source. The rest of .NET 11 and C# 15 support moves to 0.34.

#### Why

The toolchain has moved under the project. Package managers have resolved `dotnet` to SDK 11.0.100 since 2026-11-16 (not verifiable from the repository; `dotnet --version` on a fresh install confirms it). The getting-started page asks for the ".NET 10.0 SDK or later" (`website/content/getting-started/index.mdx:38`) and the CLI's own error text says "10.0 or later" (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/ExecutionWorkspace.cs:22`). At v0.30 neither statement holds on a machine with only .NET 11, for four reasons found by reading the code (no CI job installs .NET 11, so nothing has been run there):

- `calor` is a `net10.0` tool with no roll-forward setting (`Directory.Build.props:4`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Calor.Compiler.csproj:28`).
- Metadata binding, which reads .NET framework assemblies to resolve types, overloads and nullability, rejects any runtime outside 10.x (`src/Calor.Compiler/Binding/Metadata/MetadataContext.cs:565-583`). The MSBuild task runs the compiler inside the build's own runtime (`src/Calor.Sdk/Sdk/Sdk.targets:51`), so this also affects every `Calor.Sdk` build under the .NET 11 SDK.
- `calor run`, `calor test` and `calor check` generate `net10.0` projects (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/ExecutionWorkspace.cs:321`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/TestCommand.cs:448`).
- `calor init` writes a `tools/net10.0/any` path into the user's project (`src/Calor.Compiler/Init/CsprojInitializer.cs:183`, `:322`).

The other children of the original epic are not needed by that user, and each has a reason to wait. #1641 adds CI legs while the compiler shard is near its 40-minute limit (#1590). #1642 changes the argument layer of every command, so it lands after the paid agent collections of 0.31 to 0.33. #1643 needs a union spelling, which is decided in the 0.32 breaking window (#1797). #1640 covers effect and nullability entries for APIs that are new in .NET 11, which existing programs do not call.

#### Work

| Issue | Item |
|---|---|
| #1639 | A .NET 11-only machine works: the tool starts, metadata binding accepts an 11.x runtime, generated projects and the `calor init` path follow the installed runtime, three maintainer decisions are recorded in `docs/plans/0.31-toolchain-and-sweep-record.md`, and one Linux x64 CI job proves it. Closes on this scope. On the 0.31 exit chain; may ship as a 0.30.x point release. |
| #1638 | The converter parses C# 15: Roslyn upgrade, syntax-kind classification, registry entries, parse fixtures. Off the exit chain; may slip to 0.34, except for its package upgrade if #1639 turns out to need it (see Sequence). |

Moved to 0.34, epic #1852 (`[0.34] Epic: complete .NET 11 and C# 15 support (remainder of #1644)`, to be filed):

| Issue | Item |
|---|---|
| #1640 | Effect manifests and nullability metadata for .NET 11 BCL additions; CI check for missing entries |
| #1641 | CI legs for .NET 11 alongside .NET 10 |
| #1642 | Dependency refresh: `System.CommandLine` stable, Roslyn and MSBuild packages, unused old test target frameworks |
| #1643 | Native C# 15 unions and closed hierarchies with exhaustive match |
| #1855 | Remainder of #1639: the consumer matrices on .NET 11 for all five runtime identifiers (RIDs: linux-x64, linux-arm64, osx-arm64, win-x64, win-arm64), the release SDK pin, reproducible-build checks, Z3 packaging when the packages are built by the .NET 11 SDK, and the documentation pass over the pages that still name only .NET 10 |
| #1856 | Remainder of #1638: a C# 15 project in the round-trip corpus |

#### Sequence

Neither issue depends on the other. Two things fix the order in practice:

1. #1639 starts first, because it is on the 0.31 exit chain. Its three decisions (tool framework, roll-forward policy, release SDK pin) are taken in the same maintainer session as the soundness-sweep records #1769, #1778 and #1783, and are written to the same file, `docs/plans/0.31-toolchain-and-sweep-record.md`. It does not wait for the 0.30 evaluation (#1605).
2. #1638 is the first issue to leave 0.31 if the milestone runs short. One conditional edge can change that: if the .NET 11 job of #1639 shows that Roslyn 5.3.0 cannot read .NET 11 framework assemblies, the package upgrade in #1638 (its work item 1) becomes a prerequisite of #1639 and can no longer slip. The finding is recorded on both issues when it is made; from then on #1639 depends on #1638, and only work items 2 to 4 of #1638 may still move.

#### Done when

- #1639 is closed: its .NET 11-only CI job is green on `main` and its section of `docs/plans/0.31-toolchain-and-sweep-record.md` is merged. This satisfies the 0.31 exit criterion: with the released packages (built by the .NET 10 SDK), on a machine with only the .NET 11 SDK, `calor init`, `calor check` and `dotnet build` of a `Calor.Sdk` consumer succeed.
- #1638 is closed, or has been moved to epic #1852 with a comment that names the milestone it moved to. If the conditional edge in Sequence applied, its package upgrade was merged before #1639 closed.
- Every issue in the second table carries its 0.34 milestone, and #1640 to #1643 end with "Part of" the #1852 epic instead of "Part of #1644." (applied by #1759).

#### Not in this epic

- Effect and nullability entries for new .NET 11 APIs (#1640), .NET 11 CI legs (#1641), the dependency refresh (#1642), native unions (#1643). All 0.34.
- Moving the release SDK pin off 10.0.401, the .NET 11 consumer matrices over all five RIDs, and reproducible-build checks on a new pin (#1855).
- A C# 15 project in the round-trip corpus (#1856).
- The union declaration spelling (#1797, 0.32).
- Building the Calor repository itself with only the .NET 11 SDK. `global.json:3-4` pins SDK 10.0.100 with `latestMinor`; changing it is part of #1855.
- `calor lsp` on a .NET 11-only machine. It spawns `calor-lsp`, a separate `net10.0` executable that no workflow packs or publishes (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/LspCommand.cs:19`); #1855 records what happens to it.

### #1644 · [0.31] Epic: .NET 11 minimum

**Action:** amend · **Labels:** epic, p1 · **Displaces:** n/a (existing issue)

**Current**

The issue is titled "[0.31] Epic: .NET 11 and C# 15 support" and covers all of it in 0.31: converter parsing (#1638), building and targeting `net11.0` (#1639), BCL effect and nullability metadata (#1640), CI legs (#1641), the dependency refresh (#1642) and native unions (#1643). It is done when a C# 15 corpus project migrates, a `net11.0` project builds with `Calor.Sdk`, CI runs .NET 10 and .NET 11 legs, and the dependencies are on stable releases.

**Amendment**

Retitle the issue to `[0.31] Epic: .NET 11 minimum`. Replace the whole body with the text under "## Epic body" at the top of this file. Labels and milestone are unchanged. The epic closes in 0.31 when #1639 is closed and #1638 is closed or moved to epic #1852; it is not held open for the 0.34 work. #1640 to #1643 end with "Part of #1644." today; #1759 moves them to 0.34 and changes that line to the #1852 epic when it files that epic.

**Why**

The 0.31 plan had added work to the milestone and displaced none, so the epic is split and 0.31 keeps only what a machine with only .NET 11 needs. That minimum has to include the metadata manifest, the two generated project templates and the `calor init` path, because roll-forward alone leaves every compile either failing or silently without metadata. The work that leaves gets its own issues in epic #1852, #1855 and #1856, so that nothing is dropped when #1639 and #1638 close. The old title promises C# 15 support, which the 0.31 body no longer delivers.

### #1638 · [0.31] Converter parses C# 15 (Roslyn upgrade); new constructs native or preserved with a reason

**Action:** amend · **Labels:** enhancement, p1 · **Blocks:** #1643, #1856 · **Displaces:** n/a (existing issue) · **May slip:** yes

**Current**

Upgrade `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp` from 5.3.0 to the release that parses C# 15. Classify every new C# 15 syntax kind as converted natively or preserved with a reason, add C# 15 fixtures to the scorecard and the conversion snapshots, add a small C# 15 project to the round-trip corpus, and add registry entries to `FeatureSupport.cs`. Done when C# 15 sources parse, the new constructs are native or preserved with a reason, and scorecard, snapshots and harness are at baseline.

**Amendment**

Title unchanged. Five edits to the existing body; Work item 1 (the package bump and the API-break fixes) stays as it is.

1. In `## Change`, replace the second sentence (its file references are stale) with:

```markdown
The converter already defaults to `LanguageVersion.Preview` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/ConvertCommand.cs:383-385`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Mcp/Tools/ConvertTool.cs:947` and `:956-958`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/RoslynSyntaxVisitor.cs:23`). An explicit version string is parsed by Roslyn's `LanguageVersionFacts.TryParse`; add a test that `15` is accepted after the upgrade.
```

2. In `## Work`, replace items 2, 3 and 4 with:

```markdown
2. **Classify every new C# 15 syntax kind.** Commit the list of `SyntaxKind` members that the new package has and 5.3.0 does not, and a test over that list in `tests/Calor.Conversion.Tests` (beside `RegistryConformanceTests.cs`, which already ties registry claims to converter behaviour). For every new node kind (keyword and token kinds are listed but need no fixture), converting a fixture that contains it does not throw, drops nothing, and yields either native Calor or a preserved C# block whose reason `FeatureSupport.IsKnownPreservationReason` accepts (`src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/FeatureSupport.cs:1394-1400`). Kinds whose name ends in `Declaration` or `Statement` get an accepted reason automatically (`FeatureSupport.cs:1384-1392`); new expression, type and pattern kinds do not. The converter's generic fallbacks for unknown member and statement kinds are at `src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/RoslynSyntaxVisitor.cs:17314` and `:7313`. Unions and closed hierarchies are preserved by this issue; converting them natively is #1643 (0.34). Nothing may crash and nothing may be dropped silently (#1609, #1588).
3. **Add C# 15 parse fixtures**, one per new construct, as their own fixture set with the expected outcome recorded per fixture (native, or preserved with which reason). Do not add them to the conversion scorecard corpus (the 100 files listed in `tests/TestData/CSharpImport/manifest.json`): its size and exact baselines are asserted (`tests/Calor.Evaluation/Scorecard/ConversionScorecardTests.cs:31-32` and `:115`), and its fully-converted count is the figure that issues adding hard errors must show they do not lower. CI runs on the .NET 10 SDK, so a fixture asserts parsing and the conversion outcome only. If a construct cannot compile against the .NET 10 reference assemblies, say so in the fixture's recorded outcome. The C# 15 project in the round-trip corpus is #1856 (0.34), which needs the .NET 11 CI leg (#1641); C# 15 files in the scorecard corpus, with its re-baseline, are left to #1643.
4. **Feature registry.** Add an entry to `src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/FeatureSupport.cs` for each new C# 15 construct, at the level the converter delivers after item 2, written with a literal `Support = SupportLevel.<level>` line (the fixture check below fails closed on any other form). An entry added at `Partial` or `Full` needs a registered fixture: a directory under `bench/phase0-agent-native/fixtures/d-s1.5/` whose `fixture.json` names the feature key (schema in that directory's `README.md`). `scripts/check-d-s1.5-fixtures.sh` rejects a pull request that adds such an entry without one, and `DS15FixtureRegistryTests` requires the fixture to convert, compile and return its expected value on the .NET 10 runner (job `ds15-fixtures`, shown as `d-s1.5-fixtures`, `.github/workflows/test.yml:613-622`). The same pull request adds the fixture's directory name to the `ExpectedOwner` switch in `tests/Calor.Conversion.Tests/DS15FixtureRegistryTests.cs:664-690`, which throws "Unregistered fixture owner" for a name it does not list. Entries added at `NotSupported` need no fixture. Regenerate `website/public/data/feature-support.json` with `calor feature-check --list`: `TheWebsiteExportIsTheRegistry` compares the two (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/WebsiteFeatureSupportParityTests.cs:239`).
```

3. Insert before `## Done when`:

```markdown
## Why

The converter's Roslyn is 5.3.0 (`Directory.Packages.props:18`). C# 15 source needs a newer release before #1643 can convert unions and before #1856 can add a C# 15 project to the corpus. The upgrade must not require building the repository with the .NET 11 SDK (`global.json` pins 10.0.100, and releases build with 10.0.401); if it does, record that here and on #1639. Unless the finding in the next paragraph applies, the issue then moves to 0.34 beside #1855. If that finding does apply, Work item 1 still lands in 0.31 and #1639 moves the release SDK pin in the same milestone (its decision 3, option (ii)); only items 2 to 4 move. The registry entries added here change the count of unsupported entries that epic #1659 (0.34) starts from, which is why the closing comment reports them.

One finding would pull part of this issue onto the 0.31 exit chain. When `calor` runs on a .NET 11 runtime it reads that runtime's framework assemblies through Roslyn. If #1639 finds that Roslyn 5.3.0 cannot read them, Work item 1 (the package upgrade) becomes a prerequisite of #1639: it then lands in 0.31 as its own pull request, the finding is recorded here and on #1639, #1639 gains a dependency on this issue, and only items 2 to 4 stay free to move. Otherwise no 0.31 exit criterion depends on this issue: it is the first to move if 0.31 runs short, and it may slip to 0.34 without holding the release.

4. Replace `## Done when` with:

### #1639 · [0.31] Build and target net11.0: Calor.Sdk, Calor.Tasks, generated code; decide tool TFM and SDK pin

**Action:** amend · **Labels:** enhancement, p1 · **Blocks:** #1640, #1641, #1855, #1705 · **Displaces:** n/a (existing issue)

**Current**

Asks for end-to-end .NET 11 support in one issue: `Calor.Sdk` and `Calor.Tasks` working in `net11.0` projects with a .NET 11 consumer in the five-RID matrix, a decision on the tool's target framework, a decision on the release SDK pin with `global.json`, the pin action and `reproducible-builds.yml` updated together, and confirmation that Z3 packaging works under the .NET 11 SDK. Done when a `net11.0` consumer builds and tests in CI, the decisions are documented, and reproducible-build checks pass.

**Amendment**

Title unchanged. Replace the whole body with:

```markdown
## Change

Make Calor work for a user whose machine has only the .NET 11 SDK and runtime, with packages built the way releases are built (by the .NET 10 SDK, unless decision 3 below moves the pin). This issue closes on that scope. The rest of the original issue is #1855 (0.34).

The work has two ordered parts: the decision record (step 1, a maintainer session), then the implementation pull request (steps 2 to 9). Steps 3 and 4 do not depend on the decisions and can be prepared before they are made; every other step needs them.

1. **Record three maintainer decisions** (table below) in `docs/plans/0.31-toolchain-and-sweep-record.md`, under a heading for this issue, and link the file from this issue. The same file holds the soundness-sweep records of #1769, #1778 and #1783, which are decided in the same session; create the file if it does not exist yet.
2. **The tool starts.** Apply decisions 1 and 2 to `src/Calor.Compiler/Calor.Compiler.csproj`.
3. **Metadata binding accepts an 11.x runtime.** Metadata binding is the compiler reading .NET framework assemblies to resolve types, overloads and nullability; a manifest embedded in the compiler lists the assemblies it may read and the runtime versions it accepts. Either widen `sdkVersionRange` in `bench/phase0-agent-native/metadata-references-manifest.json`, or generate a second manifest from an 11.x shared framework and select by the running major version. The manifest is also the list of assemblies that may be bound (`src/Calor.Compiler/Binding/Metadata/MetadataContext.cs:641-649`), so under a widened 10.x manifest an assembly that exists only in .NET 11 is not bindable; say in the record which option was taken and why. Update `scripts/generate-metadata-references-manifest.sh` to match: it looks only for `Microsoft.NETCore.App 10.` (line 20) and writes the range as a literal (line 81). A runtime outside the supported majors must still be rejected. Effect and nullability entries for APIs that are new in .NET 11 stay in #1640.
4. **Generated projects follow the runtime `calor` is running on.** Replace the `net10.0` literals at `src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/ExecutionWorkspace.cs:321` and `src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/TestCommand.cs:448` with the major version of the running runtime. Change no other line of either template.
5. **`calor init`.** The `tools/net10.0/any` path written at `src/Calor.Compiler/Init/CsprojInitializer.cs:183` and `:322` must point at where decision 1 puts `Calor.Runtime.dll` in the tool package.
6. **Review the remaining `net10.0` literals** and give each one line in the record (changed, or kept with the reason): `src/Calor.Sdk/Sdk/Sdk.targets:8`, `:35`, `:37` and `src/Calor.Sdk/Calor.Sdk.csproj:117` (`tasks/net10.0`, a package folder name); `src/Calor.Compiler/Migration/Project/CsprojUpdater.cs:117` (`CreateCalorProjectAsync`, which has no caller under `src/`); `src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/ImportCommand.cs:447-458` (`TfmRank` has no `net11.0` entry, so that framework ranks last).
7. **Add one CI job, Linux x64 only**, named `net11-only-consumer`, to `.github/workflows/test.yml` beside `sdk-package-consumer` (line 1378) and `cli-tool-consumer` (line 1420). It packs `calor` and `Calor.Sdk` with a .NET 10 SDK, as those jobs do (`.github/scripts/test-cli-tool-consumer.sh:68`, `.github/scripts/test-sdk-package.sh:35`); under option (ii) of decision 3 it packs with the new release SDK instead. It then consumes the two packages where `dotnet --list-sdks` and `dotnet --list-runtimes` print only 11.x lines, from a working directory outside the checkout, because the repository `global.json` accepts only .NET 10 SDKs (`global.json:3-4`). Two ways to get there: a second private install directory selected with `DOTNET_INSTALL_DIR`, as `.github/actions/setup-release-sdk/action.yml:18-23` does for the release SDK, or a pack job that uploads the feed and a consume job on a runner or container that has only .NET 11. The job asserts both listings and fails on any other major, because `actions/setup-dotnet` can add a runtime to a private install (`.github/workflows/reproducible-builds.yml:39-43`). Install the tool globally (`dotnet tool install -g`), since the `calor init` targets resolve a path under `$(HOME)/.dotnet/tools/.store`. Reuse the checks of the two consumer scripts by giving them a target-framework parameter and a mode that consumes an existing feed, not by copying them. Do not add a matrix over runtime identifiers (RIDs) here: #1855 (0.34) extends this job to the other four RIDs; #1641 (0.34) reuses its way of installing an 11.0.x SDK for the test-shard and round-trip legs and does not change the job.
8. **State what is supported** in `website/content/getting-started/installation.mdx:12`, `website/content/getting-started/index.mdx:38` and the message at `src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/ExecutionWorkspace.cs:20-23`.
9. **Only if decision 1 is (b) or (c): follow the tool's build output in the benchmark harness.** These paths hard-code `net10.0` for the built compiler and task assembly: `bench/phase0-agent-native/run-pair.sh:450-451` and `:715`, `bench/phase0-agent-native/check-probe-pair.sh:37`, `bench/phase0-agent-native/harness-capture.py:483` and `:487`, and the project templates of the Calor arm (one side of a paired benchmark run), `bench/phase0-agent-native/templates/calor-arm/CalorArm.csproj.template:26` and `CalorArm.Gateway.csproj.template:10` and `:17`. Under (c) every one of them breaks and is updated in the same pull request. Under (b) the `net10.0` outputs still exist, and the record says which build the harness uses. Under (a) nothing changes. `git grep -n 'net10\.0' -- bench/phase0-agent-native ':!bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs'` lists every other occurrence: other benchmark runners (for example `run-guarantees-epoch.sh:65-66`), recorded evidence, and the `<TargetFramework>` of workspace projects. Under (c), each script that reads the tool's build output gets one line in the record, as in step 6; recorded evidence and workspace target frameworks are not edited.

If a step of the new job fails because Roslyn 5.3.0 (`Directory.Packages.props:18`) cannot read .NET 11 framework assemblies, stop and record it here and on #1638. The package upgrade in #1638 (its Work item 1) then becomes a prerequisite of this issue and must land in 0.31: add #1638 to this issue's dependencies and note on #1638 that its Work item 1 may no longer slip. If that upgrade in turn cannot be built with SDK 10.0.401, option (i) of decision 3 is no longer available; write that into the record before the decision is taken.

**Decisions for the maintainer**

| # | Decision | Options and what each costs | Plan recommends |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Framework that `calor` and `Calor.Tasks` are built for | (a) Stay on `net10.0` and run on 11.x by roll-forward. One property; the package layout and the release SDK pin do not change. The compiler then runs on a runtime it was not built against, and the new CI job is the only test of that. (b) Build for both `net10.0` and `net11.0`. Building needs the .NET 11 SDK, so `global.json`, the pin action (`.github/actions/setup-release-sdk`), `ReleaseSdkPinTests` and the reproducibility evidence all move in this issue; the tool package carries its dependency closure once per framework; the package layout checks (`.github/scripts/inspect-sdk-nupkg.sh:36-42`, `.github/scripts/test-cli-tool-consumer.sh:65`) need a second prefix. (c) `net11.0` only. All of (b); users on .NET 10 lose the tool; the benchmark harness reads `bin/<configuration>/net10.0/` and has to follow (step 9). | (a) for 0.31. #1855 revisits it when it moves the pin. |
| 2 | Roll-forward policy, if 1(a) | `Major`: uses a 10.x runtime when one is installed, otherwise 11.x; a machine with both behaves as it does today. `LatestMajor`: always the newest installed runtime, so behaviour and the framework assemblies the compiler binds against change when a user installs a newer runtime. No setting: each user passes `--allow-roll-forward` to `dotnet tool install` or sets `DOTNET_ROLL_FORWARD` (check both against the .NET SDK documentation); nothing changes in the package. | `Major` |
| 3 | SDK that builds releases | (i) Keep 10.0.401 until #1855. No cost now. (ii) Move to an 11.0.x SDK now. The byte-reproducibility evidence holds for 10.0.401 only (#1548), so new evidence, `global.json`, `.github/actions/setup-release-sdk/action.yml:23`, `.github/workflows/reproducible-builds.yml:43` and `.github/workflows/publish-nuget.yml:443` change together. Options 1(b) and 1(c) force this. | (i) |

The record gives the option chosen for each decision, the reason and the date. Filing this issue decides nothing, and whoever implements it does not choose.

The steps and the done-when are written for the recommended combination: 1(a), 2 `Major`, 3(i). Under 1(b) or 1(c) the packages in step 7 are packed by the SDK chosen in decision 3(ii), and the pin changes listed there, with the new reproducibility evidence, join this issue in 0.31: they are the release-pin part of #1855, which then only checks that the pin sites agree. Under 1(c), in addition, the .NET 10 consumer legs named in the done-when no longer apply and most of the rest of #1855 and #1641 would have to move into 0.31; if the maintainer chooses it, the record re-scopes this issue before implementation starts.

## Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- `calor` is a `net10.0` global tool (`Directory.Build.props:4`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Calor.Compiler.csproj:28`). No project file sets `RollForward`; the only roll-forward setting in the build files is the SDK one at `global.json:4`. By the .NET host's default policy a `net10.0` tool does not start where only an 11.x runtime is installed (not verified by running).
- `MetadataContext.VerifyFrameworkVersionInRange` throws when the running framework is outside the manifest's range (`src/Calor.Compiler/Binding/Metadata/MetadataContext.cs:565-583`). The range is `10.0.0 - 10.9.999` (`bench/phase0-agent-native/metadata-references-manifest.json:3`); the manifest is embedded in the compiler (`src/Calor.Compiler/Calor.Compiler.csproj:176-178`).
- The manifest lists 167 assemblies (count of its `assemblies` array). A second check throws if any of them is missing from the running framework (`MetadataContext.cs:651-667`). Whether all 167 exist in .NET 11 is not verified.
- The failure is not always visible. `Binder.GetOrCreateMetadataBinder` catches the exception and binds with no .NET metadata (`src/Calor.Compiler/Binding/Binder.cs:277-291`); `src/Calor.Compiler/Analysis/BindValidationPass.cs:999` calls `MetadataContext.Create()` with no handler in that file or around its call in `Program.Compile` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Program.cs:920-921`). On an 11.x runtime a compile therefore either fails or silently loses every check that needs metadata.
- The framework assemblies the compiler reads are those of the runtime it is running on: `GeneratedCSharpCompiler` builds its references from the process's trusted platform assemblies (`src/Calor.Compiler/CodeGen/CSharpEmitter.cs:10631`) and `MetadataContext` filters that list (`MetadataContext.cs:129`). Whether Roslyn 5.3.0 reads .NET 11 framework assemblies correctly is not verified.
- The `CompileCalor` task calls the compiler in process (`src/Calor.Tasks/CompileCalor.cs:827`) and runs in the MSBuild host runtime (`src/Calor.Sdk/Sdk/Sdk.targets:51`), so the same applies to every `Calor.Sdk` build under the .NET 11 SDK, whatever the project targets.
- `calor run`, `calor test` and `calor check` write `net10.0` projects (`ExecutionWorkspace.cs:321`, `TestCommand.cs:448`); `calor check` then runs `dotnet build` and `dotnet test` on them (`TestCommand.cs:228-230`, `:248-251`). A `net10.0` test project cannot execute without a 10.x runtime (not verified by running).
- `calor init` writes `.../tools/net10.0/any/Calor.Runtime.dll` into the user's project (`CsprojInitializer.cs:183`, `:322`).
- `Calor.Tasks` and `Calor.Sdk` target `net10.0` (`src/Calor.Tasks/Calor.Tasks.csproj:4`, `src/Calor.Sdk/Calor.Sdk.csproj:4`) and the SDK package puts the task closure under `tasks/net10.0` (`Calor.Sdk.csproj:117`, `Sdk.targets:8`).
- Releases build with SDK 10.0.401 only (`.github/actions/setup-release-sdk/action.yml:23`, `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/Release/ReleaseSdkPinTests.cs:18`).
- No workflow or action under `.github/` installs an 11.x SDK. The consumer jobs use `dotnet-version: '10.0.x'` and the `tests/SdkConsumer` template targets `net10.0` (`tests/SdkConsumer/CalorLib/CalorLib.csproj:7`, `tests/SdkConsumer/CalorLib.Tests/CalorLib.Tests.csproj:5`).

## Why

A user who installs the current .NET SDK and follows the getting-started page cannot run Calor at v0.30, and the documentation tells them they can. Roll-forward alone does not fix it: the tool would start and then either fail or silently lose metadata binding, and `calor check` would still generate projects that need a runtime the machine does not have. This issue carries one of the 0.31 exit criteria: with the released packages (built by the .NET 10 SDK; moving the repository's own SDK pin is #1855), on a machine with only the .NET 11 SDK, `calor init`, `calor check` and `dotnet build` of a `Calor.Sdk` consumer succeed. It does not wait for the 0.30 evaluation (#1605) and may ship as a 0.30.x point release.

## Done when

- `docs/plans/0.31-toolchain-and-sweep-record.md` has a section for this issue that gives the chosen option, reason and date for decisions 1 to 3, the manifest option taken in step 3, and one line for each literal in step 6 (and in step 9, if it applies). The file is linked from this issue.
- The job `net11-only-consumer` is green on `main`. In it, with only 11.x SDKs and runtimes listed:
  1. `dotnet tool install -g calor` from the local feed succeeds, and `calor --version` exits 0 and prints the packed version.
  2. Compiling the probe from `CopiedCliOutsideCheckout_UsesEmbeddedManifestWithNoTypeCheck` (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/Binding/BindingRoutingCliTests.cs:257-292`) with the same arguments exits 1 and reports `Calor0272`. That diagnostic needs .NET metadata, so it shows that binding did not fall back silently.
  3. `calor init` in a new `net11.0` class library that contains one `.calr` file (the module from the `ValidationLoopTests` fixture in item 4 will do), followed by `dotnet build`, exits 0.
  4. `calor check <directory> --format json` on the passing fixture from `ValidationLoopTests` (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/ValidationLoopTests.cs:22-43` with the contract added at `:80-86`) exits 0 with `success` true, `testsRun` 1, `contractsChecked` 1 and the status `passed` for the compile, build, test and verify stages.
  5. The `tests/SdkConsumer` template with both projects set to `net11.0` restores, builds and runs against the packed `Calor.Sdk`, with the checks at `.github/scripts/test-sdk-package.sh:126-136` (`Calor0712` present, `Calor0710` absent). A second consumer build that adds the probe from item 2 fails, and the script asserts one literal diagnostic code for it on both the .NET 10 `sdk-package-consumer` legs and this job (expected `Calor0272`; whether the task reports that code with type checking on is not verified). The same code on both shows that the `CompileCalor` task had metadata on .NET 11.
- On .NET 10 the `sdk-package-consumer`, `cli-tool-consumer` and `z3-consumer-matrix` jobs stay green. A unit test asserts that the generated `<TargetFramework>` of both templates is `net` followed by the running runtime's major version and `.0`, which is `net10.0` on those runners.
- `MetadataContextTests.CreateWithManifest_FrameworkOutsideRange_HardFails` (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/Binding/Metadata/MetadataContextTests.cs:228`) still passes, and a new test asserts that the embedded manifest or manifests admit 10.x and 11.x and reject 12.0.0. The range check reads the running version directly (`MetadataContext.cs:568`), so this needs an internal overload that takes the observed version.
- `eng/test-manifest.json` carries the new `expectedTotal` of every test project that gains tests; `scripts/check_trx.py` fails the CI run on a mismatch.
- The three texts in step 8 name the .NET versions that decision 1 leaves supported (.NET 10 and .NET 11 under options (a) and (b)), and `CHANGELOG.md` has an entry under `[Unreleased]`.

## Not in scope

- The consumer matrices on .NET 11 over all five runtime identifiers, moving the release SDK pin (`global.json`, the pin action), reproducible-build checks on a new pin, and Z3 packaging when the packages are built by the .NET 11 SDK: #1855. Loading Z3 at run time on .NET 11 is in scope here, because items 4 and 5 above need the solver.
- The other places that name only .NET 10 (`website/content/getting-started/installation.mdx:142` and `:298`, `website/content/getting-started/hello-world.mdx:14`, `website/content/getting-started/how-it-works.mdx:16`, and the rest of what `grep -rln 'NET 10\|net10' website/content` lists): the documentation pass in #1855.
- .NET 11 legs for the test shards and the round-trip jobs: #1641.
- Effect and nullability entries for new .NET 11 APIs: #1640.
- Building the Calor repository itself with only the .NET 11 SDK.
- `calor lsp`. It spawns `calor-lsp` (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/LspCommand.cs:19`), a separate executable built from `src/Calor.LanguageServer` for `net10.0` (`Directory.Build.props:4`) that no workflow packs or publishes. #1855 records what happens to it on a .NET 11-only machine.
- The target framework of the benchmark workspaces under `bench/phase0-agent-native`: the arms of the 0.30 evaluation stay on .NET 10. Only the tool-output paths in step 9 follow decision 1.

Part of #1644. Blocks #1855 and #1705.
```

**Why**

The earlier minimum (pin decision, roll-forward, one path reviewed, one smoke test) would not have made a .NET 11-only machine work: metadata binding rejects 11.x, two generated project templates target `net10.0`, and `calor init` writes a `net10.0` path. The issue has to close in 0.31, so the remainder gets its own issue, #1855, and 0.31 keeps only the minimum, with the decisions written out for the maintainer. The body also names the decision record it shares with the sweep records, reads the exit criterion as released packages consumed on a .NET 11-only machine, makes the benchmark harness paths follow a change of tool framework, writes the conditional dependency on the Roslyn upgrade on both issues, and keeps the CI addition to one Linux x64 job, which #1855 extends to the other RIDs.

---

## Epic #1594 · [0.31] Epic: test and CI health

Existing epic #1594. The body below replaces or amends it as marked.

#### Goal

A fast, non-flaky test suite and release run that people trust when it is green and believe when it is red, on the 0.30 code base. For 0.31 that means three measurable things: the release jobs pass without a flake rerun (#1749), the index build is back within twice its 0.29 cost (#1738), and the compiler test shard's wall time is measured against its 30-minute target at the 0.30 inventory (#1590). None of the three is a release gate. The 0.31 exit asks only that the release CI flakes are fixed or the reruns of the 0.31 release are recorded: #1749 is a release-process prerequisite.

#### Why

This epic was opened for 0.29 and is still open in 0.31. Of the 18 issues in its 0.29 body, 17 were closed on 2026-10-29 (GitHub close dates). Only #1590 remained, and `CHANGELOG.md:422-423` records its wall-time target as unmet at 0.29.0. The 0.30 rewrite then made CI time a binding constraint:

- **Compiler shard.** `Calor.Compiler.Tests` grew from 16,075 cases (`scripts/compiler-shard-filter.sh:11`) to 22,559 (`expectedTotal`, `eng/test-manifest.json:8`). On CI its tests summed to 2,364 s against 1,223 s on `main` before the merge, historical replays excluded (`docs/plans/2026-11-16-milestone-0.30-ci-triage.md:507`). On the 0.30 branch the `tests (compiler)` job was cancelled at its 40-minute limit (`.github/workflows/test.yml:508`) on 2026-11-19 and 2026-11-20, and once more at 40.3 minutes (same document, `:444`, `:469`, `:558`). The job now starts five test hosts at once on a 4-vCPU, 16 GB runner (four parts and a host that rebuilds archived compilers) and adds an 8 GB swap file first (`scripts/run-shard-tests.sh:47-87`).
- **Release.** Three failures during the 0.30 publish runs passed on rerun with no code change, and each rerun repeats a job of about 60 minutes (#1749). The 0.30 release also ran a dry run and then a real dispatch (`eng/performance-baselines.json:10`, `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/Release/ReleaseWorkflowTests.cs:588-593`), which the release policy says not to do (`docs/release-policy.md:13-14`).
- **Performance gate.** The index build over the 106-file fixture went from 0.38 s at 0.29.0 to about 10 s. After a first fix it measures 3.10 to 5.56 s on CI, which is 8 to 15 times the 0.29 figure. The suite ceiling moved 24.0 → 48.0 → 41.0 s and has about 5% headroom on the slowest runner class seen. Two wall-clock smoke budgets in the same suite have each failed once on a shared runner with no code cause: the 0.1 ms budget in #1749, and `DataflowAnalysis_ComplexCFG_IsTimingSmokeTest` at 60.9 ms against 25 ms. Apart from the #1749 failure, every figure in this bullet is from `eng/performance-baselines.json:10`.

Later work adds CI load on top of this. The .NET 11 CI legs (#1641, 0.34) wait for #1749, and the corpus report job (#1779) is scheduled outside the pull-request shard for the same reason.

#### Work

| Issue | Item |
|---|---|
| #1590 | Compiler test-host memory and compiler-shard wall time. The leak sources found in 0.29 are repaired and an unsplit run of 16,075 cases peaked at 3,183 MiB (`scripts/compiler-shard-filter.sh:8-13`); nothing has verified the 22,559-case inventory. The p90 target (under 30 minutes over the last 30 successful `test.yml` runs on `main`) has not been measured on the 0.30 shard layout. The amendment replaces the half of the body that predates the 0.29 repairs. May slip. |
| #1738 | Index build within 2x of 0.29.0 (at most 0.80 s on CI over the 106-file fixture; 3.10 to 5.56 s today), then the performance ceiling lowered from 41.0 s. Stays open until that measurement is met. May slip. |
| #1749 | Release CI flakes: the wall-clock smoke budgets that have failed with no code cause (0.1 ms CFG, 25 ms dataflow), the historical formatter replay losing its extracted tree, and five concurrent test hosts starving the runner. A release-process prerequisite: 0.31.0 may ship with its reruns recorded on this issue. Its three closing dry runs are maintainer dispatches. May slip. |
| #1726 | Website cold-mobile asset test times out waiting for `networkidle`. May slip. |
| #1728 | Release promotion: the design is implemented and was used for 0.30. Left: time one release from a single dispatch after #1749 closes and correct the policy text, or close (maintainer decision). May slip. |

No other child is open. The other ten work items of the 0.29 body (#959, #1121, #1032, #1031, #1042, #1115, #1116, #901, #1030, #1134) and its seven proposed closures (#859, #884, #948, #1131, #1157, #1159, #1011) were closed on 2026-10-29.

#### Sequence

One dependency: #1728 depends on #1749. The other orderings are not dependencies; they avoid wasted runs.

1. #1749 first. It is the release-process prerequisite, and its third item changes how the compiler shard runs (`scripts/run-shard-tests.sh`). Its three closing dry runs are dispatched on `main` commits before the 0.31.0 candidate is chosen, and none is the candidate commit.
2. #1728 after #1749. The release it times is the first one published after #1749 closes, because a release with flake reruns is not a measurement of the promotion path.
3. #1590's 30-run window counts `main` runs after the last change to the shard's layout, so it starts when item 3 of #1749 lands.
4. #1749 item 1 before the calibration dispatches of #1738 where possible. A failed smoke budget ends a gate run before it measures (`scripts/run_performance_gate.py:88-90`), and one calibration dispatch of the first #1738 fix failed that way and had to be rerun (`eng/performance-baselines.json:10`).
5. #1726 has no ordering.

#### Done when

- **Release flakes (the 0.31 exit criterion "Release CI flakes are fixed, or the reruns of the 0.31 release are recorded").** Either #1749 is closed by its own criterion (each failure it lists has a fix or a recorded decision, and `test (compiler)` and `release-quality` in `publish-nuget.yml` pass three consecutive dry runs without a rerun), or a comment on #1749 lists every job of the 0.31.0 `publish-nuget.yml` run that needed more than one attempt, with the run id, the attempt count and the failure. #1749 is a release-process prerequisite, not a gate: the release may ship with a rerun recorded. Only the maintainer dispatches `publish-nuget.yml`, dry runs included (`.claude/skills/create-release/SKILL.md:33-34`), so the three dry runs are maintainer actions for 0.31; each repeats the release validation (36.62 elapsed minutes and 122.22 job-minutes at v0.29.0, `docs/release-policy.md:105`).
- #1749, #1590, #1738, #1726 and #1728 are each closed, or on the day 0.31.0 is released are moved to milestone 0.32 with the `ci` label and a comment that carries the latest reading: for #1749 the rerun record above and which of its three items are fixed, for #1738 the latest `IndexBuildStaysWithinItsBudget` readings and their ratio to 0.38 s, for #1590 the latest p90 and the leak record described in the next bullet, for #1726 and #1728 the reason.
- **0.31 and this epic may close with the #1590 leak on record instead of fixed.** The record is one comment on #1590 that states either (a) a completed unsplit run of the full 0.30 inventory with its peak RSS, or (b) "not verified at the 0.30 inventory; the four-part split is kept on purpose", with the 0.29 evidence linked (`docs/plans/performance-gate-strategy.md:117-122`). The repository does not record the leak as unfixable: it records the sources found in 0.29 as repaired and the 0.30 inventory as unverified.
- When the first two bullets hold and 0.31.0 is released, this epic is closed. Moved issues stand alone in 0.32 with no parent epic. The epic is not retitled or carried to another milestone.

#### Not in this epic

- .NET 11 CI legs: #1641 (0.34). It depends on #1749.
- Dispatching `publish-nuget.yml`, as a dry run or a release: a maintainer action. No issue here authorises one.
- Warm `calor check` and MCP write latency, and a CI budget for edit-to-diagnostic time: #1818 (0.32).
- The remaining incremental-build reuse tier: #1836 (0.33).
- The corpus report job on its own schedule: #1779.
- The soundness sweep failing on `main`: #1694.
- Converter regressions after 0.30 on the default `calor migrate` path and the round-trip harness legs: #1647 (0.32).

### #1590 · [0.31] Compiler test-host memory growth is capped by sharding, not fixed (#1150 follow-up)

**Action:** amend · **Labels:** p2, tests, ci · **Displaces:** n/a (existing issue) · **May slip:** yes

**Current**

The issue covers two problems in `Calor.Compiler.Tests`. The first is memory growth it calls unattributed: one test host climbs past 9 GB on a 16 GB runner, six candidate causes were refuted, and PR #1170 capped the symptom by splitting the suite in two; it asks for a native-heap profile, then a fix or a documented reason to keep the split. The second is compiler-shard wall time: the job rose to 29-30 minutes when `W1GuideTranscriptTests` landed and its limit was raised from 30 to 40 minutes; it asks to re-measure, rebalance the split, and make that test class cheaper or move it. It is done when the allocator is fixed or documented as unfixable with the split kept on purpose, and the shard's p90 is under 30 minutes over the last 30 successful `test.yml` runs on `main`.

**Amendment**

New title: `[0.31] Compiler test-host memory growth is capped by sharding, not fixed (#1150 follow-up)`. Labels (`p2`, `tests`, `ci`) and milestone (0.31) are unchanged. Four edits.

1. In `## Problem`, replace part 1, from `**1. Unattributed memory growth.**` to the end of the paragraph that begins "PR #1170 capped the symptom", with:

```markdown
**1. Memory growth: attributed and repaired in 0.29, not verified since.** State at v0.30 (HEAD bc80daeb):

- #1150 measured a 14.7% host kill rate after anonymous memory climbed past 9 GB on a 16 GB runner (`docs/plans/2026-09-03-issue-1150-kill-rate-measurement.md`).
- Native traces taken in 0.29 attributed the growth to two sources: Roslyn reference metadata rebuilt on every call in the converter and in test helpers, and a Z3 context that `ContractInheritanceChecker` created and did not dispose. Both are repaired (`scripts/compiler-shard-filter.sh:8-13`; the probes and the fixes are described in `docs/plans/performance-gate-strategy.md:58-115`).
- After the repairs one unsplit test host completed all 16,075 cases in 13.3 minutes with a peak sampled RSS of 3,183 MiB and no swap (`docs/plans/performance-gate-strategy.md:117-122`).
- That run has not been repeated. The suite is now 22,559 cases (`expectedTotal` for this project in `eng/test-manifest.json`). The job starts five hosts at once: four parts, and one host for the historical replays (tests that rebuild an archived compiler and rerun one test with it). It adds an 8 GB swap file first, because two runs on 2026-11-20 filled the runner's 16 GB of RAM and 3 GB of swap (`scripts/run-shard-tests.sh:47-59`). Whether that pressure is five hosts running at once or growth inside one host is not known.
- Two comments in the tree disagree. `scripts/compiler-shard-filter.sh:8-16` says the identified sources are repaired and the split is kept as defence in depth. `scripts/run-shard-tests.sh:16-18` still says the split "CAPS THE SYMPTOM; it does not fix the leak".

No timing of the current layout on `main` is recorded in the repository.

3. Replace `## Change` with:

## Change

1. **Leak: close it with a record.** The native-heap profiling this item first asked for was done in 0.29 (Problem, part 1). What is missing is evidence at the 0.30 inventory. Post one of two records as a comment on this issue:
   - **(a) Verified.** A completed unsplit run of the full 0.30 inventory with its peak sampled RSS. The existing dispatch is `gh workflow run performance.yml -f profile_compiler_memory=true`.
