### #1738 · [0.31] Index build is 8-15x slower than 0.29 (symbol-index binding and the per-module effect pass)

**Action:** amend · **Labels:** bug, p2 · **Displaces:** n/a (existing issue) · **May slip:** yes

**Current**

The issue reports that the index build over the 106-file `fixture-10k` corpus went from 0.38 s in the 0.29.0 release run to 10.0-10.3 s in the 0.30 dry run, and that a CPU profile put about 60% of the time in `RecordEffectRows` → `CallGraphAnalysis.Build` → `ResolveOperatorCalls`. It asks to cut that cost, to let the index reuse effect results a build already computed, and then to lower the performance ceiling. It is done when the build is within 2x of 0.29 (about 0.8 s on the CI runner) and the ceiling is lowered. The title says "~26x".

**Amendment**

New title: `[0.31] Index build is 8-15x slower than 0.29 (symbol-index binding and the per-module effect pass)`. Labels: add `bug`, `p2` (the issue has none). Keep the opening line, `## Measurement` and `## Where the time goes` as the record of the dry run. Three edits.

1. Insert after `## Where the time goes`:

```markdown
## Status at v0.30 (HEAD bc80daeb)

PR #1740 landed a first fix. Effect summaries now scan a module's declarations instead of building its call graph (`src/Calor.Compiler/Effects/EffectSummaryBuilder.cs:90-94`, `CallGraphAnalysis.ScanDeclarations` at `src/Calor.Compiler/Analysis/CallGraphAnalysis.cs:3555`), and the index build no longer re-binds each module's C# image. Index and CLI output stayed byte-identical. The figures below are from `eng/performance-baselines.json:10`.

| | 0.29.0 | 0.30 as released | After #1740 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `IndexBuildStaysWithinItsBudget` on CI | 0.38 s | 7.68-12.2 s | 3.10-5.56 s |
| Ratio to 0.29.0 | 1x | 20-32x | 8-15x |
| Gate ceiling (`maximumMedian`) | 24.0 s | 48.0 s | 41.0 s |

The "26x" in the original title was the dry run's 10.0 s against 0.38 s. The 41.0 s ceiling is about 12% above the highest of 27 samples from nine `performance.yml` dispatches (medians 22.2 to 35.7 s), and the same record estimates about 5% headroom on the slowest runner class seen.

What remains, in that record's words, "is mostly the symbol index's single Roslyn binding of each module's image": `ProjectSymbolIndex.Build` binds every file (`src/Calor.Compiler/Refactoring/ProjectSymbolIndex.cs:182-201`). `RecordEffectRows` also still runs the effect pass once per module (`src/Calor.Compiler/Indexing/ProjectIndexBuilder.cs:324-350`) instead of reading results a build already has. No profile taken after #1740 is recorded in the repository, so the split between the two is not measured.
```

2. Replace `## Change` with:

```markdown
## Change

This issue stays open across more than one pull request. It closes on the measurement under Done when, not on a merge.

1. Done by #1740: the index build no longer re-binds each module's C# image, and effect summaries are built without a call graph.
2. Profile `ProjectIndexBuilder.Build` again at the current commit and post where the remaining time goes (3.10 to 5.56 s on CI). The original profile was `dotnet-trace` CPU sampling of five warm builds. Profile a temporary copy of the 0.30-syntax fixture, `tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/bench/phase0-agent-native/latency/fixture-10k/src` (106 files), as `IndexPerformanceFixture` does (`tests/Calor.Performance.Tests/IndexPerformanceTests.cs:33-57`). The copy is needed because indexing initializes identities and writes them, which must stay outside the registered fixture (`:44`). Do not use the copy under `bench/`: it is still in the syntax that 0.30 retired.
3. Cut the largest remaining cost. The two candidates are the per-module binding in `ProjectSymbolIndex.Build`, and the per-module effect pass in `RecordEffectRows`, which could reuse effect results a `calor build` already computed (the build-state cache, `src/Calor.Compiler/Incremental/BuildStateCache.cs`) when its inputs match.
4. Lower `maximumMedian` in `eng/performance-baselines.json` toward the 0.29 level by the rule that file records (about 12% above the highest observed CI sample), and append the samples to `noisePolicy`.

Index content must not change. Queries answer from this index, and a wrong row costs more than a slow one.
```

3. Replace `## Done when` and the sentence that follows it with:

```markdown
## Why

A stale index is never answered from. After any edit to a `.calr` file, the next `calor query` or MCP `calor_query` call rebuilds the whole index before it answers (`src/Calor.Compiler/Commands/ProjectIndexQueryReader.cs:87-99`, `src/Calor.Compiler/Indexing/ProjectIndex.cs:341-347`). The build time is therefore part of an agent's edit-to-answer loop, and it is one of the values on the "Fast warm feedback" line (the others are the edit-to-diagnostic figures of #1818, which #1836 may later improve) of the capability scorecard in the plan of record (`docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39.md`, published by #1765). In CI the same build is paid twice per run of the performance suite, once in the fixture and once in the test (`tests/Calor.Performance.Tests/IndexPerformanceTests.cs:57`, `:102`). That is one of the two recorded causes of the gate ceiling rising from 24.0 s; the other is the suite growing from 28 to 40 tests (`eng/performance-baselines.json:10`). The test's own budget is 30 s (`tests/Calor.Performance.Tests/IndexPerformanceTests.cs:79`), so it did not fail at 10 s; only the suite ceiling caught the regression, and the 0.30 release raised that ceiling from 24.0 to 48.0 instead of blocking on this issue. This issue is off the 0.31 exit chain: if the target is unmet when 0.31.0 is released, it moves to 0.32 with its latest readings posted and does not hold the release.

## Done when

- The index build over the 106-file fixture is within 2x of 0.29.0. Dispatch `performance.yml` at least five times on the final commit (`gh workflow run performance.yml --ref <branch>`). Each dispatch runs the suite once as a warm-up and three times measured (`warmupRuns` and `measuredRuns` in `eng/performance-baselines.json`) and uploads the TRX files in the `performance-report` artifact (`artifacts/performance/logs/results-N/results.trx`; `results-1` is the warm-up). Read the `index build: N.NNs` line that `IndexBuildStaysWithinItsBudget` writes to its test output in each measured run. The median of those readings is at most 0.80 s, the bound in the original done-when (twice the 0.38 s of the 0.29.0 release run is 0.76 s). If runner classes make the absolute figure unstable, the alternative is a median at most 2.0 times that of the same test at tag `v0.29.0` dispatched the same day (not verified that the tag's workflow still dispatches). The closing comment quotes every reading and says which form was used.
- `maximumMedian` in `eng/performance-baselines.json` is lower than 41.0, set by the rule in its `noisePolicy`, with the new samples appended there.
- `QueryGoldenTests` and `ProjectIndexTests` pass with no expectation changed (the "Run index/query correctness gate" step, `.github/workflows/test.yml:218-223`). Each pull request under this issue reports a comparison of the `.calor-index.json` that `calor index build <fixture copy> --output <dir>` writes before and after the change: no difference outside the fields that identify the compiler build.
- The closing comment gives the final ratio to 0.29.0, for the scorecard.

## Not in scope

- Warm `calor check` and MCP write latency, and a CI budget for edit-to-diagnostic time: #1818 (0.32).
- The remaining incremental-build reuse tier: #1836 (0.33).
- The wall-clock smoke budgets in the same test project: #1749.

Part of #1594.
```

**Why**

The title still says 26x after a first fix brought the build to 8 to 15 times its 0.29 cost, and the profile and the first Change item describe code that #1740 replaced. The amendment names the two costs that remain and turns "within 2x on the CI runner" into a measurement with a stated method, because nine dispatches of one commit had suite medians from 22.2 to 35.7 s. The issue is off the exit chain: it is written to stay open across several pull requests, and into 0.32 if the target is not met by the release.

### #1749 · [0.31] Release CI flakes: 0.1 ms CFG smoke budget, formatter replay losing files, compiler shard starving the runner

**Action:** amend · **Labels:** ci, tests, p1 · **Blocks:** #1728, #1641 · **Displaces:** n/a (existing issue) · **May slip:** yes

**Current**

The issue lists three CI failures during the 0.30 publish runs, each of which passed on rerun with no code change and cost a rerun of a release job of about 60 minutes: a 0.1 ms wall-clock assertion in `CFGConstruction_SmallFunction_IsTimingSmokeTest` that measured 0.11 ms; the historical formatter replay failing with `DirectoryNotFoundException` when its extracted tree disappeared mid-run; and the hosted runner losing contact while five test hosts ran at once (load 36 on 4 vCPUs). It is done when each has a fix or a recorded decision and the compiler shard and the release-quality job pass three consecutive release dry runs without a rerun. It has no labels and no parent line.

**Amendment**

Title and milestone unchanged. Labels: add `ci`, `tests`, `p1` (the issue has none). Four edits; nothing else in the body changes.

1. Append to item 1, after "or count work instead of time.":

```markdown
The same applies to every wall-clock smoke budget in `tests/Calor.Performance.Tests/VerificationPerformanceTests.cs` that has failed with no code cause. A second one is on record: `DataflowAnalysis_ComplexCFG_IsTimingSmokeTest` (`:380-400`, budget 25 ms) measured 60.9 ms in one `performance.yml` dispatch during the calibration of 2026-11-22, in code the change under test did not touch, and passed on rerun (`eng/performance-baselines.json`, `noisePolicy`). Fix or decide both together.
```

2. Append to item 3, after "or move the replay host to its own job.":

```markdown
The pull request that changes `scripts/run-shard-tests.sh` also rewrites its comment at `:16-18` to agree with `scripts/compiler-shard-filter.sh:8-16` and with the leak record #1590 posts (its record (a) or (b)); if #1590 has posted neither, the comment says the 0.29 sources are repaired and the 0.30 inventory has not been run unsplit. The same pull request re-pins the script in `tests/TestData/DeterminismProtocol/protocol.json`.
```

3. Append to the "Done when" paragraph:

```markdown
The three dry runs are dispatched on `main` commits before the 0.31.0 release candidate is chosen; none is the candidate commit, so that #1728 can time that release from a single dispatch. Only the maintainer dispatches them. If a smoke test is removed, split or added, `expectedTotal` for `tests/Calor.Performance.Tests` in `eng/test-manifest.json` and `expectedTestCount` in `eng/performance-baselines.json` change in the same pull request; `scripts/check_trx.py` enforces the first.
```

4. Add after the "Done when" paragraph:

```markdown
This issue is a release-process prerequisite, not a 0.31 exit gate. If it is still open when 0.31.0 is released, post a comment here that lists every job of that `publish-nuget.yml` run that needed more than one attempt (run id, attempt count, failure); the issue then moves to 0.32 with the `ci` label and does not hold the release.

Part of #1594. Blocks #1641, #1728.
```

If the maintainer closes #1728 as completed (its option (a)), drop `, #1728` from that last line.

**Why**

The issue has no parent and no labels, and its first item names one test although a second wall-clock budget in the same file has failed the same way, so a fix scoped to the one test leaves the other to fail a later release run. The 0.31 release goes through the same jobs, and the .NET 11 CI legs (#1641, 0.34) and the timed release in #1728 wait for this issue. The comment rewrite is added because #1590 leaves the stale "CAPS THE SYMPTOM; it does not fix the leak" text at `scripts/run-shard-tests.sh:16-18` to the next pull request that re-pins the script. The dry-run rule is added because `docs/release-policy.md:13-14` says not to dispatch a dry run and then a real run for one candidate, and #1728 times a release from a single dispatch. The count rule is added because item 1 allows a smoke test to be replaced and both counts are pinned at 40 (`eng/test-manifest.json:74`, `eng/performance-baselines.json:5`). The code behind all three failures is still present at HEAD bc80daeb: the 0.1 ms assertion (`tests/Calor.Performance.Tests/VerificationPerformanceTests.cs:572`), the replay's extraction into a `calor-formatter-history-*` temporary directory (`scripts/registered_ledger_historical_replay.py:344`, started by `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/LosslessFormattingTests.cs:778-788`), and the five hosts (`scripts/run-shard-tests.sh:63-87`). That script's own comments tell whoever takes item 3 the two things the body omits: it already adds an 8 GB swap file (`:47-59`; whether that was in place for the failing run is not verified), and its SHA-256 is pinned, so a change needs a re-pin in `tests/TestData/DeterminismProtocol/protocol.json` in the same pull request (`:5-7`). The issue is a release-process prerequisite and is off the 0.31 exit chain: the exit asks that the flakes are fixed or the reruns of the 0.31 release are recorded, so it may slip to 0.32 without holding the release.

### #1726 · [0.31] Website cold-mobile asset test intermittently times out waiting for networkidle

**Action:** move-in (from none) · **Labels:** tests, ci, p2 · **Displaces:** n/a (existing issue) · **May slip:** yes

**Current**

The issue records one failure of the `browser (/calor)` job in the Website regressions workflow at unchanged website source: the cold-mobile asset-budget case hit its 30-second timeout in `page.goto(..., { waitUntil: 'networkidle' })`, with four local font requests unfinished in the trace, and passed on a rerun of the same commit. It asks for an investigation of the pending font requests and of whether `networkidle` is the right readiness boundary, and forbids raising the timeout, weakening budgets or adding silent retries. It has no milestone, no labels, no parent and no done-when.

**Moved from** no milestone because it is a CI flake with no owner: it is one of eleven open issues that had no milestone when 0.30 shipped, and it belongs with the other CI-health work under #1594.

**Amendment**

Milestone 0.31. Labels: add `tests`, `ci`, `p2`. Title unchanged. Append after `## Scope and follow-up`:

```markdown
## Where to look (HEAD bc80daeb)

- The failing case is `cold mobile brand transfer stays within explicit asset budgets` (`website/tests/assets.spec.ts:8-39`). It aborts every `https://` request (`:10`), disables the cache and throttles the page through the DevTools protocol (`:13-17`: 20 ms latency and `downloadThroughput: 5 * 1024 * 1024`, which that protocol reads as bytes per second), then waits for `networkidle` (`:19`).
- Its assertions read the resource-timing entries whose names match `calor-logo`, `calor-lava`, `og-image` or `favicon` (`:20-22`, `:27-28`), four file sizes on disk (`:29-32`) and the logo images' `naturalWidth` (`:33-36`). None of them reads a font, and the requests that never finished in the trace above are fonts.
- The site is served by `website/tests/serve.mjs`, one Node process that reads each file whole per request (`:12-32`). The fonts are self-hosted `woff2` files (`website/src/app/fonts.ts`).
- Playwright runs one worker with `retries: 0` and keeps a trace on failure (`website/playwright.config.ts:5-10`). The config sets no timeout, so the 30 seconds is Playwright's default.
- The job is `browser` in `.github/workflows/website-tests.yml:30-83`, with one leg per base path (`:36-40`). The workflow runs on pull requests that touch `website/**` or the workflow file (`:4-6`) and has no manual dispatch. Its trace upload sets no retention (`:79-83`), so the preserved trace expires on the repository default (not verified; GitHub's default is 90 days). Download it before working from it.

## Done when

One of these, stated in the closing comment. Both use the same repeat run: in `website/`, after `npm run build`, `npx playwright test tests/assets.spec.ts -g "cold mobile" --repeat-each=100`, once for each base path (`NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_PATH` empty and `/calor`, as the workflow's matrix sets it), on a GitHub-hosted `ubuntu-latest` runner. A draft pull request that temporarily replaces the `npm run test:e2e` step with this command is enough; the failure was on a hosted runner, so a local run does not count.

1. **Cause found and fixed.** The pull request names why the four font requests never finished, or replaces `networkidle` with a readiness condition that names the resources the assertions read and says why that is the right boundary for a cold-transfer measurement. The repeat run passes 100 of 100 for both base paths, and its run id is in the pull request. The cache and throttling setup (`:13-17`), the assertions (`:26-37`) and `retries: 0` are unchanged, and no timeout is added or raised.
2. **Not reproduced.** The repeat run passes 100 of 100 for both base paths with no code change, and no `browser` job has failed in `assets.spec.ts` in the 30 most recent completed runs of the Website regressions workflow (`gh run list --workflow website-tests.yml --limit 30`). Close as not planned with those run ids, and reopen with the new trace if it recurs.

Part of #1594. This issue is off the 0.31 exit chain and may move to the next milestone without holding the release.
```

**Why**

The issue has no milestone, parent or closing condition, and a flake report without one stays open indefinitely. The second branch reuses the rule the 0.29 epic body applied to flake issues: open only if reproduced in the last 30 runs. The issue is off the exit chain and may slip to 0.32 without holding the release.

### #1728 · [0.31] Release: promote a validated immutable bundle instead of repeating full validation

**Action:** amend · **Labels:** ci, documentation, p2 · **Depends on:** #1749 · **Displaces:** n/a (existing issue) · **May slip:** yes

**Current**

The issue says the release process repeats validation in pull-request CI, a dry run and the real publishing run, and proposes validating one protected-main candidate once and then publishing those exact bytes after approval. Its eight acceptance criteria cover the promotion boundary (identity, digest, fail-closed negatives), credential confinement, retries, tests and documentation, and a comparison of elapsed time and runner usage with the previous process. It was filed during the v0.29.0 release as a future improvement.

**Amendment**

What happens to this issue is a maintainer decision that has not been taken. It is recorded in the tracker sign-off (the closing comment of #1759) and in a comment on this issue. The design is implemented and was used to publish 0.30, so the body describes work that is merged. Options:

| Option | What it costs | What is recorded |
|---|---|---|
| (a) Close now as completed | Nothing. `docs/release-policy.md` keeps an estimate where the issue asked for a comparison, and keeps a paragraph saying the path cannot be used yet. | The closing comment below. |
| (b) Narrow to "time one release on the promotion path and correct the policy record" | No engineering. The issue stays open until the first release after #1749 closes (normally just after 0.31.0, or later if #1749 slips), then one documentation pull request. The maintainer supplies one input, the date reviewers were set on `nuget-release`. | The new title and replacement body below; closes with measured rows in `docs/release-policy.md`. |
| (c) Leave as filed | An agent handed the issue would re-implement a merged design. | Nothing. |

The plan recommends (b). The one criterion the repository itself reports as unmet is the timing comparison, and the saving the design exists for was not realised at 0.30, which ran a dry run before the real dispatch and then needed flake reruns (#1749). Option (a) is acceptable if the maintainer does not want an issue held open across a release. No issue then owns the estimate and the stale paragraph; the closing comment names both so that the next change to `docs/release-policy.md` (#1765 and #1783 each add a section to it) can correct them. Filing this plan does not choose.

If (a): post this comment and close with `state_reason: completed`. The dependency on #1749 then lapses; remove `, #1728` from the closing line that the #1749 amendment adds.

```markdown
Closing as completed. The promotion design is implemented and is the only publication path: `publish-nuget.yml` validates once and seals `release-bundle`, and `promotion-check` and `publish` verify it with `scripts/release_promotion.py` before the push. The negative tests are in `scripts/test_release_promotion.py`, and the design, retries and intentional duplicate checks are in `docs/release-policy.md`. The 0.30 packages were pushed through it (run 44947647159). Not done: the elapsed-time comparison in `docs/release-policy.md` is still an estimate, and its "Activation constraints" paragraph still describes the state on 2026-10-30.
```

If (b): new title `[0.31] Release: time one release on the promotion path and correct the policy record`. Milestone 0.31 is unchanged. Labels: add `ci`, `documentation`, `p2` (the issue has none). Replace the whole body with:

```markdown
## Change

The promotion design this issue asked for is implemented and is the only way a package is published. What is left is one measurement and two paragraphs that no longer describe the repository. No workflow or script changes.

1. **Time one release.** The first release published through `publish-nuget.yml` after #1749 closes (expected to be 0.31.0; a 0.30.x point release counts if it comes after #1749 closes) is run as one dispatch: no `dry_run: true` dispatch for the same candidate commit, and a failed job is re-run inside the same run. Afterwards read from the GitHub API, by the method at `docs/release-policy.md:109-112`: elapsed minutes, the sum of job-minutes over non-skipped jobs, the number of run attempts, and the approval wait, taken as the `publish` job's `started_at` minus the `promotion-check` job's `completed_at`.
2. **Record 0.30 as it ran.** From the same API, list every `publish-nuget.yml` dispatch made for 0.30 (`gh run list --workflow publish-nuget.yml --event workflow_dispatch`) with its `dry_run` value, attempts, conclusion, elapsed minutes and job-minutes. Three run ids are known from the repository and from #1749 (Baseline); confirm there are no others.
3. **Update `docs/release-policy.md`.** Under "Timing evidence and estimate", add the rows from steps 1 and 2 beside the v0.29.0 baseline (`:103-107`) and replace the estimate paragraph (`:114-119`) with what was measured. Replace "Activation constraints" (`:121-130`) with the current state: the date required reviewers were set on `nuget-release` (the maintainer supplies it) and the run that first published through `promotion-check`. Identify releases in the new text by run id and date. `calor self-check docs` scans every Markdown file under `docs/` (`src/Calor.Compiler/SelfCheck/DocDriftChecker.cs:268`) and fails on a line that contains the current compiler version (`CheckHardcodedVersion`, `:951-978`); a `<!-- drift:ignore -->` marker exempts only the line after it, as at `docs/release-policy.md:100-101`, and cannot sit between table rows.

Only the maintainer dispatches a release. This issue does not authorise one; it measures one that happens anyway.

## Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- `publish-nuget.yml` validates a candidate once. `package` uploads `release-bundle` (`.github/workflows/publish-nuget.yml:421-560`); `promotion-check` (`:562-588`) and `publish` (`:590-695`) verify it with `scripts/release_promotion.py`. `publish` is the only job in the `nuget-release` environment (`:603`) and holds the only `dotnet nuget push` under `.github/workflows/` (`:679`).
- `scripts/release_promotion.py:35-72` checks the repository, workflow, run, candidate commit, the six gate results, branch protection, required reviewers with self-review prevented, the main-only branch policy, and the artifact's id, digest and expiry. `:75-86` refuses unexpected, duplicate, directory and symlink archive entries.
- `scripts/test_release_promotion.py` holds nine tests of that boundary. `scripts/test_release_checks.py:304` loads them, and that file runs in `.github/workflows/test.yml:106` and `.github/workflows/publish-nuget.yml:283`. `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/Release/ReleaseWorkflowTests.cs` checks the workflow's shape (for example `:127`, `:268-274`). These were read, not run.
- `docs/release-policy.md:9-96` documents the design, the trust boundary, retries and the intentional duplicate checks. `.claude/skills/create-release/SKILL.md:109-175` gives the single-dispatch procedure.
- `docs/release-policy.md:114-119` calls its timing an "Estimate, not a new measured result" and says "No new release was dispatched to time this implementation". `:123-130` says that on 2026-10-30 `nuget-release` had no required reviewers, that promotion refuses that configuration, and that a hosted run was still required.
- 0.30 was published on 2026-11-21 (`CHANGELOG.md:7`). `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/Release/ReleaseWorkflowTests.cs:588-593` records: "The packages were pushed, then the install check failed on every platform and blocked the GitHub release (run 44947647159)". The push runs only after `promotion-check` and the environment approval, so the path has been used once. The run's attempts and timings are not verified from the repository.
- 0.30 was not a single dispatch. `eng/performance-baselines.json:10` cites the 0.30 release dry run (run 44917750117), and `docs/release-policy.md:13-14` says not to dispatch a dry run and then a real run. #1749 cites first-attempt failures in runs 44935998950 and 44947647159; the first of those run ids appears only in that issue and is not verified.
- The v0.29.0 baseline is 86.35 elapsed minutes and 256.37 job-minutes over a dry run and a real run (`docs/release-policy.md:103-107`).

## Why

As first filed, this issue asked for the promotion design itself, with eight acceptance criteria. Read against HEAD, each has an implementation with a test or a documented section (Baseline), except the last: "compare elapsed time and runner usage with the current process" is met only by an estimate, in a document that still says the path cannot be used. The saving the design exists for is the dry validation that cost 36.62 elapsed minutes and 122.22 job-minutes at v0.29.0. It was not realised at 0.30, which ran a dry run first and then needed reruns for flakes (#1749). One timed release from a single dispatch is the evidence that closes the issue. It is off the 0.31 exit chain and may slip to the next milestone without holding the release; if 0.31.0 is the release that gets timed, it closes just after that release.

## Done when

- `docs/release-policy.md` has a row for one release published from a single dispatch, with the run id, elapsed minutes, job-minutes, attempts and approval wait, computed by the file's own method, and a row for each 0.30 dispatch.
- `grep -n "Estimate, not a new measured result\|but no required reviewers" docs/release-policy.md` prints nothing (exit code 1), and the file states the measured difference from the v0.29.0 baseline in elapsed minutes and in job-minutes.
- If the timed release needed a dry run, a second dispatch or a rerun, its row says so and why. The issue still closes: it reports the cost and does not gate on it.
- `calor self-check docs` exits 0 (the spec-drift step, `.github/workflows/test.yml:266-267`).

## Not in scope

- Any change to `publish-nuget.yml`, `scripts/release_promotion.py` or the `nuget-release` environment settings.
- Reusing pull-request results as release evidence, and benchmark publication (both excluded by the original issue).
- The release flakes themselves: #1749.
- The other additions to `docs/release-policy.md` in this milestone, which are separate sections: the release close-out checklist (#1765) and the green-sweep rule (#1783).

Part of #1594. Depends on #1749.
```

**Why**

The issue asks for a promotion path that `docs/release-policy.md:9-96` documents as implemented and that `publish-nuget.yml` already enforces, so as filed it describes merged work. The path was also already used for 0.30, so what is left is the timing comparison and two stale paragraphs. Closing or narrowing is the maintainer's choice, and the plan files maintainer decisions with their options written out instead of assuming the answer. Under (b) the issue depends on #1749, because the release it times is the first one after the flakes are fixed; it is off the exit chain and may slip to 0.32 without holding the release.

## Epic #1776 · [0.31] Epic: adoption decisions that gate 0.36 and later

New epic. Labels: epic.

#### Goal

Take in 0.31 the maintainer decisions that milestones 0.36 to 0.39 wait on, and open the paperwork with the longest lead time:

- whether anyone may be asked to become the external maintainer of a pilot component, or the independent methods reviewer of the 0.37 comparison;
- what happens to the paused PP-W-rows research;
- a first draft of the 0.37 protocol, and a first sizing of what that comparison would cost.

Every deliverable is a document under `docs/plans/`. No issue here changes the compiler, uses CI time, spends money or has an agent contact anyone.

#### Why

- 0.36 needs "a named external maintainer" and treats a missing one as "a blocker, not replaced by creator self-evaluation" (#1701). 0.37 takes its final tasks from that pilot (#1708) and cannot start collection without an independent fairness review (#1709, #1712). 0.38 and 0.39 consume the 0.37 verdict.
- Outreach has never been authorised. The last gate record lists no permitted contacts, no outreach window and "Contacts made" at 0 (`docs/plans/safe-delegation-m0/v0.23/r2a-adopter-unavailable.md:25-30`, 2026-10-01). The methods reviewer seat is recorded "Vacant; no consenting person evidenced" (`docs/plans/safe-delegation-m0/authorization.md:68`, 2026-09-09).
- Naming an external adopter is a decision the maintainer reserved (`docs/plans/call-s-adjudication.md:187-190`), so no agent can advance it. Left where they are, both absences are discovered at the last step: #1701 sits in 0.36 and the reviews in 0.37.
- #1648 was filed in 0.32, although three things wait on it earlier or name it as their predecessor: the #1708 protocol draft that starts in 0.31 (#1791) needs the admission rule its closing record carries forward (the test a task must pass before it counts as evidence about effect rows or contracts), #1711 depends on the same rule, and the 0.31 legacy-dialect allowlist (#1763) must list the 52 retired-syntax `.calr` files whose class it decides.

These items need the maintainer, not an agent and not CI, and they can be taken in one sitting: #1777, #1648 and, if its draft is ready, the request in #1786.

#### Work

| Issue | Item |
|---|---|
| #1777 | Decision: authorise or decline outreach for an external adopter and for an independent methods reviewer; a contact log on the authorise branch; a default so the release is not held |
| #1648 | Decide the paused PP-W-rows research (resume with a plan or close), with a closing record that carries the admission rule forward and classifies the retired-syntax fixtures |
| #1791 | Open the #1708 protocol draft and, only if outreach is authorised, the #1701 recruitment record |
| #1786 | One-page sizing and budget request for #1708 and #1712, drafted from the archived E1a run-to-run spread |

#### Sequence

1. #1765 (epic #1758) merges first. The decline branch of #1777 applies the adopter fallback that document holds.
2. #1777 and #1648 are decided in the same session. #1648 waits on nothing and may be decided earlier; #1763 (epic #1761) reads its result.
3. #1791 after both: the recruitment record exists only on the authorise branch of #1777, and the task-selection sections of the protocol draft are written from the closing record of #1648. Its two files may merge separately; the recruitment record waits only on #1777.
4. #1786 at any point. If it is ready, it is put in front of the maintainer in the same session; nothing waits for it in 0.31.

#### Done when

- `docs/plans/0.31-outreach-decision.md` is merged with at least one dated entry for each of the two items: authorise, decline, or that item's default line, which contains the words "not decided, treated as decline until changed". This satisfies the 0.31 exit criterion on the outreach decision on every branch of the #1605 criterion, "no paid run authorised" included.
- #1648 is closed with `docs/plans/0.31-ppw-rows-disposition.md` merged, and #1254, #1262, #1267 and #1436 are either closed as not planned or carry a dated plan.
- `docs/plans/0.37-comparison-protocol.md` exists as a draft; on the authorise branch `docs/plans/0.36-pilot-recruitment.md` exists too. Or #1791 has moved to 0.32 with the note #1833 must carry.
- `docs/plans/0.37-sizing-and-budget-request.md` exists as a draft with its refresh log, or #1786 has moved to 0.32.

#### Not in this epic

- Writing the adopter fallback table (#1765, epic #1758) and applying it at the checkpoint (#1833). This epic cites the table and, on a decline, records the date from which it applies.
- Recording an adopter's consent or naming the adopter: #1833 and #1701.
- The two methods reviews (#1857), the task supply inventory (#1865) and the collection budget decision (#1867).
- Freezing and registering the #1708 protocol in Annex A of `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md`: #1708.
- The registration and spend decision for the 0.31 evaluation: #1770.
- Putting the `W-00x` files on the legacy-dialect allowlist: #1763 applies the class #1648 records.
- Any paid run, any contact made by an agent, any compiler or harness change.
- Three follow-ups that no issue owns. Each is filed as a new issue only if the #1648 closing record calls for it, and none is 0.31 work:
  - Under option (b) of #1648, changing the published status from "paused by the user" to "closed without a verdict" on three website pages, together with the assertions in `website/tests/public-claims.spec.ts` that pin the wording.
  - If the maintainer wants the six `W-00x` pair directories removed: removing them together with the blob-SHA pins, the tests and the tooling that hold them byte-identical.
  - Enforcing a class for the 220 `.calr.inc` fragments of the redesigned experiment. The #1763 check reads `.calr` files only, so the class #1648 records for them is a statement that no check enforces.

### #1777 · [0.31] Decision: authorise and log outreach — an external adopter and an independent methods reviewer

**Action:** new · **Labels:** dev-experience · **Depends on:** #1765 · **Blocks:** #1791, #1833, #1857 · **Displaces:** decision-only

#### Change

Take and record one maintainer decision with two items. The agent assigned prepares the record with the options below; the maintainer decides; no agent contacts anyone.

1. **Adopter outreach.** May candidates be asked to become the *external adopter*: a maintainer outside the project who would own one Calor component inside an existing application (#1701)?
2. **Methods reviewer outreach.** May candidates be asked to become the *independent methods reviewer*: a person who checks, before any comparative run, that the 0.37 design can support the conclusions it is registered to draw? #1712 does not start without the two reviews #1857 holds.

This record does not take the decision the maintainer reserved. That decision is the naming of an adopter ("the Call 3 crossing (a named adopter)", `docs/plans/call-w-adjudication.md:75-77`); consent is recorded later, at #1833, and nothing in this record names an adopter. Whether asking candidates falls under the reservation is the maintainer's reading to make: if it does, item 1 is a decline.

**Options, per item.**

| Option | Meaning | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Authorise | A named candidate list, a window, and a contact log. | The maintainer's hours. Candidates hear about a pre-1.0 language that has one breaking window left (0.32). |
| Decline, adopter | Record "demand unproven; outreach not authorised". The adopter fallback in `docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39.md` applies from that date. | None now. From that date the rows that table holds are held (as proposed: 0.38, 0.39 and the pilot-dependent issues of 0.36), 0.34 conversion coverage is narrowed, and the scope of 0.37 is chosen at #1833. |
| Decline, reviewer | Record the seat as vacant. | None now. #1857 records the vacancy and #1712 does not start. |

The *adopter fallback* is the table that #1765 writes in advance in the plan of record: what is held, re-scoped or closed in 0.34 to 0.39 while no external maintainer has consented.

**Recommendation.** Authorise both. The adopter window ends no later than the planned close of 0.33, where #1833 reads it; the reviewer window may run longer, and ends before #1708 is frozen in 0.37. Asking is the "evidence-led demand-building" the standing policy allows, the decline consequences are already written, and without it the vacancy is found in 0.36 and 0.37. The reviewer is needed on every path where #1712 collects, fallback included. Decline is the better choice if the maintainer will not have the hours to support an external maintainer through 0.36.

**An authorise entry records** (modelled on the table in `docs/plans/safe-delegation-m0/v0.23/r2a-adopter-unavailable.md:23-31`):

- Candidates permitted to be contacted. For the adopter, in the strategy's order of evidential value: a willing open-source project, then an internal Microsoft team; the maintainer's own projects do not count. A project or organisation is named. A person is named only if their role is already public or they agreed; otherwise the row carries an opaque reference and the maintainer keeps the key outside the repository.
- Who may make contact. Recommended: the maintainer only. Agents may draft text and never send it.
- The window: a start (a date, or a named event such as "#1605 stage 1 published") and an end as a calendar date.
- What a contact is told, at least: Calor is pre-1.0 with one maintainer and a tested path back to C#; no compatibility promise exists until the edition is declared (#1805); the project has not established an economic or safety advantage over C#; nothing is promised about native compilation or whole-application migration.
- What may be offered: hours of maintainer support, and payment (an amount, or none).
- Reviewer only: the independence conditions (not the maintainer; has not contributed to Calor; authors none of the #1708 tasks or acceptance checks or the #1711 controls; no stake in the result), the scope (the #1708 protocol and the #1709 fairness check), and an allowance ceiling in hours and fee.
- A contact log, empty at first: date, candidate reference, channel category, who made contact, outcome (no reply, declined, interested, agreed in writing), next step. No message text.

**Default.** If an item has no entry when the 0.31 exit criterion on #1605 is met in any of its three forms (stage 1 published, the reduced design of #1770 published, or "no paid run authorised" recorded under #1770), the agent preparing the 0.31 release adds a dated line so the release is not held: `Adopter outreach: not decided, treated as decline until changed.` or `Methods reviewer outreach: not decided, treated as decline until changed; seat vacant.` The default has the consequences of a decline with one difference: it is not the maintainer's finding, so "demand unproven" is not written and no issue is closed or re-scoped on the strength of it. Issues the fallback holds stay held; closing or re-scoping waits for an explicit decline here or for #1833.

**Changing it.** Entries are never edited. A later dated entry in the same file supersedes an earlier one. The latest entry for an item governs. An authorise entry may follow a decline or the default at any time. For the adopter item: before #1833 the window is then shorter and #1833 says so; after #1833 has recorded outcome F (no consent; the fallback applies) it reopens outreach, and any consent is recorded as a later dated entry in the #1833 record, which is the only thing that lifts the hold. For the reviewer item: a later entry naming a consenting reviewer lifts the vacancy #1857 records. When a reviewer agrees, a dated entry names them (with their consent) and gives their affiliation, the independence statement against the four conditions above, conflicts disclosed, scope, allowance, and a deadline for each of the two reviews; #1857 reads that entry.

**Where.** `docs/plans/0.31-outreach-decision.md`. It takes effect when the maintainer merges it.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- Outreach has never been authorised. The gate record of 2026-10-01 lists permitted organisations "None", permitted human contacts "None", no outreach window, and "Contacts made" at 0 (`docs/plans/safe-delegation-m0/v0.23/r2a-adopter-unavailable.md:25-30`). Its authority says "Permitted contacts: none" (`docs/plans/safe-delegation-m0/v0.23/r0-authorization.md:50-52`).
- The repository holds no dated recruitment log after 2026-08-04, and the earlier record says unknown activity "is not a failed search" (`docs/plans/safe-delegation-m0/supply-status.md:17-27`). No file named `docs/plans/0.31-outreach-decision.md` exists.
- Call W (2026-08-04) recorded "DEMAND UNPROVEN" and the policy "do not fund new adoption depth ahead of demand" (`docs/plans/call-w-adjudication.md:28-43`). The same record allows "evidence-led demand-building" and reserves "the Call 3 crossing (a named adopter)" to the maintainer (`:73-77`; restated in `docs/plans/call-s-adjudication.md:187-190`).
- An adopter requires at least one non-maintainer reviewer; candidates by evidential value are a willing OSS project, an internal Microsoft team, and, as pilot substrate only, the maintainer's own projects (`docs/plans/agent-native-strategy.md:73-74`).
- The methods reviewer seat is "Vacant; no consenting person evidenced" and the review allowance "Not authorized" (`docs/plans/safe-delegation-m0/authorization.md:68-69`, 2026-09-09). On 2026-10-01 the maintainer substituted cross-family AI review, which that record says "is not independent human review" (`docs/plans/safe-delegation-m0/v0.23/r1-methods-governance.md:19-31`). The close-out of that inquiry lists an adopter organisation and a human methods reviewer among the things that would restart it (`docs/plans/safe-delegation-m0/v0.23/r5-classification-and-action.md:190-198`).
- The earlier record asks that names and messages are not published without consent (`docs/plans/safe-delegation-m0/supply-status.md:83`).
- The adoption guide states "no broader compatibility promise yet", one maintainer, and that the project "has not established a qualifying adopter, independent adopter handoff, economic advantage, or safety advantage over protected C#" (`website/content/guides/adoption-playbook.mdx:12-13`, `:19-22`).
- #1701 says recruitment "can start immediately" and that inability to recruit "is reported as a blocker, not replaced by creator self-evaluation" (tracker snapshot of 2026-11-23).
- `docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39.md` does not exist yet; #1765 creates it with the adopter fallback.

#### Why

The plan's long pole is not engineering. 0.36 to 0.39 depend on an external maintainer, and the record shows the adopter missing on three occasions (2026-08-04, 2026-09-09 and 2026-10-01, in the baseline above) with outreach attempted on none, because it was never authorised. The same is true of the methods reviewer, whose absence was one of the inputs the study stop of 2026-09-09 listed as missing (`docs/plans/safe-delegation-m0/decision.md:52`). Both searches take calendar time that no agent can compress, and both are decisions only the maintainer can take. This is the only 0.31 exit criterion no agent can advance, so it is filed as one decision with both branches written and a default that keeps it from holding the release. Deciding now also makes the decline branch useful: the fallback is applied early and the later milestones are planned on it, instead of being discovered blocked.

#### Done when

- `docs/plans/0.31-outreach-decision.md` is merged by the maintainer and holds, for each of the two items, at least one dated entry, the latest of which governs: authorise, decline, or the default line.
- An authorise entry has every field listed under Change: at least one candidate, a window whose end is a calendar date, the statement of what a contact is told, and the contact log table.
- An adopter decline contains the words "demand unproven" and links the adopter fallback section of `docs/plans/roadmap-0.31-0.39.md` with the date from which it applies. A reviewer decline says the seat is vacant. A default entry is the line given under Default, word for word, with its date.
- The record states that it names no adopter, records no adopter's consent and authorises no paid run.
- #1791 and #1833 each have a comment quoting the adopter entry; #1857 has a comment quoting the reviewer entry.
- On an adopter decline: every issue the merged adopter fallback table marks held (as proposed: #1697, #1701, #1705, #1706, #1707, the pilot-specific parts of #1702 to #1704, and #1699, #1700 and their children) carries a comment that links this record and the table and says it is held. Nothing is closed, and #1659 is not commented: #1833 records the 0.34 scope and the 0.37 option. On the default line the same comments are posted and say "held by default, not a maintainer finding".
- The pull request adds that one file. Nothing changes under `docs/plans/safe-delegation-m0/v0.23/`, the closed 0.23 record whose Markdown files `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/Plans/SafeDelegationV023BoundaryTests.cs` checks, or under `docs/plans/evidence/`, where `tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/Plans/EvidenceTreeGuardTests.cs` fails on any new file. No test is added, so `eng/test-manifest.json` does not change.

#### Not in scope

- Recording consent or naming the adopter (#1833, #1701), and writing the fallback (#1765).
- The two reviews (#1857) and any collection budget (#1867).
- Contact by an agent, under any option.
- Maintaining the contact log after the merge: each update is a small pull request by the maintainer, and #1833 reads the log.

Part of epic #1776. Depends on #1765. Blocks #1791, #1833, #1857.

As registered it runs only on its pinned compiler, and a result would describe 0.18 enforcement.
```markdown
- Every harness binding registered for it pins `run-pair.sh` and `harness-capture.py` by SHA-256, and neither file matches at HEAD. The latest binding by issue number, `bench/phase0-agent-native/registrations/ppw-rows-stage1/gateway-instrument-amendment-1434.json`, differs from HEAD in 4 of 30 files (`harness-capture.py`, `ppw-budget-gateway.py`, `ppw-gateway-budget.py`, `run-pair.sh`; compared with `sha256sum`). The 0.31 harness issues (#1766 to #1767) are planned to edit `run-pair.sh` and `harness-capture.py` again.
- No pilot trial has completed. Two invalid or censored attempts are preserved (`CHANGELOG.md:1982-1983`), and a $51.04 request reservation is recorded as unresolved exposure, not verified spending (`bench/phase0-agent-native/README-ppw-gateway.md:8-11`).
- The first fixture set, the six `bench/phase0-agent-native/pairs/W-00*` directories (77 tracked files; 52 are `.calr`, all in the `§` syntax, none with a copy under `tests/TestData/ActiveSyntax/`), cannot be translated in place. Its twelve starters are frozen by git blob SHA in the registered PP-W6 row, and `PpWRowsRegistrationTests.PairStarterCopiesMatchTheFrozenBlobShas` (`tests/Calor.Compiler.Tests/Effects/PpWRowsRegistrationTests.cs:781-804`) and `test_starter_blobs_match_pair_json_and_the_freeze` (`bench/phase0-agent-native/tests/test_ppw_pairs.py:179-190`) fail if a byte changes.

Independent of 0.30, the status is published: the website describes the study as "paused by the user" (`website/content/benchmarking/results.mdx:64-66`, `website/content/benchmarking/effect-rows-study.mdx:12`, `website/content/benchmarking/methodology.mdx:54`), and `website/tests/public-claims.spec.ts:214-291` and `:751-755` assert that wording.

| Option | Cost |
|---|---|

A design in 0.30 syntax would be a new registration, not a resumption. |
| (b) Close | The registered design aimed at the agent-level benefit of effect rows ends without a verdict. The question passes to the blinded boundary-defect controls of #1711 (0.37). The instrument code and its tests stay in the tree unless a follow-up removes them, and the published status needs a follow-up edit. |

The plan recommends (b): the registered design is tied to a compiler and a syntax that 0.30 retired, no trial has completed, and the design's most useful product, the admission rule, is carried into #1708. The maintainer decides. Choosing (a) does not itself reactivate the research; it schedules the reactivation steps #1254 names.
```

```markdown
Whichever option is chosen, it is recorded in `docs/plans/0.31-ppw-rows-disposition.md`. The agent assigned prepares the file; the maintainer's merge is the decision. The file contains:
```

### #1791 · [0.31] Open the long-lead drafts: the #1708 protocol and, if outreach is authorised, the #1701 recruitment record

**Action:** new · **Labels:** dev-experience · **Depends on:** #1648, #1777 · **Blocks:** #1701, #1708 · **Displaces:** text-only · **May slip:** yes

#### Change

Open two working documents under `docs/plans/` so that the long-lead work of 0.36 and 0.37 starts in 0.31. Text only: no code, no test, no run, no contact with anyone. The two files may be delivered as separate pull requests: the first waits on #1648, the second only on #1777.

**1. `docs/plans/0.37-comparison-protocol.md`, always.** The draft of the protocol that #1708 finishes and freezes for the 0.37 comparison (conventional C#, C# equipped with executable specifications, and Calor on the same tasks). Its first line reads: "Draft. Not registered, not frozen; authorises nothing. #1708 finishes and registers it." Sections:

- **Outcome measures.** For each measure: definition, unit, direction, how it is observed, and what is recorded when it cannot be observed (unknown, never zero). Cover accepted-task outcome against independent acceptance checks; attempts to acceptance; total engineering cost with the components #1698 lists (specification authoring, integration, agent and validation execution, review, rework, maintenance); held-out performance where a task has a performance target; detection of seeded defects (defects injected on purpose), kept apart from natural failure rates. Start from the fixture-scale definitions in `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md:35-41` and say what changes at component scale.
- **Cost accounting.** Agent spend is read from the agent's own `total_cost_usd`, never priced by hand from token counts (the rule in `docs/plans/pp-w5-spend-authorisation.md:18-21`). Human time is logged by category, and an unknown duration is a labelled estimate, never zero. Setup is charged per condition, reusable tooling once, and Calor's learning material (primer, exemplar) to the Calor condition.
- **Independence.** Who may author requirements, acceptance checks, held-out inputs and defect controls; that independence means separate ownership and withheld outcomes, not a different agent session; where the methods reviewer enters (#1777, #1857); and the label every output carries if only the maintainer and AI agents take part ("non-independent"; AI review is a quality control, not independent review, `docs/plans/safe-delegation-m0/v0.23/review-protocol.md:7-9`).
- **Invalid-run rules.** What makes a run invalid, the retry cap (today: two re-attempts per slot, then the slot counts as a task failure, `docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md:13`), that no run is silently replaced, and what a leak of held-out data does to a run.
- **Task selection**, **Stratification**, **Admission rule.** Written from the closing record of #1648 only (`docs/plans/0.31-ppw-rows-disposition.md`, item 5). Quote its admission rule verbatim with a link, name the three strata #1708 will use (effect-and-contract, neutral, C#-favoured), and write "fixed by #1708 before screening" wherever a number belongs (shares, minimums, the exclusion threshold). No candidate task is listed or screened here.
- **Open questions** for #1708.

**2. `docs/plans/0.36-pilot-recruitment.md`, only if the adopter item of #1777 is an authorise entry.** The working record for #1701. Its first line reads: "Working record for #1701. Records no consent; consent is recorded at #1833." Headings, each holding "not yet recorded" until a candidate exists: Candidate (the reference from the contact log in `docs/plans/0.31-outreach-decision.md`; no name that file does not already carry); Application context; Component and its API; Current build and deployment; Representative workload; Desired improvement; Maintenance expectations; Current costs; Baseline (clean build, tests, source and toolchain identities); Permitted assistance and intervention log; Gaps (language, BCL or verifier forms the component needs and Calor does not support).

The gate is read from the adopter item of `docs/plans/0.31-outreach-decision.md` as recorded. While that item has no entry, the second file waits. On a decline or the default entry, it is not created and the issue closes on the first file alone; if a later authorise entry supersedes that, the recruitment record is opened under this issue if it is still open, otherwise under #1701.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- Neither file exists, and `docs/plans/` holds no protocol for the 0.37 comparison.
- #1708 says "protocol drafting can start earlier"; #1701 says "recruitment and baseline work can start immediately"; #1697 says "Start #1701 recruitment/scope immediately" (tracker snapshot of 2026-11-23). No outreach is authorised: `docs/plans/safe-delegation-m0/v0.23/r2a-adopter-unavailable.md:25-30`.
- The admission rule (the test a task must pass before it counts as evidence about effect rows or contracts) has no home outside the paused PP-W-rows study: its source is rules R1 to R8 of that study's redesign (`docs/plans/2026-09-05-ppw-rows-fixture-redesign.md:105-137`), and #1648, which carries it forward into `docs/plans/0.31-ppw-rows-disposition.md`, is open.

#### Why

The tracker already says both pieces of work can start now (#1708, #1701, #1697), and protocol design and recruitment take calendar time that a later milestone cannot recover. With that exception, this issue may slip to 0.32 without holding the 0.31 release.

### #1786 · [0.31] One-page sizing and budget request for #1708 and #1712

**Action:** new · **Labels:** documentation, tests · **Blocks:** #1865, #1867 · **Displaces:** text-only · **May slip:** yes

#### Change

Write `docs/plans/0.37-sizing-and-budget-request.md`: one page, addressed to the maintainer, that says what the 0.37 comparison (#1708 defines it, #1712 runs it) has to measure, how many repetitions the known run-to-run variability supports, what that would cost, and what the design could not detect. It is a draft from archived data. It authorises nothing: the spend decision is #1867, and filing this issue does not authorise spend. No run is made, paid or unpaid.

Terms. A *pair* is one benchmark task with a fixture for each language and one shared set of held-out tests. An *arm* or *condition* is one setup a run is made under. *Equipped C#* is the 0.37 condition that gives C# executable specifications and analyzers (#1709). A *stratum* is one of the three task groups #1708 fixes in advance: effect-and-contract, neutral, C#-favoured. *CV* is the coefficient of variation, the standard deviation divided by the mean.

Sections, in this order. Sections 1 to 6 together are at most 800 words.

1. **Status.** "Draft of <date> from the E1a archive: pre-0.30 syntax, model not pinned, two fixture-scale pairs, no equipped-C# condition. Authorises nothing."
2. **Measures.** The measures being sized, from #1698 and #1708: accepted-task rate against independent acceptance checks; cost per accepted task (agent spend sized; human time listed, not sized); iterations to acceptance. One line each: the unit, and which comparison uses it. Calor against equipped C# is the gating one.
3. **Variability.** Within-condition and between-task spread for each measure. Each number is followed by the command that reproduces it from the archive. For this draft, the table in the baseline below.
4. **Repetitions.** For the ratio of mean per-run cost between two conditions on one task, the standard error of the log ratio is about sqrt((c1² + c2²)/n), with n runs per condition and CVs c1 and c2. Give the smallest ratio distinguishable from 1 (one-sided 5%, 80% power: exp(2.49 × the standard error)) at n = 5, 10, 20 and 30, and recommend n. Then the task count: the unit of analysis is the task (`docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md:83-88`), so say what can be claimed per task, and what cannot be claimed about a task class from the small set #1708 asks for. State the minimum number of admitted tasks per stratum that the request assumes, labelled as an assumption: the E1a archive (two pairs, no spread in C# iterations, no equipped-C# condition, model not pinned) sizes cost ratios only and cannot yield a task count. The first refresh replaces the assumption. #1865 compares supply with that number.
5. **Request.** Projected runs (tasks × conditions × n, plus control runs), cost per run per condition with its source, the projected spend, and a requested ceiling in dollars. The ceiling covers the projection at 4x, the largest overrun on record, or the page says why not. Compare it with the standing $1,500 per-epoch ceiling (`docs/plans/agent-native-gates.md:81`). A reviewer allowance set under #1777 is a separate line.
6. **Limited power.** In plain words: the smallest difference the proposed design can detect on each measure; that a rare-defect rate cannot be bounded tightly (zero events in n runs leave a one-sided 97.5% upper bound of 1 − 0.025^(1/n): 8.81% at n = 40, 4.51% at n = 80, `docs/plans/roadmap-v0.20.md:59`); and which of the inputs the September stop listed as absent (`docs/plans/safe-delegation-m0/decision.md:43-52`) are still absent.
7. **Refresh log.** A table: date, milestone close, data source (epoch directory and commit), what changed. Row 1 is this draft. Row 2 is pre-filled: "first milestone close-out after #1605 stage 1 has published: due". Refreshing is a step of the milestone close-out checklist that #1765 adds to `docs/release-policy.md`, not part of this issue.

#### Baseline (HEAD bc80daeb)

- No sizing exists for the 0.37 comparison. The stop of 2026-09-09 recorded that no scenario could be sized because inputs were absent, among them task supply, "event rates, discordance, cost variation", executable estimators and a methods reviewer (`docs/plans/safe-delegation-m0/decision.md:43-52`).
- The archive this draft uses is E1a, the attribution experiment of July 2026: 2 pairs × 3 arms (C#, Calor, and Calor with a syntax sheet, the exemplar, in the prompt) × 30 runs, pre-0.30 syntax, `"modelPin": "default"` (`bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/e1a-attribution/pins.json:6`). Per-run cost, measured here by reading `total_cost_usd` from each `<pair>/<arm>/run-*/agent.json` in that directory (180 files, total $370.52):

| Pair | Arm | Mean $ | SD $ | CV |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| N1-003-csv-row | csharp | 0.598 | 0.086 | 0.14 |
| N1-003-csv-row | calor | 3.647 | 1.094 | 0.30 |
| N1-003-csv-row | calor+exemplar | 2.232 | 0.226 | 0.10 |
| W3-003-kv-journal | csharp | 0.503 | 0.067 | 0.13 |
| W3-003-kv-journal | calor | 4.205 | 1.347 | 0.32 |
| W3-003-kv-journal | calor+exemplar | 1.166 | 0.162 | 0.14 |

Reproduce from the repository root (prints pair, arm, runs, mean, SD, CV, then the total):

```sh
python3 - <<'EOF'
import json, glob, statistics as st, collections
c = collections.defaultdict(list)
for f in glob.glob('bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/e1a-attribution/*/*/run-*/agent.json'):
    c[tuple(f.split('/')[-4:-2])].append(json.load(open(f))['total_cost_usd'])
for k, v in sorted(c.items()):
    print(*k, len(v), round(st.mean(v), 3), round(st.stdev(v), 3), round(st.stdev(v) / st.mean(v), 2))
print(round(sum(map(sum, c.values())), 2))
EOF
```

- The N1-003 calor cell includes one invalid slot (run 1, three API errors, $0.0009; `bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/e1a-attribution/N1-003-csv-row/calor/run-1/invalid.txt`). Without it the cell is 29 runs, mean $3.773, CV 0.23.
- Iterations to green: C# was 1 in all 60 runs, so it has no spread. Calor means were 2.50 and 2.27, and with the exemplar 2.23 and 1.00 (`bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/e1a-attribution/VERDICT.md:13-17`). The exemplar removed 0.18 of the excess on one pair and 1.00 on the other (`:15-16`): two tasks already disagree by that much.
- Worked example of section 4, computed here from the table: at CVs of 0.14 and 0.32 the standard error is 0.110 at n = 10 and 0.064 at n = 30, so the smallest distinguishable ratio is about 1.32 and 1.17.
- Cost per run at real scale, three calibration runs of 2026-08-03 on pre-0.30 syntax with model `sonnet`: $0.45 for C#, $3.47 for Calor (`docs/plans/w4-dryrun-spend-authorisation.md:46-57`). Realised spend has exceeded projection by 2.8x ($92.83 against about $33, `docs/plans/pp-w5-spend-authorisation.md:18`, `:86`) and by about 4x ($79.39 against under $20, `bench/phase0-agent-native/epochs/guarantees-probe-001/VERDICT.md:20-24`), in both cases on pre-0.30 syntax.
- #1708 says "Select repeated trials and ordering from expected variability, pilot calibration, and budget. Disclose limited power instead of adopting unsupported universal percentage targets."; #1712 requires explicit authorisation before paid collection (tracker snapshot of 2026-11-23).

#### Why

The study stop of 2026-09-09 was recorded partly because no defensible design could be sized: cost variation was unmeasured, no cap was authorised, and the published arithmetic was illustration, not a power analysis (`docs/plans/safe-delegation-m0/decision.md:18-21`, `:30-52`). Leaving that arithmetic to 0.37 repeats the failure at the last step, after a pilot has been run. A one-page draft now gives the maintainer an order of magnitude when deciding outreach, gives #1865 the minimum task count to compare supply with, and gives #1867 a document to refresh instead of one to invent. The E1a spread is the wrong syntax and has no equipped-C# condition, which is why the page is a draft with a refresh log, and why the first refresh is at the first close-out after #1605 stage 1 has published, the first measurement on the 0.30 language. This issue may slip to 0.32 without holding the 0.31 release.

#### Done when

- `docs/plans/0.37-sizing-and-budget-request.md` is merged with the seven sections in order, and sections 1 to 6 total at most 800 words (`wc -w` on that part).
- Every number in sections 3 to 6 is followed by a `path:line` or by the command that reproduces it, and every number measured on the pre-0.30 syntax says so.
- Section 4 gives the smallest distinguishable ratio at the four values of n, recommends one, and states a minimum task count per stratum, labelled as an assumption until the first refresh.
- Section 5 names a ceiling in dollars and the multiple of the projection it represents.
- Section 6 names at least one claim the proposed design cannot support.
- The refresh log has its two rows.
- The page says it authorises nothing and names #1867 as the decision. #1865 and #1867 each have a comment linking it.
- The pull request adds that one file. No test is added, so `eng/test-manifest.json` does not change.

#### Not in scope

- Authorising any spend, and the paid-run conditions (abort triggers, zero-cost `--null-agent` pass, one pinned model, a reading written in advance for each outcome): #1867.
- The refreshes at later milestone close-outs (the close-out checklist from #1765).
- The protocol (#1791, #1708) and the count of available tasks (#1865).
- New runs of any kind.

Part of epic #1776. Blocks #1865, #1867.
